# Changelog

This file records user-visible and operational changes that reach `main`. Pull requests add concise entries under **Unreleased**; the integration agent moves shipped entries into dated sections when cutting a release. Pull requests and Git history remain the detailed implementation record.

## Unreleased

- Keep earlier steps' text on screen while a reply in an older agent-linked chat streams its next step. The live-stream writer accepted those chats but the step-checkpoint writer skipped them, so each step's text disappeared as soon as the next model call started streaming. Admission, the live-stream writer and the checkpoint writer now share one rule for which conversations use unified streaming: agent-linked chats get step checkpoints like other chats, while Slack-mirrored, surface-bound and unbound automation chats stay on the legacy path instead of streaming text that would never be checkpointed. Convex tests now cover every conversation kind on both writers, plus a multi-step agent-linked reply.
- Remove "New workspace" from the mobile app. Mobile is now a companion for workspaces that already exist: people create workspaces, run setup and choose a plan on web or desktop, and the phone never offers paid features. The surfaces guide records the rule in its parity table.
- Open the Overlay chat after onboarding instead of Work. Finishing setup now lands people in the conversation they just had, the workspace Overlay agent's thread on the Agents page, where they can keep talking; their first task is still created and runs in Work. The final onboarding message now says so, onboarding prefers the workspace chief agent so that thread is the Workspace Overlay chat, and the handoff that keeps the onboarding screen up until the next page paints now waits for the Agents page instead of the Work task panel. Tests cover the handoff and the new route. A member whose workspace isn't subscribed yet now sees **Finish setup** instead of **Open Work**, since that button also lands in the chat.
- Install the admin console's Vercel build with Bun. Its project still ran `npm ci` from the dashboard settings, which failed once the repository's npm lockfile was replaced by `bun.lock`; `apps/admin/vercel.json` now sets the install command (installing Bun first if the build image lacks it), so the setting lives in the repository.
- Switch the whole repository to one Bun install. Bun replaces npm as the package manager and script runner (`packageManager: bun@1.4.5`); every app still runs on Node.js, Electron or Hermes as before. Desktop, mobile and the Chrome extension join web and `packages/*` as workspaces of one `bun.lock`, replacing six npm lockfiles, and depend on shared packages with `workspace:*` instead of `file:` paths. The install uses Bun's isolated layout, so each package sees only what it declares: mobile keeps Expo's React 19.2.0 beside web's 19.2.8, and imports that only worked through npm hoisting are now declared (for example `tsx`, `sharp`, `@ai-sdk/gateway` at the root, React Navigation and the Babel test plugins in mobile). It also fixes mobile's code highlighting, which lazy-loaded an unrelated hoisted Shiki 3 while the shared UI uses Shiki 4. Existing versions were migrated from the old lockfiles, not re-resolved. A clean install of every surface from a warm cache took about 14 seconds locally. CI installs through one shared step with Bun's download cache; Vercel, EAS (`bun` in `eas.json`, agent packages built after install), Docker images and Dependabot use Bun. Supply-chain guards carry over: 7-day minimum release age (`bunfig.toml`), install scripts only for listed `trustedDependencies`, frozen-lockfile installs, and the production audit, license checks and desktop SBOM now walk `bun.lock` (`scripts/ci/bun-lock.mjs`) because `bun audit` cannot leave out dev dependencies. Nested npm overrides have no Bun equivalent, so the patched versions they forced are now pinned directly (`@vercel/cli-config` 0.4.0 and `@vercel/cli-auth` 0.4.2 for zod 4.6.7, `brace-expansion` 2.1.8 and 5.0.12, `axios` 1.21.0 to match the desktop's pin). npm still publishes the Agent Host packages. Docs and agent instructions now say `bun install` and `bun run`.
- Make the mobile app's tests pass against the current web code and run them whenever shared code changes. The `Mobile app` workflow now also runs on changes to `packages/`, `src/shared/`, `src/features/automations/`, `convex/` and `package-lock.json`, so a web change that breaks mobile fails CI before it lands. The 13 failing node tests and 17 failing transcript tests were stale mocks (the Workspace | Personal mode module and new agent-detail imports, a moved Chat route, a composer renamed to `MessageCapsule`), plus two scroll-gesture tests that read positions before the web's word-by-word stream pacing had finished moving the transcript; they now wait for it to settle. The node tests also now check that new chats, notes, automations and personal turns carry the current mode.
- Let shared copy and download buttons be handled by the host app. `@overlay/ui/platform-actions` provides `usePlatformActions` (browser clipboard and plain-text download by default) and a `PlatformActionsProvider` to override them; the generated-UI card, draft review and code blocks use it, and a failed generated-UI download now shows "Download failed. Try again." beside the button instead of failing silently. The mobile transcript uses the provider so Copy and Download act on the message that was tapped; it had been overriding `navigator.clipboard` for the whole page, which lost which message was copied and ignored Download.
- Turn on server push in the mobile app: signed-in devices with notification permission now register their Expo token with `notifications/tokens`, so mentions, direct messages and agent approvals reach the phone while the app is closed. Signing out unregisters the device.
- Fix the tests the repository rename broke, which had turned main's required CI red: three pull-request fixtures whose links said `LayerNorm/overlay` while their repository field still said `overlay-web`, and a link-chip test whose expected label shortened with the new name.
- Show an agent's reply step by step while it runs when another agent's reply started it. In rooms and work threads, a turn triggered by another agent's message was skipped by the step projection, which only accepted a person's message as the trigger, so only tool rows and the newest sentence showed until the turn ended. (The rest of the original fix, for person-triggered agent threads, landed separately on Nov 9.)
- Adapt the mobile push backend to current main: agent approvals now arrive as harness waits, so the removed connected-agent approval table no longer has its own push path, and the "has a message waiting" notice for an absent connected agent also pushes to mobile. Until any device registers, notifications schedule no delivery work, so the backend costs nothing before the mobile app turns server push on (it still keeps that off until device QA).
- Add native-authenticated Expo push registration with device/token rotation, cross-account ownership transfer and sign-out revocation. New activity, mentions, agent approvals and input waits schedule preference- and mute-aware push delivery; invalid Expo devices are revoked from send tickets and receipts. Account deletion also clears push registrations.
- Fix large tool results being wrapped over and over when a reply is saved. Each save stage (live progress, model stream, transcript, delivery, message save) shrank tool inputs and outputs over 4,000 characters again, including ones an earlier stage had already shrunk, so a result ended up three or four layers deep with its budget spent on escape characters and only about 150 characters of real data. Later turns, `read_chat` and trace views lost what earlier tools returned. Shrinking is now done once and left alone afterwards.
- Rename the GitHub repository from `LayerNorm/overlay-web` to `LayerNorm/overlay`, since it now holds every surface. Links, clone instructions, package metadata and the preview, production and build scripts now identify the repository by its permanent numeric ID instead of its name, so this and any later rename cannot break deployments; the Docker image stays `ghcr.io/layernorm/overlay-web`.
- Fix the mobile bundle picking up a web-only `@/` import: the shared API schemas imported the onboarding schema through the web alias, which the mobile app cannot resolve. They now use a relative import. The new mobile workflow caught this on its first run.
- Record the tenant decision for the new `usageDays` billing table in the self-hosting tenancy guide, which the tenant-boundary CI check requires and which main was failing.
- Bring every Overlay surface into this repository. The desktop app (`apps/desktop`), the mobile app (`apps/mobile`) and the Chrome extension (`apps/chrome`) move in with their history, replacing the two Git submodules and the untracked, separately versioned extension, so a change to shared code, an API or a feature can update and check every surface in one change. The desktop now links the shared desktop bridge package instead of keeping a synchronized copy. Mobile App Store builds and mobile CI use the repository's current shared code instead of cloning two older pinned web commits. Each app keeps its own lockfile and gets its own path-filtered workflow (`Desktop app`, `Mobile app`, `Chrome extension`); signed desktop releases now publish `desktop-v<version>` releases here, which installed Mac apps check for updates. Production web builds are skipped when a commit only changes those apps. Removes a dead web fallback that loaded Composio from the desktop folder. Root lint, typecheck and tests continue to cover only web, backend, packages and admin. Mobile's test suite still has failures that already existed against current web, and the Chrome side panel does not build against the current shared chat package; both are recorded in `internal/plans/MONOREPO_SURFACES_PLAN.md`. Adds the living doc `internal/develop/surfaces.md` with a per-surface feature parity table.
- Fix "something went wrong" on first load (`billing/browse:workspaceSummary` timing out). The workspace billing summary added up every charge of the billing period one by one, about 18,000 rows for a busy workspace, on every load and again after every charge. Charges now also add to daily totals per account, day, person, resource and model (`usageDays`), and the itemized usage list and Spend per person add up those few rows, reading single charges only for the partial day a period starts on. A one-time backfill (`billing/usageTotals:backfillDailyTotals`) counts earlier charges.
- Point the desktop submodule at Overlay Desktop 0.6.0, which ships desktop first-run setup, and record in the desktop onboarding plan that Phases 1–3 are live on the web and released on desktop, with Settings → Import and Phase 4 still to build.
- Record in the never-stop-runs plan that its first slice is deployed and that the stalled-run sweep reported the existing orphaned runs in production.
- Add first-run setup to Overlay Desktop for new accounts. Someone whose only workspace is the personal one made at sign-up, and who hasn't started setup, now gets six short screens in the desktop app before the Overlay conversation: who the workspace is for (a new team workspace is created right away), about you, bring your setup, model keys for private threads, this computer, and a plan (Free, or $8 a month through the existing checkout; team workspaces are paid only). Everyone else opens the app as before. Bring your setup lists what Claude Code, Codex, Claude Desktop, Cursor and Gemini CLI keep on the computer (instructions, skills, subagents and MCP servers) and imports only what's ticked: instructions become memories, skills become personal skills, subagents become creator-only agents and remote MCP servers become personal MCP servers. Local (command) MCP servers are shown as coming later. The desktop app reads and sends the contents itself, so file contents and header tokens never reach the page. The models screen offers keys found in the login shell (`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` and others, shown by name and last four characters) and checks them before keeping them in the keychain; Claude and ChatGPT plan sign-ins show as coming soon. The conversation then starts after the name, work and team questions (`start` accepts a `prefill`), and `GET /api/v1/onboarding/session` returns `desktopSetup` to decide who sees setup. Adds the `localSetup` bridge capability (Overlay Desktop 0.6.0), the plan `internal/plans/DESKTOP_ONBOARDING_PLAN.md` with its mockup, and tests for the gate, the prefilled start, the setup screens, scanning and importing. Also registers main's new `/api/v1/harness/stalled` callback as an internal route in the API boundary, which the docs check needs.
- Fix the stalled-run sweep missing older stuck runs. It read only the 200 most recently updated runs in its 30-day window, so a run stuck for days behind many newer finished runs was never checked. It now pages through the whole window, 100 runs per query.
- Stop ending agent runs on temporary failures. A dropped model call, a provider timeout, outage or rate limit, or an Overlay fault inside a model step used to end the run after a few tries ("The model connection dropped", "Overlay hit an internal error"). Model calls now retry until they succeed, backing off from 5 seconds to 5 minutes, and a faulted step is replayed from its last checkpoint with no limit. If tools had started before the fault, the replay tells the model which ones may have run so it checks before repeating them, and after two failures in a row it tells the model to split large output into smaller pieces.
- Alert on any run that still ends without finishing. Stopped runs (`harness_run_stopped`), runs on their fifth, tenth… retry (`harness_run_retrying`) and runs whose workflow died while they stayed running (`harness_run_orphaned`) are reported to Sentry. A Convex cron checks quiet running runs every 10 minutes against the Workflow SDK through a new internal route, `POST /api/v1/harness/stalled`.
- Keep a computer running while a terminal session (a build, render or server) is still running, even after the run that started it ends or pauses between tool calls. The idle check asks the machine instead of stopping it and killing the job.
- Add `internal/plans/NEVER_STOP_RUNS_PLAN.md`: how runs stop ending on timeouts, dropped connections, provider errors, deploys and crashes, so they end only when finished or cancelled and pause only for budget or the person's input. It takes ideas from Vercel's eve and from opencomputer and orders the work in seven phases with a fault-injection suite and a production alarm as proof. Long commands on a computer run as jobs that call back to the run when they finish or need input, with a heartbeat as the backstop, instead of being polled.
- Fix HTML pages, Markdown files and long memory imports, reported by users.
  - HTML files can now be saved to Files and viewed as working pages, in the Files page, the chat file panel and an agent's right panel. Before, Files refused HTML from an agent's computer (so agents saved pages as plain text), and a page kept in object storage showed its download path (`/api/v1/files/…/content`) instead of the page. Previews now load the file from `/api/v1/files/<id>/content?view=page`, which serves any `.html` file (kept in its row, as text in object storage, or as an upload) as its own document under `sandbox allow-scripts allow-forms allow-modals`: its scripts and buttons work, but on an opaque origin with no access to the app, its cookies or its APIs. Pages given inline (`srcdoc`, blob or data URLs) inherit the app's nonce-only script policy, which is why buttons on earlier previews did nothing.
  - Fix the content routes' security policy actually applying. The proxy set the app's policy (which allows inline scripts on API responses) before the route ran, and Next.js keeps a header that is already set, so a route's own `sandbox` policy was silently dropped. The proxy now leaves `/api/v1/files/<id>/content` and `/api/v1/outputs/<id>/content` to the route, which sandboxes HTML, SVG, XML and scripts (`nosniff` on everything). Without this, an uploaded HTML file opened directly would have run on overlay.works.
  - HTML and Markdown files in the Files page open rendered, with an Edit button for the source; plain text files still open as text. Before, both opened as a raw text box, so an agent's Markdown never looked formatted. Uploaded files kept in object storage are no longer offered as editable text, since their listed content is not their text.
  - The agent `write_file` tool now says to write Markdown as plain Markdown, never backslash-escaped, and that a web page should be a complete `.html` file. A user's agent-written `glossary.md` had every `**` escaped as `\*\*`, which no renderer shows as bold.
  - Saving a long context brief as memories (onboarding's "Save context", or any memory import) no longer fails with "memory content produced too many chunks". Pasted text is split into ~300-character memories, so the 20,000-character limit allowed up to about 67 pieces against a cap of 20. Neighbouring pieces are now joined into at most 20 memories, so every accepted paste is saved whole.

- Stop cutting off subscription models (Claude, Codex and the other connected subscriptions) after five minutes of silence. While a model builds one large tool call (a whole page or file), the subscription bridge sends nothing, and Node's default fetch ends a body that is silent for 300 seconds. The run failed with "The model connection dropped", retried the same call into the same wall, and stopped after several tries (diagnostic `hd_1v7ll0rnb4zyk`: seven five-minute failures before the stop). Calls to the bridge now allow the same 780 seconds of silence that BYOK endpoints got earlier, just under the 800-second workflow step limit.
- Show in global search (⌘K) whether each result is Personal or Workspace. A result from the other mode than the one you are in carries a small Personal or Workspace tag, and an All / Personal / Workspace switch at the right of the search box filters the results (agents and connectors, which have no scope, show under All only). Neither appears in a one-person workspace. Search results now carry their scope from Convex, which also makes opening a result land in its own mode instead of the current one. The search dialog also drops its dividing lines under the search box and category header, matching the rest of the app. This was the last open item of the tool-scoping plan, whose remaining facts now live in `internal/develop/workspace-and-personal.md` and `internal/develop/tool-catalog.md`; the plan is deleted.
- Rebuild the home page around Overlay as the AI Chief of Staff. The old page sold "self-driving companies with AI employees" and still advertised connecting Codex, Claude Code and Cursor, which Overlay no longer supports; agent avatars, the Overlay orchestrator, Slack and personal/workspace scopes were not shown at all.
  - New headline ("The AI Chief of Staff that works with your team") and subline, with Get started and Add @Overlay to Slack (the Slack install link, which signs visitors in first). Page title, description and share text follow.
  - The hero shows the team of agent creatures around Overlay and a taller, clickable preview of the app laid out like the real shell: the icon rail switches between Work, Agents, Chats and Files, Personal/Workspace changes the data, agents and threads open their conversations, and the right panel has Details, Library and Computer. A second preview shows a team channel where people, agents and Overlay work together.
  - New sections: AI agents with superpowers (memory, a 3×3 grid of tools, a computer), Overlay assigning work down a tree of agents, AI employees on an org chart, multiplayer channels, personal vs workspace scopes, make your company self-driving, in the cloud and accessible from anywhere, Overlay in Slack, and a plain closing call to action. The Overlay for Mac section, the open-source/multiplayer/cloud grid and the "Get Overlay" platform card are replaced.
  - The header is now Product, Pricing, Docs and Blog with Discord and GitHub buttons. Product opens a menu of what Overlay does and where it runs: web, Mac, mobile browser, Slack and MCP apps.
  - The FAQ answers what an AI Chief of Staff is, what agents can do, where Overlay runs and how personal and workspace spaces differ, instead of describing connected agents.
  - The approved mockup is `internal/plans/mockups/landing-chief-of-staff.html` (rebuild with `python3 internal/plans/mockups/build-landing-chief-of-staff.py`).
- Tidy the primary rail and hide scrollbars. The rail's bottom held four look-alike icon buttons (theme, help, settings, and a Workspace | Personal switch whose two people icons were hard to tell apart).
  - The Workspace | Personal switch moves to the bottom of the rail, right above your account button, and Workspace now uses a building icon, so the two modes no longer look alike. Both modes stay inside the current workspace.
  - Dark/Light mode, Settings, Help and docs, and Submit a bug move into the account menu, above Sign out. The Help popover is replaced by those rows; the bug report dialog itself is unchanged.
  - Scrollbars are no longer drawn anywhere in the app. Lists, transcripts and the right panel still scroll.
  - The temporary chat button in the chat header loses its gray fill and only shows a background on hover, like the other header icon buttons.
- Stop agents writing their answer twice after a tool fails. A failed or refused tool result made the agent post a "progress update" right away, while it already had everything for its answer. It wrote the answer as that update and then again as the reply, so a room message showed the same paragraph two or three times. A failure now asks for an update only if the agent goes on to more tool work without saying anything; when its next step is the answer, the answer explains the failure itself.
- Let agents find archived skills and automations to restore. `list_skills` and `list_automations` take `archived: true`, as `list_files` and `list_tasks` already do; in a room others can read, only archived Workspace items are listed. Archived automations are paged (`beforeUpdatedAt` on `GET /api/v1/automations`), so an older one is still found. Turning on an archived automation is now refused with "restore it first"; before, it reported the automation as on while it stayed hidden and never ran.
- Turn on Data and APIs for agents saved before it existed. Their saved tool lists did not name the new tools, so only new agents and the default agent could run paid APIs. A one-time data update (`migrations/grantApiTools:run`, run once per Convex deployment after it ships) adds the four tools to every agent with a saved tool list. Agents with an empty list are left alone, and turning the group off later sticks.
- Record the private threads release in its plan: Phase 3 (Overlay tools and the free tier) and the Phase 1–2 gap fixes are merged and live on web, Convex production is deployed, Agent Host 0.7.0 is on npm, and Overlay Desktop 0.5.0, which bundles it, is signed, notarized and published.
- Update the `overlay-mobile` submodule to the iOS app with the Workspace | Personal switch. It sits in the tab bar's bottom-right circle, and Search moves into each tab's header. Every tab follows the chosen mode, as the web does, and the app catches up with web production: one-way Move to workspace, no bring-your-own agents, Guest role or Slack importer, and Spend per person on Billing. Web and server code are unchanged.

- Give private threads (Cloud sync off, Overlay Desktop) their agent's Overlay tools, and add a free tier for them. Before, a private thread could only use the computer it ran on: no memory, notes, files, tasks or web search. Now, before each turn, Overlay Desktop asks the server for a one-hour token for that person, workspace and agent (`POST /api/v1/mcp/private-thread`) with its own signed-in session, so the page never holds it, and the Agent Host calls the agent's tools over Overlay's MCP server (`/api/mcp`). Each call sends only what that tool needs; the conversation still stays on the computer. If no token can be had (signed out, offline), the turn runs with the computer's tools only. Which tools: in a paid workspace, the agent's whole grant, charged as usual. In an unpaid workspace with only this person, the new free tier: memory, files and notes, tasks, skills and web search, with 100 MB of storage per person (up from 10 MB) and 50 web searches a month per person, counted on the server before each search (new `privateThreadAllowances` table); at the limit the tool tells the agent plainly instead of failing. Memories and files saved in a workspace without a plan (or without budget left) are now stored as text and found by word search, instead of not being indexed at all and making search fail; search by meaning still needs a plan, and items saved this way are not re-indexed with embeddings after an upgrade until they change. Team workspaces without a paid plan get no Overlay tools. Overlay Cloud computers, media generation, automations, connected apps and Overlay-billed models stay paid. The paid-first plan is amended to allow this one exception, since Overlay runs no inference for private threads. The Agent Host and bridge protocol are now 0.7.0 (adds `@ai-sdk/mcp`), and the composer's Cloud sync note says what Overlay tools send.
- Close the gaps left in private threads' first release. Private threads now get the same screen tools as synced threads on this computer: screenshots and mouse and keyboard input on macOS, and the browser and open-URL tools on macOS and Linux, with the same approval rules (typing, keys and clicks marked consequential ask first). Long private threads no longer fail when they outgrow the model's context window: before a step that would pass 80% of it, older messages are folded into a summary written by the thread's own model, using the cloud harness's compaction, and the summary is saved with the thread so later turns don't summarize again. Screenshot images are dropped from finished turns, so they aren't stored or sent to the model again. The computer tools' inputs, approval rules and browser result text, and the compaction code, now live in one place in the bridge protocol package, used by both the cloud and the Agent Host, instead of being copied. A workspace that never set up billing can now connect This computer (it gets the free limit of one computer) instead of failing with a server error, which is what lets the free tier reach private threads at all. At the computer limit, connecting a computer now replaces your own offline one (the one unseen longest) instead of refusing, so reinstalling the app or moving to a new laptop doesn't leave you stuck: there was no other way to remove an old computer. Online computers and other members' computers are never replaced, and the refusal that remains says to upgrade in plain words. From review: a free `web_fetch` of several pages now counts each page against the monthly allowance (before, a ten-page batch counted as one); deleting an account now deletes the person's allowance counts; Stop and the 15-second deadline now also cover loading the tool list, and a connection that finishes after Stop is closed; and `internal/develop/mcp-access.md` and `internal/develop/tool-catalog.md` now describe private-thread tokens (no grant row, one hour, membership checked on every request) and the free tier's tools. New host tests cover compaction, quitting mid-turn and reopening, screenshot images and the per-platform tool list.
- Let a run recover by itself from "Overlay hit an internal error" when that is safe. If Overlay's own run records disagree, or an unclassified error is thrown, before any tool of that step has started, the run now waits briefly and asks the model for that step again, up to twice per hour-long segment. If a tool had already started, the run still stops, so nothing (a sent message, a created record) can happen twice. Revoked access, funding, deadline and provider-account failures are never retried. Each recovery is logged with its cause.
- Stop long agent-to-agent asks from ending with "Overlay hit an internal error" after about two hours (diagnostic `hd_1wnui4flb6dda`). The asking agent's run moves to a fresh workflow segment every hour. When that happened while it waited on another agent inside a tool call, the wait surfaced as a blank tool failure and the run kept its "waiting for children" label, so its next tool call failed the ownership check ("Native delivery execution changed") and stopped the task.
  - The label a run shows (running, or what it waits on) no longer decides whether the run may act. The harness state and the workflow owner decide that; only a finished, failed or cancelled run is refused. This removes the whole class: the same mismatch had already stopped asks twice before.
  - An hourly handoff during a wait is no longer a tool failure. The agent is told the other agent is still working and to read again, and the run moves to its next segment.
  - Any wait on another agent that ends without a result sets the run back to running.
  - When the ownership check does fail, the server log now says which record disagreed.
- Keep an agent's task alive when an agent it asked fails. Asking another agent (for example the Chief asking the LinkedIn Agent) could stop both with "Overlay hit an internal error":
  - A dropped connection while a model call was being claimed, before it was sent (an HTTP/2 GOAWAY reported as "fetch failed"), ended the asked agent at once. It now gets the same automatic retries, up to three, as a call that drops mid-response.
  - A run that ends with a model call reserved but never sent now drops that marker. It cost nothing and can never be sent, but it blocked the asking agent from collecting the result.
  - If waiting for an asked agent ends in an error, the asking agent's run is set back to running before the agent continues. It stayed "waiting for children", so the asking agent's next tool call failed its ownership check and stopped the whole task.
- Let a room message include one of your Personal items on purpose. In a channel or group DM an agent only sees Workspace items; now a note, file or automation you @-mention in your message (the room's @ menu already listed them) is sent with it, and the agent's reply to that message may open it. Nothing else Personal becomes reachable, and lists and searches stay Workspace-only. Room messages store these as `includedItems` (at most 10).
- Finish most of the tool-scoping plan's open items, so agents work with Personal and Workspace the way people do.
  - Agents can move a Personal item to Workspace, archive it, or restore it (`move_to_workspace`, `archive_item`, `restore_item`) under the person's own rules, for files, notes, outputs, skills, automations and tasks. They are offered only when the person's message in that turn asks for it, also for agents whose grant includes them, and never to outside apps. In a room others can read nothing moves, and only Workspace items archive or restore. Archiving a folder no longer takes along a Personal file inside it that the caller could not archive on its own. `list_files` can list archived items so an agent can find what to restore.
  - Teammates can open Workspace files and generated media saved to Workspace. The content link used to answer only the creator, so a shared image listed but would not load. A video the server refuses to start is now reported as failed instead of pending.
  - File, note and task results say whose they are (`owner: you` or the creator's name), so an agent can say "Maya's note" instead of guessing.
  - Generated images and videos can be saved to Workspace (`scope`), and always are in a shared room or workspace automation. They are now saved in the workspace they were made in; before, they had no workspace, so they never appeared in workspace lists and could not be moved. `migrations/outputWorkspaces:backfillOutputWorkspacesByServer` fixes existing ones from their chat.
  - `migrations/personalMemoryVisibility:makePersonalMemoriesPrivateByServer` marks people's memories saved before private-by-default as theirs, with their search chunks, so a teammate can no longer recall them. Agents' memories stay workspace knowledge.
- Add Data and APIs: agents and chats can now find and run thousands of paid third-party APIs through Monid (lead and contact search, company and people enrichment, social and review data, scraping, SEO, 3D models, speech and more) without the person connecting an account or buying a subscription.
  - Four new tools: `search_apis` finds endpoints in plain words with their prices, `inspect_api` shows an endpoint's input schema, price and notes, `run_api` runs it, waits for slow ones up to 10 minutes, and saves the files Monid returns to Files, and `save_api_file` saves a file a result links to (such as a 3D model) to Files, only for this workspace's runs. Very large results are saved whole to Files with a preview.
  - Each run is checked against the workspace budget first and charged afterwards at Monid's reported cost plus the usual usage fee (for slow runs, the cost Monid reports when the run is read back); its cap at Monid is what the workspace has left, so a run cannot overspend it. Retries never charge twice.
  - Only read-only endpoints run. Endpoints that create or change accounts, phone numbers, inboxes or other stored resources, or that send messages, place calls or make bookings, are refused, because Overlay's one Monid account is shared by every customer.
  - Offered on paid plans when the deployment has `MONID_API_KEY`: always in personal chat, to new agents as the "Data and APIs" group (existing agents turn it on), and to outside apps at the Everything level. Chat rows read "Searched APIs for …", "Read … API details" and "Ran … · $0.12".
  - The self-verticalize skill now looks for the domain's APIs and records which endpoint to use for each step. New living doc `internal/develop/monid.md`.
  - Paid runs settle reliably: a failed status check no longer abandons a run Monid accepted (it is retried, or the run is stopped and any cost still charged), runs in one agent step go one at a time so they cannot overspend the same balance, outside apps over MCP wait at most 4 minutes so a run is stopped and charged before the 5-minute request limit, the tools stay offered on a paid plan with a low balance so auto top-up can start, and an unreadable side-effect catalog page now refuses every run instead of shortening the blocklist.
- Move every CI job from Namespace runners back to GitHub's free `ubuntu-24.04` runners (the repository is public). Namespace's trial capped us at 32 vCPU, about one pull request's worth, so jobs waited 1–3.5 minutes typically and 10–15 minutes at worst; on GitHub, jobs started in about 3 seconds when it ran CI in October. Jobs run on smaller machines, so each one takes longer; a contract test now fails if any workflow uses another runner. The Namespace-only dependency-volume experiment is removed.
- Speed up required CI without checking less. The lint, typecheck, compilation and test jobs no longer download the ~176 MB of generated mockup HTML under `internal/plans` (no check reads it; secret scanning still scans everything). The shared dependency step now pins exact Node patch versions, so pull requests stop missing main's dependency cache when runner images ship different patches; merge-queue runs no longer save dependency trees and only main saves the Next.js compiler cache, so the full 10 GB Actions cache stops evicting main's copies. Each run notes whether the dependency cache hit. A new `scripts/ci/ci-timing-audit.mjs` splits recent CI jobs into waiting, setup and running time, and `internal/plans/CI_SPEED_PLAN.md` records the audit and the remaining phases.
- Keep workspace agents off personal providers, and scope Settings → Providers by mode. A workspace agent set to its creator's personal key or subscription failed every turn in a channel or Slack thread (workspace runs may only spend workspace connections), and only showed "internal error".
  - Settings → Providers follows the Workspace | Personal switch: Personal lists and adds your own connections, Workspace lists and adds the workspace's, which every member can use (a teammate's are read-only). API keys can now be workspace connections too, not only subscriptions. The add dialog says who can use the new connection; onboarding adds the workspace's.
  - Workspace agents can only be saved, or moved to the workspace, with an Overlay model or a workspace provider, and their model pickers offer only those.
  - `migrations/workspaceAgentModels:moveWorkspaceAgentsOffPersonalModelsByServer` moves existing workspace agents on a personal, missing or other workspace's connection to the default Overlay model and lists them.
  - A run that still meets a personal model ends at once (no retries) with its own message (`WORKSPACE_PERSONAL_MODEL`) instead of an internal error.
- Record in the tool-scoping plan that the room-rule work (PR 625) is merged and deployed.
- Finish removing the workspace Guest role: the production migration ran (no guest memberships, 2 guest invitations turned into member invitations, nothing else to clear), so the schema no longer accepts `guest`, the `workspaceResourceGuests` table and the retired `guestExpirationDays` and Slack `guests` fields are gone, and the one-time migration and the helper that read old guest rows as members are deleted.
- Show owners and admins what each person spends, and let them cap it (Workspace → Billing → Spend per person). Personal mode spends the workspace's budget, and until now nobody could see or limit one person's share. The card lists every member's spend this billing period, their automations and agents included, live. A cap counts what the person already spent this period, stops their paid work once reached with "Your spending limit in this workspace is used up", and renews every period.
  - Every charge now counts against the person who started it as well as the paying program, and every check before a paid call reads both. Before, a call paid for by an automation or agent skipped its owner's own limit.
  - Spend limits renew instead of expiring: a limit whose period ended used to block everything until an admin reset it.
  - Chats and agent turns now honour spend limits. The Harness model boundary only checked the workspace's 2% line, so a limit stopped media, transcription and computers but never an ordinary chat. A capped person's task ends with its own message (`MEMBER_SPEND_LIMIT`: "You have reached the spending cap a workspace admin set for you"), not a generic error.
- Remove the workspace Guest role. No customer has asked for it, and it complicated every permission check. Everyone in a workspace is now an owner, admin or member.
  - Existing guests become ordinary members, and guest invitations become member invitations. Until the migration below has run, a stored guest already reads as a member.
  - Item-level guest sharing is gone with it: Settings → Workspace → Guests, "Invite a guest", the Share dialog's "Invite as guest", the guest role in the role picker and role list, the guest expiry policy, and the Slack "Guests" toggle (it only covered Overlay guests; Slack Connect is unchanged). Server routes, services and Convex functions for resource guests are deleted, and the Share dialog's unused `canInvite` flag is dropped from the sharing response.
  - Invitations, role changes and Convex argument validators no longer accept `guest`; separate, stricter rate-limit buckets for guests are gone.
  - After deploying, run `migrations/removeGuestRole:migrateGuestsToMembersByServer` once per `table` (`memberships`, `invitations`, `resourceGuests`, `sharingPolicies`, `slackSettings`) with `dryRun: true` first, then until `isDone`. It turns guest memberships and invitations into member ones, deletes the leftover `workspaceResourceGuests` rows, and clears the retired `guestExpirationDays` and Slack `guests` fields. The `guest` schema literals, those fields, the `workspaceResourceGuests` table and `convex/lib/legacyGuestRole.ts` are removed in a follow-up once it has run everywhere.
- Close the remaining gaps in the room rule, so agents in a room others can read, and workspace automations, can no longer reach anyone's Personal items through their tools. Before, only lists and searches were limited: an agent could still read or edit a Personal file, note, task or automation by id, its new notes, files, tasks and automations landed in the summoner's or automation owner's Personal, computer tools could fall back to the summoner's own Mac, and it could ask their private agents or Personal Chief.
  - Reading or changing an item by id (`read_file`, `write_file`, `move_file`, `get_note` and the note edits, `delete_note`, `get_task`, `update_task`, `comment_on_task`, `update_automation`, `pause_automation`, `delete_automation`) refuses a Personal item there, with a message the agent can pass on.
  - Notes, files, folders, tasks and automations an agent creates there go to Workspace. Elsewhere the create tools take an optional `scope`, Personal by default. `save_skill` saves nothing there, since an unreviewed skill would steer every agent.
  - Computer tools there use only the agent's own cloud computer, never a person's Mac or personal cloud computer.
  - A person's creator-only agents and Personal Chief can't be asked from there (`ask_private_agent`), decided on the server from the asking conversation.
  - Every search hit, and every file, note and task an agent lists or reads, now says whether it is Personal or Workspace.
- Retire the finished Workspace and Personal plan: its rules and where each is enforced now live in the living doc `internal/develop/workspace-and-personal.md` (added to the AGENTS.md doc table), code comments point there, and the open guest question moved to `todos.md`.
- Record the live preview test of Workspace and Personal P6 in the plan (workspace automation without its owner's private memory or personal-only tools).
- Give people their baseline member role when they sign in (password, sign-in callback and the preview test login), not only when they first open the app. Automation runs enforce that role's capabilities, so someone who had only used the API, and every fresh PR preview driven by scripts, had every automation refused with `authorization_denied models.use`. Opening the app still does the same thing, and a failure at sign-in is logged without blocking it.
- Record Workspace and Personal P6 and P7 as merged, deployed and backfilled in the plan (PR 621; 1,665 production file chunks marked private, none in Workspace yet).
- Run workspace automations with the workspace's reach, and make workspace files searchable by every member (Workspace and Personal plan, P6 and P7). Before, a workspace automation ran with its creator's personal connectors, MCP servers, model keys, private memories and skills, so anyone who could edit or trigger it acted through someone else's accounts, and what it learned became the creator's private memory. Workspace files were indexed for their uploader only, so nobody else's chats or Personal Chief could search them.
  - A workspace automation's thread now counts as a workspace context, decided in the same place as shared rooms: only workspace connectors (`workspace_…` tools) and workspace MCP servers, workspace-scoped lists and searches, workspace memories, skills and retrieval, and no memory profile. Runs without an agent re-check the automation's scope on every step. What it learns is workspace knowledge, kept by its agent or, without one, by the workspace chief.
  - Shared rooms and workspace automations may only use workspace model connections. A personal key or subscription is refused with "Workspace runs use workspace accounts", instead of being spent on everyone's behalf.
  - A file in Workspace is now indexed once for the workspace, so one search finds it for every member who isn't a guest; a teammate's private files can't crowd it out, and it stays searchable after its uploader leaves. Personal and archived files stay indexed for their uploader only, and a file chunk with no marking counts as private. Moving, archiving or restoring a file (or a folder and its contents) re-files its chunks in small batches until done. Knowledge search, automatic retrieval in rooms and `search_in_files` now find workspace files. `knowledge/knowledge:backfillFileChunkVisibilityByServer` re-files existing chunks (dry run first, until `isDone`).
  - A workspace automation's own messages (its instructions) are no longer extracted as its creator's private memories.
  - The agent-context security test now runs in CI (it was missing from the Vitest config).
- Show Move to Workspace and Archive in an open note's header (Workspace and Personal plan, P4 follow-up). The editor dropped a note's creator and scope when it loaded the note, so the header never offered them and notes could only move from the Files list; `NotebookNote` now keeps `userId`, `scope`, `archivedAt` and `archivedFromScope`. Also delete `integrations/slack:hasPersonalOverlayByServer`, which only the web release before PR 617 called; production web has run later releases since.
- Record Workspace and Personal P4 and P5 as merged and deployed in the plan, and add the `lib/agentVisibility` module that PR 620 left out of the generated Convex API types (the production deploy regenerated it).
- Make moving something to the workspace one-way, confirmed, and honest about accounts (Workspace and Personal plan, P4 and P5). Before, anything could be moved back and forth between Personal and Workspace with one click, an automation could become a workspace automation while still running as its creator's private agent, a personal connector could be relabeled as a workspace connector that still used the person's own account, and sharing an MCP server shared its creator's sign-in with every member.
  - Nothing moves back to Personal. The scope engine, `POST /api/v1/scope` and Work's own update refuse it for files, notes, skills, MCP servers, connectors, automations and tasks (`one_way`), and the Move to Personal buttons are gone. Agent visibility follows the same rule: a workspace agent can't be made private again, and only its creator can move a creator-only agent to the workspace (when the workspace lets members move items, or they are an owner or admin), which also adds it to the workspace's public channels like a new workspace agent. The confirmation says that its computer, and anything signed in on it, becomes usable by everyone. Agents can no longer move a Work task through `update_task`; that one-way move needs a person's confirmation.
  - Every Move to Workspace, single or bulk, now asks first: "Move to workspace?", what changes, and "You can't move it back to Personal." A saved agent's editor shows its access read-only, with Move to workspace (confirmed) for a private agent; after the move the editor opens in Workspace, and saving the editor never sends access, so a stale form can't undo a move.
  - Moving an automation checks its agent. If the agent is your private agent, the dialog offers "Move {agent} to the workspace too" (ticked by default) and both move in one transaction; without it the move is refused. Someone else's private agent or your Personal Chief blocks the move.
  - Connectors no longer move at all (`connector_reconnect_for_workspace`). In Personal, a connected connector's menu offers Connect for workspace, which starts the existing workspace-account sign-in.
  - Moving an MCP server to the workspace leaves your sign-in behind: tokens, bearer tokens and custom headers are removed in the same mutation, OAuth servers show Not connected, and a server that had credentials is off until its creator connects it again and someone turns it on (connecting no longer switches it on by itself, so a server turned off on purpose stays off). MCP servers already shared before this change keep their credential.
  - Removes the Workspace and Personal mockup (`internal/plans/mockups/workspace-personal.html` and its feature): every screen it showed is now production.
  - Tests cover the one-way refusal for every resource and for Work updates, agent visibility (both directions, who may move, channel join), automations moving with and without their agent, connectors refused, MCP credentials stripped, the member-move setting for agents, the route's `withAgent` handling (the request schema used to drop it, so automation moves with their agent always failed) and the editor's access display. Tested on the PR preview: notes, agents, automations with their agent, and MCP servers; Connect for workspace was not exercised with a live sign-in.
- Split Overlay into a Workspace mode and a Personal mode, and give each person a Personal Chief that lives only in Personal (Workspace and Personal plan, P2 and P3). Before, every section had its own Personal | Workspace toggle and opened on Personal, each person's personal chief was also called "Overlay", and a second, shared copy of every member's chief was listed in Workspace and joined every public channel, so it could be mentioned there.
  - One Workspace | Personal switch now sits at the top of the primary rail (also on Settings and in the mobile drawer). Workspace is the default everywhere, including a bare workspace link, and the choice is remembered. Sections no longer have their own toggle. Links now name Personal (`scope=personal`) and leave Workspace out.
  - Agents are listed by who can see them, not by who created them: Workspace lists Overlay and workspace-visible agents; Personal lists your Personal Chief and your creator-only agents. A new agent takes its visibility from the mode it is created in, so the New agent dialog no longer asks.
  - Your Personal Chief is named "{First name}'s Chief" ("Personal Chief" without a name), is created the first time you open Personal instead of for every member when anyone loads the agent list, and is not listed in Workspace, in mentions, people lists or Settings → Agents. In Slack it only answers its owner in a DM. Its computer is visible only to you, not to the workspace owner. Personal chiefs no longer draw the Overlay mark.
  - A new channel no longer adds every active agent: personal chiefs and creator-only agents stay out of channels, so teammates can't mention someone's private agent there. Found in preview QA, where a member's mention list in a new channel showed the owner's creator-only agent and both members' chiefs.
  - Three one-time Convex mutations clean up existing data, in this order. `migrations/personalChiefs:retireSharedPersonalChiefsByServer` archives the shared copies (they leave every room and can't be mentioned; rows and threads are kept). `migrations/personalChiefs:removePersonalAgentsFromChannelsByServer` takes personal chiefs and creator-only agents out of channels they were added to. `migrations/personalChiefs:renamePersonalChiefsByServer` renames chiefs still called "Overlay" or "…'s Overlay" and keeps names people chose. All take `dryRun`.
  - Review fixes: the agent page and Agents panel create the Personal Chief from the mode on screen, so it appears even when browser storage is blocked. A personal workspace's old default agent becomes its owner's Personal Chief on any visit, so it never shows as a second "Overlay" in Workspace. Retiring a shared chief copy hands its threads and automations to chiefs their people can still open: the owner's own threads and personal automations go to the owner's private chief, other members' threads and automations and workspace automations to the workspace chief, and an automation's thread always follows the automation. Archived workspaces and owners who left or are now guests are skipped and counted (`leftBehind`) instead of failing the run. The rename only touches names earlier versions generated, never one a person typed. Personal chiefs and creator-only agents can't be added to an existing channel or a group DM, and the private-channel picker no longer offers them. A departed member's chief computer is visible to the workspace owner again for cleanup. A lone chief tile grows to fit its name. `integrations/slack:hasPersonalOverlayByServer` stays until production web runs this release (todo to delete it).
  - Tests cover the Workspace default, the rail switch, the agent rule per mode, lazy chief creation, naming, the Slack DM rule, computer visibility, channel and group-DM membership and the three migrations.
- Record that the Workspace and Personal plan's P1 (memories follow the place) is merged, deployed and migrated in production (192 agent memories moved to their person), and what was tested.
- Make memories follow the place they were learned in, not the agent that saved them (Workspace and Personal separation, P1). Before, every workspace agent remembered as itself, so something you told it in a private 1:1 chat became workspace knowledge that teammates' agents and Slack channels could recall, and a creator-only agent's memories were recalled by the whole workspace. Now an agent adds workspace knowledge only in a workspace context, and only if the whole workspace can see the agent. A workspace context is a room others can read, such as a Slack channel or a shared chat, or a thread the agent was asked in from one. Everything it learns in a 1:1 chat is the person's private memory. So is everything it learns in an automation, which still reads its owner's private memories: until workspace automations get workspace-only reach (plan phase P5), saving what they learn for the workspace could leak private facts. Personal Overlays and creator-only agents never make workspace knowledge. This applies to memories agents save with tools, to memories extracted from their replies (Convex now checks the room and the thread's lineage itself) and to automations. The memory API refuses agent-owned writes for creator-only agents and personal Overlays. Settings → Memories marks your private memories as Private and adds a Move to workspace action with a confirmation, which calls a native Convex mutation that checks the signed-in owner. The move is one-way, and memory updates can no longer change who can see a memory. Checked on the PR preview: a workspace agent's save in a 1:1 thread stayed private, its save in a public channel became shared workspace knowledge that a teammate can see, and the move works in light and dark at desktop and phone widths. A one-time server mutation (`knowledge/knowledge:moveAgentMemoriesToTheirPlaceByServer`) moves existing agent memories: a personal Overlay's to its owner, a creator-only agent's to its creator, and a workspace agent's from a 1:1 chat to that person. Memories from shared rooms, or with no known room, stay shared. It replaces the personal-Overlay-only move, which already ran in production. Review fixes:
  - Reply extraction now uses the same room lineage as tool saves. A thread an agent was asked in from a shared room counts as shared.
  - Moving a memory updates its search index directly, so teammates' agents can recall it. A re-index would have skipped the unchanged text.
  - The one-time move also moves search vectors to the new owner.
  - A private extracted memory never merges into one the person already shared.

  Tests cover the owner rule, the backfill, the one-way move, and automation threads staying private.
- Run an agent's independent tool calls at the same time. When a model asks for several tools in one step, read-only lookups (web search and fetch, memory, notes, tasks and file reads) now run together instead of one after another, and the agent is told to batch independent calls. Calls that touch the same thing still run one at a time in the order the model asked: writes to one file (including the same file spelled differently, such as `/tmp/a.txt` and `/tmp/sub/../a.txt`), a file and its publication to Files, commands on one computer, screenshots and other desktop or browser actions, and edits to one note or task. Reading a note or task waits for an earlier edit to it, so an agent that edits and then checks sees its change. File calls with a relative or `~` path lock the whole computer, because their file can't be known in advance. Live tool rows in agent threads now update one call at a time on the server, so calls running together no longer hide or reopen each other's rows. Control tools, calls that need approval and unclassified tools still run alone. Because overlapping calls each renew the run's lease, a renewal that loses the race to another call now retries after checking that the same execution still owns the run, and the harness admits calls one at a time; before this, one of the overlapping calls could fail before it ran. Tests cover the locks, the retry and the one-at-a-time admission.
- Add the Overlay iOS app as the `overlay-mobile` Git submodule (`LayerNorm/overlay-mobile`), next to `overlay-desktop`, so web, desktop and mobile changes can be made and checked together in one checkout. The mobile app already reads `packages/*` and `src/shared/*` from the web repository; it now reads them from `main` instead of a separate web copy. Root lint and React Doctor skip the folder, as they do for desktop, and the architecture guide explains how to set it up. The mobile code pinned here was written against an older web branch and does not typecheck against `main` yet, so that work is still to do.
- Stop the workflow step projector that filled production logs with about 100 errors a minute. Since automations started running as messages in their own thread, their runs store an `automation-thread:<runId>` owner token instead of a Workflow SDK run ID. The projector still asked the Workflow SDK for those IDs every ten seconds, got `WorkflowWorldError` back each time and never marked a run finished, so it retried the same runs forever. It had nothing left to project, so the Convex cron, its runner and the `POST /api/v1/automations/project-events` bridge are removed. Runs, their threads and their status are unaffected. Step events projected before that stay readable. A new CI test checks that every Convex cron points at a function that still exists, because cron targets written as `internal as any` escape typechecking.
- Add a plan for separating Workspace and Personal (`internal/plans/WORKSPACE_PERSONAL_SEPARATION_PLAN.md`): everything belongs to exactly one place, Workspace is the default, personal contexts may read workspace but never the reverse, and only the owner moves something from personal to workspace. It maps how memories, files, chats, agents, skills, automations, connectors, MCP servers, provider keys and computers are owned today, the leaks found, and seven phases to fix them. Planning only; no behavior changes.
- Record the hourly Slack state cleanup in the on-prem Convex inventory so its CI check passes.
- Keep what your personal Overlay remembers private to you. It used to save memories as itself, which made them workspace knowledge: anyone's Workspace Overlay could recall them, including in a Slack channel where everyone reads the answer (found while testing Slack on the PR 612 preview). Memories it saves while chatting, from automations or from its replies now belong to its owner and are private, so shared rooms never use them. Other agents still remember for the workspace. A one-time server mutation (`knowledge/knowledge:movePersonalOverlayMemoriesToOwnersByServer`) moves memories saved before this to their owners.
- Fix agent turns that included an approved or denied step failing when they finished. Saving the turn wrote the approval's id and status on the tool step, which the message schema didn't allow, so the whole turn failed with a schema error and no answer was saved. The schema now accepts those optional fields. Found while testing Slack approvals on the PR 612 preview.
- Add Overlay to Slack, Phase 3. Owners and admins can now choose who may ask Overlay in Slack: all linked members, everyone except people they block, or only people they allow, with a toggle per linked member on Settings › Connected apps › Slack. The Guests toggle now works. Someone who asks before linking their account no longer loses the question: it is kept for an hour and answered as soon as they link. People from another organization in a Slack Connect channel get a short note that Overlay only answers members of the workspace that added it; before, a bug looked them up in their own Slack workspace and they got no reply at all. Answers for Slack users without an Overlay account are not included yet. Review fixes: a second message in a Slack thread while the first is still being taken in is no longer dropped; when Overlay accepts a question but can't start on it (budget, missing Overlay, or a question asked before linking that is now refused) the asker gets a private note with the reason or an Open Overlay link instead of silence; Ask privately can't start two runs from two clicks and its button is replaced by the result; a retried reply posts only the missing part instead of repeating earlier messages, and every part of the turn's answer is posted; questions keep their links, people's names and line breaks; agent replies can no longer ping @channel, @here, @everyone or people; a revoked bot token removes the installation like an uninstall; org-wide Enterprise Grid installs are refused with a clear message on Settings; expired Slack state is deleted hourly; a web reply only followed by outside (MCP) agents is no longer mirrored to Slack; the "replied in Overlay" header is bold.
- Add Overlay to Slack, Phase 2. When the person who asked in Slack replies to that thread in the web app, the reply is posted in the Slack thread under the Overlay bot as "<name> replied in Overlay", and Overlay's answer follows it there. When a run needs approval, Slack gets a card with Approve, Deny and Open in Overlay; only the person who asked can answer it, and the answer goes through the same check as the web approval (shared in `submitAgentRunApproval`). In a channel thread, people who have a personal Overlay get a one-time "Continue privately" button that moves their question to a direct-message thread with their personal Overlay, where their own memories and files are available. Not yet tested against a real Slack app.
- Add Overlay to Slack, Phase 1. An owner or admin adds the Overlay Slack app from Settings › Connected apps › Slack, and members link their Slack account with Sign in with Slack. Mentioning Overlay in a channel or messaging it directly becomes a message in the asker's own Workspace Overlay thread, which runs like any web turn with the asker's access; the reply is posted back to the Slack thread with Slack-ready formatting (tables, code, citations as links, long replies split). People who haven't linked get a private prompt to connect; guests are not answered, and channels shared with other companies only when the workspace turns Slack Connect on. Slack channel threads are treated as shared rooms, so Overlay does not load the asker's personal memories, files or skills there, and a reply is not posted if the asker unlinks or leaves the workspace before it finishes. Settings › Connected apps becomes a list of GitHub, Slack and MCP, each with its own page, and threads from Slack show a Slack mark and an Open in Slack link. Built on Vercel's Chat SDK with its state kept in Convex. Needs the `SLACK_*` settings in `internal/develop/slack-integration.md`.
- Add a proposed plan and mockup for Overlay in Slack (`internal/plans/SLACK_WORKSPACE_OVERLAY_PLAN.md`, `internal/plans/mockups/slack-integration.html`). Slack becomes a first-class integration built fresh on Vercel's Chat SDK, with only the Workspace Overlay installed. The plan covers who can ask it, an access rule that keeps answers within what the requester and the channel may see, how it chooses between itself, workspace agents and the requester's personal agent, Slack-ready formatting, and how Slack threads appear as Overlay threads in the web app. Settings → Connected apps lists GitHub, Slack and MCP, and each opens its own settings page. Planning only; no behavior changes.
- Let agents record a cloud computer's screen as proof of work. The new `computer_recording` tool starts a recording, lets the agent mark each step or check as setup, step, pass or fail while it works, and on stop saves an MP4 to Files with one chapter per mark and a summary such as "3 checks: 2 passed, 1 failed", which the reply can embed. Recordings are private by default (personal Files, or workspace Files when asked or in a shared room). A public share link is made only when the agent asks for one on stop; that needs the person's approval in Ask mode and is refused when the workspace has public links turned off. An agent given only this tool can save its recordings without also being given `computer_publish_file`. Recording runs on cloud computers only. Marks made after a recording hit its time or size limit are refused instead of silently dropped, stopping twice returns the same video without touching other processes, and each computer keeps only its five newest recordings and deletes the raw capture once the MP4 is written. An agent granted `computer_recording` but not `computer_publish_file` can still save its recordings; the backend's publication check accepts either grant, so production Convex must be synchronized after this merges. A saved recording shows as a file card under the reply, and two stops of one recording at the same time wait for each other instead of both rewriting the video (driver version 15). Tests cover the approval gate, the publishing grant and the driver's chapters, limits and cleanup.
- Split the AI SDK failure description into small helpers so it passes the web complexity gate again. The previous change that classified network timeouts as provider timeouts pushed `describeAISDKFailure` over the complexity limit, which failed the lint check on every open pull request. Behaviour is unchanged.
- Stop cutting off long replies from bring-your-own-key models after five minutes. A connection to an OpenAI-compatible provider was closed after 300 seconds with no data, and a reasoning model such as Claude Fable 5.2 can be silent that long while it thinks or writes a large tool call. The agent re-sent the same request, hit the same limit again, and showed "The model connection dropped… Send your message again", which could never succeed. The silence limit is now 780 seconds, just under the 800-second workflow step limit. A request that still times out is now reported as a provider timeout instead of a dropped connection, because the classifier now reads the network timeout the AI SDK keeps as the error's cause.
- Explain why an agent can't use your Mac, and label the agent's Computer panel as the cloud computer. When an agent tried your own computer and it wasn't available, it only heard "not connected", so it couldn't say why, while the Computer panel beside the thread looked like the computer it meant. The tool now tells the agent whether your Mac is offline (and when it last checked in), connected with computer control off, or never set up, and to point you to Settings → Desktop → This computer and its "Let agents run threads on this computer" switch, which also keeps it on after the desktop app restarts or signs out. When an agent names no computer and there is no cloud Computer, it now hears your Mac's reason instead of a generic cloud error. The panel tab now reads "Cloud computer", and its captions say the agent is working in the cloud. Tests cover each reason.
- Let the mobile app accept workspace invitations, edit your name and photo, create workspaces and request billing. These account routes only accepted browser sessions, so the app's sign-in was refused with "This endpoint requires a signed-in browser session" and Account settings showed your email as your name. They now accept a browser session or the app's verified sign-in token; API keys and service calls are still refused. When accepting an invitation from the app, Overlay reads your verified email from your sign-in provider, since the app's token does not carry it. Account deletion still requires the browser.
- Add OpenCode to the Subscription tab of Connect a provider. Pick the Go plan or Zen (pay as you go), paste an OpenCode API key, and every chat model on that plan is added to the model picker. It is saved as a personal API-key connection on OpenCode's locked endpoints, so it works even where the subscription sign-in service is not configured. New OpenCode Zen provider preset (`https://opencode.ai/zen/v1`) that checks the key with a request for a nonexistent model, so connecting never spends credits. OpenCode model discovery now keeps only chat models (Zen also lists embedding, image, realtime and routing models), and `muse-` models use the Responses API on both plans. OpenCode Go is shown as the subscription it is: its models sit in their own section at the top of the model picker with the OpenCode logo and plug badge and no "API key" tag, and its Providers row shows the logo and "Subscription · Personal". OpenCode Zen models also show the OpenCode logo but keep the API key tag, since Zen is pay as you go. Inference for both still uses the ordinary API-key path. Adds the OpenCode logo mark.

- Make every loading state look like the page that replaces it, so nothing jumps when data arrives. An audit found about 50 skeletons that sat in the wrong place or had the wrong shape; the finished plan is removed now that every phase has shipped. Loading screens now keep the real header, tabs, search and composer and draw placeholders only where data will appear, in the loaded view's own column and row sizes: Work's List, Board and Timeline load in the composer's column with the composer in place; Organization, the task and assignment panels, chats, DMs, channels, agent threads, Activity, Archived, the agent editor, Extensions, Settings, workspace settings, Notes, Files and automations each have one matching skeleton instead of a sequence of different ones (several showed a chat or task list first, or a blank page). Sidebar rows (Agents, threads, Chats, Work, Files, Automations, the workspace switcher) load at their real height, "No channels yet" and Work's empty labels no longer flash first, and automations no longer swap icons after loading. Marketing pages load inside the real nav, sign-in inside the real split page, shared chats in their real column, and `/` shows the boot loader it resolves behind. Shared fixes: the shimmer no longer forces 64px placeholder blocks or square corners on round placeholders; routes without their own skeleton show a header and an empty body instead of the Work list. Removed unused skeletons for Projects, Knowledge, Outputs and Memories routes and the unused `Tile`/`ListRow` primitives and `SidebarMenuSkeleton`. The interface-design doc records the rules. Checked on the PR preview by holding page and data loads in light, dark and phone widths; marketing, sign-in and share loading screens were checked in code only.
- Keep the `/docs` prefix in the canonical URLs that Overlay documentation publishes for crawlers.
- Add an Overlay comparison hub with Devin, OpenHands and Lindy comparisons, a Devin alternatives roundup, and a guide to running AI employees. Link the pages from the marketing footer, publish canonical comparison, documentation and REST API reference URLs in the sitemap, and expand the structured data and LLM text guides so search and answer engines can discover them.

- Keep Work inside the composer's width. The List, Board, Timeline and Organization views now sit in the same centered column as the task composer, like a chat transcript, instead of running edge to edge, and List and Timeline rows lose their divider lines (nowhere else in the app uses them). The approved mockup is `internal/plans/mockups/work-list-width.html`.
- Keep the app opening when a workspace's billing wallet is not set up yet. `GET /api/v1/bootstrap` failed with a server error in that case, which left the mobile app stuck on "Unable to open your workspace"; it now loads with no entitlements, as the subscription and model-catalog routes already did.
- Let people who only use the mobile or desktop app reach their workspace. The apps read chats, Work, files and other workspace data straight from Convex, which checks the user's saved roles, but only the web app's page shell ever saved the default member role. A person who signed up on their phone and never opened the web app was therefore denied everything (Work showed "not available in this workspace"). Native sign-in and session refresh now save that role too, the same way the web shell does, and never fail sign-in if saving it does. Tests cover the helper.
- Finish moving to `overlay.works`. The operator console now lives at `admin.overlay.works` (`admin.getoverlay.io` redirects there), the account-deletion error and the help FAQ point people to `support@overlay.works`, and agents' git commits use `agent@overlay.works`. Preview automation now removes a PR's old `pr-<n>.preview.getoverlay.io` address once its new `preview.overlay.works` preview is ready or the PR closes, so the old preview wildcard can be retired once open PRs have moved; the preview HTTPS probe no longer checks for the retired staging host. Tests cover the old-address removal.
- Point the desktop submodule at Overlay Desktop 0.4.0, the release that ships private threads (Agent Host 0.6.0, now on npm) and connects to `overlay.works`. The private-threads plan now records Phases 1 and 2 as merged, published and released; they have not yet been checked in the released app.
- Move PR previews to `pr-<number>.preview.overlay.works`. Preview automation, the Vercel build guard, preview sign-in and callback checks, the preview billing plan and the QA scripts now read one preview domain constant per runtime (`src/shared/web/preview-origin.ts` for the app and Convex, `scripts/release/preview-domain.mjs` for release and QA scripts) instead of repeating `preview.getoverlay.io` patterns. `*.preview.overlay.works` is registered on the `codex/preview-unassigned` branch for certificates only, and the preview GitHub App and WorkOS sandbox accept the new callback hosts. PRs built before this change keep serving on their old `preview.getoverlay.io` address; because a preview build runs the PR's own build guard, an open PR must merge this main before its next preview build, which then moves it to the new address. The old wildcard is removed once no open PR uses it. Tests and living docs use the new host.
- Switch production to `overlay.works`. Production's app URL, backend callback addresses and email senders now use the new domain (emails come from `support@overlay.works`, the welcome email from `welcome@overlay.works`, and replies reach a real inbox), and the release checks verify `overlay.works` as the production host. `getoverlay.io` permanently redirects to it. The generated Convex API types also regain the `surfaces/surfaceExecutions` module that production runs.
- Let the welcome email come from its own sender address. Set `OVERLAY_WELCOME_EMAIL_FROM` (for example `Overlay <welcome@overlay.works>`) and new users' welcome email uses it; every other email still uses `OVERLAY_EMAIL_FROM`. This replaces the unfriendly no-reply sender for the first email people get.
- Make `overlay.works` Overlay's main address in code, docs and published files. Links in emails, share cards, search metadata, the sitemap, the API description, Agent Host instructions, the install script and the docs now point to `overlay.works`; `docs.overlay.works` and `docs.getoverlay.io` both send readers to the docs; and file uploads accept the new address. Live sign-in and callback addresses still come from each deployment's app URL, so production keeps working on `getoverlay.io` until its app URL is switched. The regenerated API description also picks up two request fields earlier changes added.
- Let desktop threads run on this computer without syncing to the cloud. A thread set to This computer now has a Cloud sync choice under the message box before its first message: Not synced runs the agent's loop on the computer, stores the transcript only there, and calls the agent's model with the person's own Anthropic, OpenAI, Google or OpenRouter key. Keys are added in Settings → Desktop → Model keys, checked with the provider and kept in the system keychain. These threads list under their agent with a crossed-out cloud, never appear in a browser or on another device, and show tool rows, approvals for commands, and Stop like synced threads. The agent loop is a new `overlay-agent-host private` command in Agent Host 0.6.0 (AI SDK `ToolLoopAgent`, transcript in a local SQLite file), and the desktop bridge gains a `localThreads` capability. Overlay tools (memory, files, web search), Sync to cloud, local subscriptions and the free tier are not built yet (`internal/plans/LOCAL_PRIVATE_THREADS_PLAN.md`). Nothing shows until Agent Host 0.6.0 is published and a desktop release with the new bridge ships. The Phase 5 navigation test now expects the rail order main already ships (Agents, Chats, Work), which had been failing every pull request's full suite.
- Plan private local threads and draft their design. `internal/plans/LOCAL_PRIVATE_THREADS_PLAN.md` records the owner's decisions for desktop threads that are not synced to the cloud: their agent loop runs on the computer in the Agent Host on the AI SDK's `ToolLoopAgent`, their transcript is stored only there, they keep the agent's Overlay tools, and they can use the person's own API keys, subscriptions through a local CLIProxyAPI, or Overlay-billed models. A new free tier and its allowances are still to be set, and it conflicts with the paid-first plan until that plan is amended. The mockup `internal/plans/mockups/private-threads.html` (feature `private-threads`) adds a Cloud sync choice beside This computer and the folder, a Not synced label and thread-list mark, and a one-way Sync to cloud action.
- Keep generated videos visible and give file links the same choice as web links. A file an agent created (a video, an image, a document) stayed as a card only until the reply linked to it in text; once the finished reply said "Video: [film.mp4](…)", the card disappeared. Now only embedding the file hides its card, so videos stay in the reply like notes do. Clicking a link to an Overlay file in a reply now offers Open in sidebar or Open in Files (following the same link setting as web links) instead of always leaving the conversation for Files. File and note previews in the agent panel lose the rule under their header, and their expand icon becomes an open-in-Files icon beside Download (the old footer is gone). Videos in the panel, the Files page and chat cards play on the light or dark theme surface instead of black.
- Keep links and computer control inside an agent's right panel. A link opened in Overlay from an agent thread now opens as a tab of the thread's right panel (site name, reload and open in new tab above the page) instead of a second right sidebar; opening it again switches to its tab.
