Taking control of a computer no longer covers the conversation with a full-page stream: the Computer tab's stream turns interactive in place with Release control right below it, and the panel widens on its own, returning to its earlier width on release. The working orb's "Thinking" line now lines up with the agent's name, and the default sidebar order is Agents, Chats, Work (members' own order is kept), so `/app` opens on Agents by default. The link embeddability check moved to `src/shared/web/link-preview.ts` so chats and agents share it. Mockup: `internal/plans/mockups/agent-panel-links-and-control.html`.
- Fix every computer's screen and browser tools. Since the change that shrank screenshots (driver version 11), the server expected version 11 while the driver it installed still reported version 10 (the number was written twice and only one copy was changed), so every screenshot, click, browser and open-URL call failed. Agents saw "Python 3 is required to run the desktop driver" and later "the check returned unexpected output". The driver now takes its version from the same constant the server checks, a test runs the real driver and compares the two, and each computer replaces its stale driver on its next action without a restart.
- Show top-ups in the Billing tab's credit bar. The bar measured only the monthly allowance, so it stayed full after a top-up even though agents could keep working. It now shows how much of the allowance and this period's top-ups together is used, with a small mark where the monthly allowance ends, and the label reads "% used" instead of "% allowance used". The summary now includes the allowance's share (`credits.allowance`).
- Let agents spend top-up credit after the monthly allowance runs out. Once a workspace used up its monthly credit and started spending a top-up, the balance check counted the top-up spending twice and read the remaining top-up as $0, so every agent and computer action was refused with "Your workspace has less than 2% of its budget left" while the Billing tab still showed top-up credits left. Found on a workspace with $2.71 of an $8 top-up unspent. The remaining budget is now the unused allowance plus the top-up balance; the stop line is still 2% of the period's total.
- Stop "Change plan" from starting a second subscription. On a workspace that already had a plan, the button opened a new Stripe subscription checkout: Stripe charged for it and would keep charging every month, but Overlay kept the existing plan and never applied the new one. Change plan now opens the billing portal, the plan-amount field shows only before a workspace subscribes, and the server refuses a new subscription checkout while a plan is active.
- Say why a computer's desktop driver did not start. Any failure of the driver's start-up check (a command that could not run, a crash, a timeout or unexpected output) was reported as "Python 3 is required to run the desktop driver", even on machines where Python works. Found when an agent's screenshots and browser actions on a Box computer all failed with that message while its own commands showed Python 3.12 installed, so it reinstalled packages and suggested contacting support. The error now names the real cause (for example "the check exited with code 1: PermissionError…" or "the check timed out") and carries the check's exit code and last 1,000 characters of output; the server logs the stage and exit code. "Python 3 is required" is now reported only when `python3` itself is not found. A failed driver upload also keeps its error.
- Make Claude Fable 5.2 work on a connected Claude subscription. It appeared in the model list but every request failed with "The model provider could not complete the request", because the subscription bridge ran upstream code from before Fable 5.2 existed while its model list updates on its own. The bridge now embeds the upstream revision that added Fable 5.2's request rules (CLIProxyAPI `6db7d4b8`, 110 commits newer). Overlay's Codex token-refresh patch was rewritten for upstream's changed refresh code and still reports the token endpoint's HTTP status. The bridge build and its tests pass; sign-in, token refresh and replies for each subscription provider have not been re-checked with real accounts on the new revision.
- Stop a screenshot the model connection refuses from ending an agent's run. When a model request was rejected as invalid or too large right after a tool returned an image, the run stopped with "The model provider refused this request because it could not accept part of it". Found when the Video Agent, on a connected Claude subscription, took its first computer screenshot 14 minutes into a task. The request is now sent once more with tool-returned images replaced by a note, so the agent carries on from text and can say what it could not see; images you attach yourself are never removed. Computer screenshots are also captured at 8 bits per channel, less than half the size of the previous 16-bit captures (driver version 11). The subscription bridge's real HTTP status and error code are now logged, because the generic 400 Overlay reported hid why this request was refused.
- Keep the scope you are in when you open Chats. Clicking Chats in the sidebar always opened a new Personal chat, even coming from the Workspace scope of Work, Agents or Files, so you had to switch back to Workspace every time. Chats now opens a new draft in the scope already on screen: Personal stays Personal, and Workspace opens a new workspace chat.
- Stop a failed tool call from ending an agent's whole response. Any tool that threw (a web page that could not be fetched, a dropped connection to a provider, an unreachable agent) used to stop the run with a red "A tool operation ended without a confirmed outcome" and a diagnostic ID, even for a lookup that changes nothing. Found when one `web_fetch` connection failure ended a Product Agent reply mid-task. The failure is now that call's result: the agent reads what failed (secrets removed, 300 characters at most) and retries, takes another route or tells you what went wrong, and other tool calls in the same step still run. The tool row still shows the error. `web_fetch` also retries a dropped connection or a busy provider (429, 502, 503, 504) once before failing; a timeout is not retried. `TOOL_OUTCOME_UNKNOWN` is no longer produced and stays only for earlier runs. This replaces the per-tool fixes for `ask_agent` and `post_message` refusals with one rule for every tool.
- Stop a mistyped room from failing an agent's whole turn. When an Overlay agent posted with `post_message` to a room it named instead of identifying (for example `general` instead of the channel's id), or hit another refusal it could fix (not in the room, too many mentions, an empty or too-long message, or the post limit), the run stopped with "A tool operation ended without a confirmed outcome", although nothing had been posted. Found when a new workspace's chief tried to show two agents replying in #general. These refusals are now the tool's result too, and the not-found message and the tool's `conversationId` description tell the agent to pass the id from `list_chats`. Caller-identity failures and refusals after a message is posted still fail as before.
- Simplify the agent sidebars. The right panel of an agent thread opens on the agent's avatar and name with one plain list below: Settings first, then Computer (now an ordinary row that still shows whether the computer is running or asleep), Assignments, Progress, Changes and Files. The search box and the shortcut hints are gone, and "+" opens that same view as a new tab instead of a searchable list. The line under the panel's header is gone. In the agent list each agent shows at most five threads, then "Load more", so a busy agent no longer pushes the others off screen; the thread you have open always stays listed. A lone pinned chief (the Personal tab) is now a centred tile instead of spanning the whole width, an open chief's threads start a little below its tile, and "Load more" lines up with the thread titles (mockup: `internal/plans/mockups/agent-sidebar-panels.html`).
- Stop a refused question from failing an agent's whole turn. When an Overlay agent asked an agent it was not allowed to ask (one already in the same chain of questions, itself, an unknown name, or past a limit), the refusal crashed the asking agent's run, so the person got a failed reply with no explanation. Found when Claude Code, joined over MCP, asked the workspace Overlay to ask it back. The refusal is now the tool's result, so the agent reads why and answers with what it has. Faults such as an unreachable agent still fail as before.
- Let any agent that speaks MCP join a workspace as an agent of its own. ChatGPT, Claude, Claude Code, Codex and others already connect to `/api/mcp`, but acted only as the person who connected them: no name, no place in channels, and no way to be reached. A connection can now call `join_workspace`; nothing changes until a workspace owner or admin approves it in Settings → Connected apps, and until then the connection can only check its request. Once approved it is an agent in the workspace (a new `mcp` agent kind) that reads and posts in rooms under its own name (`list_rooms`, `read_room`, `post_message`), asks other agents and is asked by them (`list_agents`, `ask_agent`, `read_agent_reply`), and collects what is sent to it from an inbox (`check_inbox`, `wait_for_messages`, `reply`). MCP cannot start an agent's turn, so a mention, direct message or question waits in the inbox: a connected agent sees it on its next tool call (every result says when messages are unread) or at once while it waits on `wait_for_messages`; an agent that is away gets it when it next connects, the asker is told so instead of waiting, and the owner is notified at most once an hour. The Chief and other Overlay agents can ask an outside agent and mention it in rooms. Loops are bounded: a question asked right after another agent's message continues that chain and its 10-hop limit, and an outside agent's questions and posts are counted per hour and per day. What an outside agent writes is marked for Overlay agents as information, not instructions, and what it reads from others is framed the same way. The owner or an admin can pause or remove an agent, disconnecting the app removes it, and `OVERLAY_MCP_AGENT_NETWORK_DISABLED` turns the whole feature off. Adds the `mcpAgentJoins` and `mcpAgentInbox` Convex tables and `GET, POST /api/v1/mcp/agents`. Waking an agent that is not connected (Agent Host for Claude Code and Codex, MCP Events for ChatGPT) is not built yet.
- Fix asking an agent and waiting for the answer from an outside AI app. Reading the reply sent the backend one field it does not accept, so `ask_agent` over MCP failed with a server error instead of returning the agent's reply. An Overlay agent's run also accepts a question written by an outside agent, opened for the person that agent acts for.
- Show what a working agent is doing. A live response used to end with the Overlay mark looking around; now a small dot-lattice orb (adapted from the MIT-licensed AICSS orbs) sits on the row of the tool that is running, with a motion for the kind of step: asking another agent, searching or reading, or using a tool. A running agent tool says who is asked and what, for example "Asking Quill: What did you most recently do?". Between tools, one line under the output shows the orb with "Thinking" or "Writing the reply" and how many seconds it has run. The orb is never shown twice and disappears when the response settles. Date lines in chats and agent threads are now the date alone, without rules. The pinned chiefs (the workspace Overlay and your own chief) sit side by side as tiles at the top of the agent list, like pinned conversations in iMessage, instead of as separate blocks; a tile has no background until it is open, one opens at a time, and its threads show below the tiles. Every block of a response is now the same distance apart: the assignment row moved closer to the text and further from the action buttons.
- Turn an agent thread's right panel into tabs. Progress, Computer, Changes, Files and the agent's settings each open as a tab, and files opened from the thread open as their own tabs instead of replacing the panel. With no tab open, the panel shows the agent's home: its avatar, name, role and description above the list of panels, and opening one replaces the home with the tabs; closing the last tab brings the home back. The panel starts on the home the first time. Progress shows the run's plan and every step the agent took, Terminal shows the commands it ran on its computer with their output (secrets in commands are hidden), and Changes shows the thread's pull requests and the files it changed with line counts; there is no line-by-line diff yet. The panel opens at 480px, can be dragged wider or expanded over most of the thread, and remembers its tabs and width; earlier Details and Library choices carry over as the agent and Files tabs. The pinned Overlay agent now sits above the other agents in Personal and Workspace as a tile with its avatar and name; clicking it shows or hides its threads, as other agent rows do. "+" (⌥T) opens the same list with search and the computer's status. Shortcuts use Option only (⌥T for a new tab, ⌥F for Files), so they never take over Print or another browser or text-editing command, and they do nothing while a dialog is open. There is no Terminal tab: commands the agent ran stay as rows in the conversation and in Progress. Agent threads keep **People** in the ⋯ menu.
- Line up the first icon in the primary sidebar (Work by default) with the Personal/Workspace switch beside it. The icons sat 11px lower, so the switch looked out of place next to the logo and panel title, which already line up. The icons now start right under the logo.
- Show each Chief assignment inside the response that made it. The assignment box used to sit once under the whole conversation; each agent response now ends with one collapsed row for the assignments it made, built like the tool-call and "Worked for" rows: the assignment icon and a summary such as "Assigned 3 tasks · 2 completed · 1 working". Opening it lists one line per assignment with its state, agent and task, each linking to its thread; a failed one turns red and shows its reason (for example that its run ended before it started) underneath. Assignments whose response can no longer be traced, and the "Assigned from" link in an assignment thread, stay below the conversation. The assignments query now returns, for each assignment, the response that made it, when it was made and why it failed; it finds the response through the run that made the assignment. Agent threads get an **Assignments** panel listing every assignment grouped by response, each group with "Show in conversation". Chats and channels replace the People button with one sidebar button that opens on Assignments, with People and Pinned as tabs beside it, each with its count. Agent threads keep their own panel button. The collapsed row starts with the avatars of the agents the work went to instead of a branch icon, and each line starts with its agent's avatar (mockup: `internal/plans/mockups/assignment-avatars.html`).
- Bring `package-lock.json` back in line with `npm install --package-lock-only`. Main's lockfile still marked 27 packages `"dev": true` that npm no longer marks, so the lockfile check in Lint and boundary checks failed on every pull request. Installed packages do not change.
- Let an agent on a subscription model ask an agent on a gateway model, and the reverse. An asked agent inherited the asking run's way of paying, so when the two used different kinds of model the asked agent stopped with "The selected model can no longer run this task" before making a call. It now pays according to its own model. Changing an agent's model while it is running now reports a model problem instead of "no longer has the authorization it needs".
- Stop offering `resolve_assignment` to connected agents and outside AI apps. Resolving a Chief assignment only works inside the Chief's own native Harness run, so everywhere else the tool could only refuse; it is now withheld from the MCP servers like the computer tools.
- Keep agent runs that were already running during the Slack removal deploy from failing. Those runs still use the previous deployment's code, which asks Convex whether the run is a Slack turn on every step; a temporary `surfaces/surfaceExecutions:getSurfaceTurnExecutionByHarnessRunId` now always answers no (null), the answer every non-Slack run already got. It is removed once those runs have finished.
- Regenerate `convex/_generated/api.d.ts` so its types list the assignment, turn-queue and automation-run modules that production Convex now serves. Types only; runtime behavior is unchanged.
- Fix the connector sign-in return. After connecting an app from Connectors or onboarding, the provider sent the second tab back to an Overlay page that booted the whole app and could sit loading forever, while the original tab only caught up after a refocus. That callback is now a tiny static page with no app shell: it tells the original tab the connection changed and closes itself, or says the tab can be closed when the browser refuses. The original tab now updates in place: while a sign-in is pending it re-checks on cross-tab messages, when the tab becomes visible again and every few seconds for up to two minutes, then shows the connector as connected with a toast. Onboarding now hears the same cross-tab signal, and Connectors, onboarding and chat connector cards share one subscription helper. When a sign-in fails, the callback keeps showing the escaped error and sends a failure signal scoped to that sign-in attempt; only its matching original tab stops waiting and shows a generic failure (a toast on Connectors, an inline error in onboarding). A failure from an older or unrelated attempt no longer cancels the current one. Mocked callback and subscriber tests execute the emitted script and cover attempt isolation, concurrent subscriptions, and safe storage fallbacks. The local integration guide documents the callback and return behavior.
- Open chats, agent threads and note conversations you visited recently without waiting. The app now remembers the last 32 transcripts you opened in this tab (up to the newest 100 messages each, about 8 MiB in total) and shows them immediately, at the place you were reading, while the live data loads; the live data then replaces the remembered copy without duplicates or a jump. Hovering or focusing a chat or agent thread in the sidebar for 120 ms reads its first page in advance (two at a time, read-only, with nothing left subscribed), so the first open is fast too. The memory is cleared when you sign out or switch account or workspace, a conversation that turns out deleted or no longer accessible is forgotten and shown as unavailable, and a remembered copy never permits an action: sending, loading older messages and every other action still wait for the server. Adds tests for the cache bounds, identity clearing, late results, eviction, restore and write-back, and for the prefetch delay, concurrency and read-only queries. A remembered room or channel you can no longer read (you were removed, or it was deleted) clears its remembered messages as soon as that is known, and a hover prefetch that times out closes its read.
- Show the note assistant's replies the way chat shows them. The assistant panel beside a note used to receive each reply through the send request and show text only once a whole paragraph was finished, with its own message bubbles, tool rows and a separate "thinking" mark. It now sends with the same Convex delivery as chat, so text appears as it is written, and the panel draws the note's conversation with chat's own reply renderer: the same tool rows, the single 24px Overlay mark while a reply is in progress, the interrupted notice after Stop and the failure notice when a run fails. The panel now also shows the note's earlier conversation after a reload or when the note is reopened, instead of starting empty each time, and it keeps the newest reply in view until you scroll up. The editor still becomes read-only while the assistant works, edit review still follows each run, and Stop or leaving the note still stops the run. Chat and the note panel read conversations through one shared hook, and a new `chat/personal:getByClientId` query finds a note's chat without creating one. Removes the old stream translation and the unused "thinking" item, and adds tests for the shared read, sending and settling a note turn (completion, failure, refusal, Stop), and the panel rendering the chat body and mark. A note looks up its assistant chat only while the assistant panel is open, and someone who can read notes but not chats still gets a working note instead of an error. A request the server refuses before saving it puts the prompt back in the assistant's composer.
- Draw agent-thread and room replies with the same renderer and loading mark as chat. Chat exchanges and agent replies now share one response body (`AssistantVisualBlocks`, which `ChatExchange` used to duplicate) and one rule for the 24px Overlay mark (`responseLoadingPresentation`): the mark shows below the author as soon as a reply is pending, stays through text and tool-only periods, and goes away when the reply settles or fails. Agent replies no longer show a separate three-dot row before their first words. Avatars, author rows, reactions, threads and approval cards stay as they were. The mark stays hidden while an agent waits for your approval or answer, as before.
- Show live replies the same way in every thread, read straight from Convex. Personal chat used to receive its live text through the send request, which the server filled by checking Convex every 150 ms and relaying what it found; the chat now reads the reply directly from Convex like agent threads, DMs, channels and automation threads already did, so text no longer waits for that extra hop and no server function stays open for the length of the reply. The send request ends once the run is admitted (it still returns the run identity and source citations), and the turn finishes when Convex reports the run done, so the reply notice, first-chat activation and errors behave as before. Every thread now uses one hook that asks Convex only for text it does not have yet: each update sends back the newest, still-growing 8 KiB piece instead of the whole reply, so long replies stay cheap for every viewer. Saved reply steps also record whether they hit the output limit, so live reads stop re-parsing earlier steps. Act retry and API callers keep the streamed response. Adds tests for incremental reads (only new text, retry and generation filtering, access denial), the hand-off from live to saved text, and turn completion. The read cursor no longer carries the stream sequence, so it only changes when a new segment starts: a growing reply is one live subscription instead of a new query (and an empty re-run) on every update. Chats and rooms wait for their settings before reading, so one opened mid-reply no longer goes blank for a moment.
- Remove the Slack bot and external history import so Overlay can focus on the core platform. Agents can no longer be connected to Slack channels (the agent settings "Reachable on" section, the Slack app install and callback, the Slack events webhook, channel bindings and the Slack reply path in agent runs are gone), and the workspace settings **Import** tab with its Slack history backfill, worker route and cron is gone. Chat-platform bots will return later on Vercel's Chat SDK. Slack is still available to agents as a Composio connector. Stored data is untouched: the surface and import tables stay in the Convex schema (marked retired) so existing rows remain valid, and conversations that were mirrored from Slack still open as read-only history. The unused Chat SDK packages (`chat`, `@chat-adapter/slack`, `@chat-adapter/state-memory`) and the Slack environment variables in `.env.example` are removed, and the route catalogs, Convex workflow and integration docs no longer describe the removed features. The site description and `llms.txt` no longer say agents can be reached in Slack, Telegram or iMessage.
- Keep Chief assignments from slowing or blocking ordinary conversations. Only assignment threads now take turns one at a time (Chief and default-agent DMs start turns directly, as before); the queue reads at most the 50 most recent runs; a queued turn is checked again every 30 seconds instead of backing off for up to 15 minutes; assignment recovery reads a bounded number of rows, pushes launches waiting on their parent back 30 seconds, and can no longer stop ordinary harness continuations; runs from before shared-room tracking decide from their source room instead of losing private tools; the assignment list, status and result reads are bounded; and Work keeps loading past pages that hold only assignments the viewer cannot see.
- Add a design mockup, `internal/plans/mockups/chief-assignments.html`, for how Chief assignments should appear before any of it is built: the assignment box inside the response that made it and as wide as its text, one tabbed right sidebar for agent threads, chats and channels (one button opens it, "+" opens a new tab that lists the views that conversation has, Assignments is one of them, and an agent's sidebar opens on its avatar and that list until a view is opened), and a delegated agent that cannot run shown as one failed assignment with its reason instead of stopping the Chief with an internal error. A switch compares it with the app today. No product behaviour changes.
- Refuse to save an agent with a model Overlay does not offer. An agent, or a Chief creating one for you, could be saved with a mistyped or invented model name and then failed its first task with “This task no longer has the authorization it needs.” Creating an agent or changing its model now checks the model and says what is wrong; the Chief’s create-agent tool uses the default model unless you name one. A task whose model is withdrawn later now says “The selected model can no longer run this task” and to choose another model, instead of pointing at workspace membership.
- Check the agent chosen for a new automation. It must be a live agent in the same workspace that its creator can see (and that the whole workspace can see, for a workspace automation), the same rule as opening a thread with that agent; otherwise the request answers 404 "Agent not found". Before this, a member could set another member's private agent and run it on a schedule. A quick double press of Enter in the new-automation composer also no longer creates the automation twice. Sending a new automation with attachments or @mentions now asks you to remove them instead of silently dropping them, since instructions are saved as plain text. An agent automation now shows that it runs on the agent's own model rather than offering a model choice it would ignore. A daily time skipped when clocks spring forward (such as 2:30 AM) runs at the same minute an hour later instead of an hour early.
- Rebuild Automations as threads. The Automations page is now the chat view: you type what should happen, pick when (every hour, day, weekday, week or month, at a time in your own time zone) and optionally an agent next to the model picker, and send. That creates the automation, runs it once right away and opens its thread, and later runs arrive there as messages. A right-hand details panel (like the agent panel) holds the name, on/off switch and next run, Run now, schedule, time zone, model, recent runs with Retry, and Delete. The old Chat/Edit tabs, the automation editor form and the temporary-chat control are gone from Automations. Schedules now keep their wall-clock time in the automation's time zone, so a 9:00 AM run stays at 9:00 AM after a daylight-saving change (they were stored as a fixed UTC time and drifted an hour), and a new 'every weekday' schedule runs Monday to Friday. The agent's automation tools also take local times. An automation that runs as an agent opens in that agent's thread view with the same details panel, and the agent picker lists the agents of the current Personal or Workspace scope once each. A run that is waiting for your answer or approval no longer blocks the schedule: the next scheduled run still happens in the same thread, and the details panel shows the waiting run as "Waiting for you". Before, every later run was skipped until you answered, so the automation looked dead. The `automation-threads` mockup and `internal/plans/AUTOMATION_THREADS_PLAN.md` record the design and the diagnosis behind it.
- Simplify an agent's tool toggles from 16 to 12. Knowledge, Files and Notes become one **Files** group (search, read and edit files, folders and notes), Image generation and Video generation become **Media generation**, and Ask other agents joins **Agents**, which now shows the Agents icon instead of a wrench; Tasks and Automations use the Work and Automations icons. The new-agent dialog drops the Read only preset, since reading and editing files are now one toggle; outside apps keep their Read only access level. Agents that had all of the merged groups keep them. An agent that had only some of a merged group's old parts shows that toggle off and loses those tools when its settings are next saved. Outside apps at Everything still can ask agents and still cannot create or edit them.
- Give the desktop app its own signed-out home. Signing out in the desktop app used to land on the marketing website, and a signed-out window showed the app as a guest. The window now shows the Overlay wordmark and "Your AI Chief of Staff." from the loading screen with a **Sign in** button (and "Create an account"); both open the browser sign-in, the screen waits with "Open browser again" and "Cancel", and signing in returns to the workspace. Browsers are unchanged: web sign-out still goes to the website and signed-out workspace links still go to the sign-in page. The page is `/auth/desktop`; no desktop release is needed.
- Lead the landing page with "Enabling self-driving companies with AI employees." The hero headline no longer rotates between two lines. "Give your agents a workspace" (with "Create swarms of agents" below it) now heads the Create / Manage / Deploy section, and "The open source multiplayer cloud control plane for AI employees" heads the cloud, multiplayer and open source cards; both headings are centered and keep the colored, linked keywords. The page title and share titles use the new line. The "Any MCP app" chip no longer wraps out of its box, and those three cards no longer stretch past the screen on phones. The approved mockup is `internal/plans/mockups/landing-self-driving-companies.html`.
- Let agents self-verticalize through skills. Skills can now belong to a domain (a folder such as `3d-design`, with an optional domain map), carry trigger phrases, and record where their knowledge came from. Agents get `find_skills` (ranks workspace and built-in skills for a task and says when nothing fits), `load_skill` (reads a skill's outline or one section at a time), `search_skill_registries` (searches public skills on skills.sh and skillmd.com, results marked untrusted), and `save_skill`. Skills an agent saves are always personal; a person shares them with the workspace after review. Five built-in skills ship with every workspace: self-verticalize, frontier-research, skill-reuse, skill-authoring and skill-testing. Agents saved before this change keep their Skills tools and gain the new ones.

- Load less JavaScript before the chat page can be used. The chat entry bundle drops from 1,096 KiB to 689 KiB gzip (production analyzer build). The app shell no longer carries the Markdown, math and code renderer: the onboarding conversation, the chat transcript and direct messages now load as separate chunks, with the transcript's usual "Loading conversation" placeholder while they arrive and an idle-time preload so they are normally ready before a conversation opens. The scroll-to-latest control keeps tracking the viewport after the deferred transcript mounts. Product analytics (PostHog) loads after startup and only when analytics is configured, keeping events in order. The app shell loads its desktop bridge contract and schema library only inside the desktop app; route-specific settings code is outside that guarantee. Behavior is otherwise unchanged; the chat entry is still above its 680 KiB budget, which stays as it was.
- Keep typing responsive while a reply streams. In agent threads and other rooms, every streamed update used to re-render every message in the thread, because the live query returns fresh copies of unchanged messages and each row rebuilt its handlers; unchanged messages now keep their identity and rows skip re-rendering unless something they show changed (their content, status, edit state, reactions, reply count, highlight or turn actions). In personal chat and rooms the message box no longer re-renders on streamed updates; its buttons always call the current action, and it still updates when its own text, attachments, loading state, queue or context meter change. In personal chat, a finished reply no longer re-renders on every streamed update just because the reply in progress started after the page loaded. An agent thread's computer and folder settings now stay the same object between updates, so its footer and + menu don't force the message box to re-render. Adds render counters for the chat view, message list and room transcript, plus counters that name which input caused a message box or finished-reply re-render, documents how to measure typing in agent threads, and adds tests for both the skipped and the still-required re-renders. The message box still sends with the latest handler when only the handler changed (for example after picking an @mention in a draft that already had text), so mentions and the confirmation before mentioning a person are kept. An agent reply that just finished shows enabled retry and delete right away instead of keeping the locked state from while it ran.
- Turn on unified durable streaming for every workspace, including workspaces created after the earlier exact-ID enrollment. New personal chat, workspace-scope chat, workspace agent and automation replies stream through the persisted stream, so they flow smoothly, survive reloads and keep partial text on Stop, instead of new workspaces falling back to appending text to the message row in bursts. Setting `OVERLAY_UNIFIED_STREAMING=off` on the web deployment (then redeploying) stops new runs from using it; runs already started keep their path. The old `OVERLAY_UNIFIED_STREAMING_WORKSPACE_IDS` list is no longer read and can be deleted. Tests now cover default-on enrollment, the kill switch and immutability for admitted runs.
- Make common actions respond on click instead of after the server answers. Switching workspaces navigates at once (each workspace address already names its workspace, so saving it as the default happens in the background) and no longer hides the page behind a loading state while a request finishes. Assigning a task shows the new assignee immediately and reverts with an error if the server refuses. The task composer clears on submit so the next task can be typed right away; if creation fails, the task and all its settings remain in a retryable item below without replacing current work. Creating an agent closes the dialog right away, opens the agent as soon as it exists, and provisions its computer afterwards instead of making the dialog wait for it; a refusal reopens the dialog with the draft intact.
- Make streamed replies flow smoothly. Agent replies arrive from the server in bursts of up to 200 characters every quarter second, and chat replies in smaller bursts, which made text jump. The transcript reveals each burst a little at a time on word boundaries and shows the complete final text as soon as streaming ends, catching up at once after a background tab. Group rooms keep following the newest text as it reveals. The 24px chat mark stays visible for the full active response and disappears when it settles. Agent-thread replies also show the 24px Overlay mark below the reply while it is still streaming, as chat replies already did, instead of only moving the avatar's eyes.

- Point the desktop submodule at Overlay Desktop 0.3.1, the release that runs threads on this computer without Node.js installed (the app carries the Agent Host and its own Node.js runtime). The website's download serves the newest release on its own; the desktop discovery plan records what was checked on the published build including a real thread run on this computer from the installed 0.3.1 app; a Mac with no Node.js at all and the automatic update from 0.3.0 are still untested.
- Let Stop and steer retry cancellation when a previous request failed, so a still-generating channel reply does not remain stuck behind a one-time stop attempt.
- Let Stop reconcile an authorized terminal agent run with its still-generating reply, preserving completed output and failed diagnostics while releasing the channel composer.
- Settle personal work replies using their initiating user's ownership instead of requiring room-agent authorship.
- Let each person arrange the primary sidebar. Hovering **More** shows a pin on every page that moves it into the sidebar, and **Customize** at the end of More opens a dialog to reorder pages and move them between the sidebar and More (drag, or the arrow keys on a row's handle). The sidebar always keeps at least one page. The arrangement is saved to the account (`sidebarPinnedPageIds` and `sidebarMorePageIds` in the UI settings), so it follows the person to every device, and both the double and the single sidebar use it. The first page in the sidebar is now the page the app opens to: `/app`, the sign-in redirect and the brand link go there, and to Work for anyone who never arranged their sidebar.
- Show who people are with their avatars. The Direct Messages list shows the other person's account photo or the agent's creature instead of a generic icon (two overlapping avatars for a group), the People panel of a room shows photos and creatures instead of initials only, and person tags in room messages carry the account photo. The live room list returns the first two other members of each direct message (`otherParticipantPrincipalIds`) for this.
- Restyle the tags in the message box. A tagged person or agent shows their avatar and name without the "@"; the sent message still carries `@name`. A pasted or typed web address becomes a link chip with a short label; the sent message carries the full address. Hovering a tag says who it is and what sending does ("Sam will be notified when you send", "Atlas will reply here when you send"), and hovering a link shows the address with Open and Copy link. A restored draft gets its link chips back, and its people and agent tags when the name matches exactly one member.
- Send settings saves to the server one at a time, in order. Several quick changes (for example moving a page a few places in Customize sidebar, or a drag that passes over several rows) used to be sent at once, and an older save could reach the server last and store a layout the person had already changed.
- Remove the `people-sidebar-composer` mockup, which the three changes above replace, and rebuild the canonical app mockup from the production components.
- Point the desktop submodule at the change that ships the Agent Host and its own Node.js runtime inside the desktop app, so threads on this computer will no longer need Node.js installed once the next desktop release is published. The desktop discovery plan records that this is merged but not yet released or tested in a signed build.
- Unblock the dependency security audit, which began failing every pull request when a high-severity slowdown flaw (GHSA-fc5q-39xc-vjpf) was published for every release of `picomatch`, a file-pattern library with no fixed version yet. The three flagged copies run only during the build (Sentry's webpack loader and the Workflow builder) on our own patterns, never on user input in production, so they get a narrow exception that expires on 2026-12-01 and fails the audit again then, forcing an upgrade once a fix ships.
- Stop killing a task that waits on another agent for more than a minute. While an agent waited for an answer (for example X Poster waiting on Video Agent), the once-a-minute check that the task may continue compared two records of its status that legitimately differ during that wait, decided the task had been taken over, and stopped it with "This task no longer has the authorization it needs". The check now accepts a waiting task; who owns the task is still verified. The same fault is fixed for chats that hand work to a sub-agent.
- Report faults in Overlay's own task bookkeeping as an Overlay internal error instead of "This task no longer has the authorization it needs. Check workspace membership and tool access". That message is now reserved for cases where access really changed, so a bug no longer looks like a permissions problem.
- Record in the desktop discovery plan that the landing page entry points and the one-click download page are merged, live and verified, and what was not tested.
- Keep subscription providers' model lists current without the refresh button. The lists for connected ChatGPT/Codex, Claude, Grok, Meta, Kimi and Devin accounts came from a catalog frozen into the subscription bridge when it was built, so a model released afterwards (Claude Fable 5.2, for example) never appeared, even after pressing refresh. The bridge now downloads the published catalogs when it starts and every three hours, falling back to the built-in copy if the download fails. Overlay re-reads each owned, active subscription's list when the app loads and the stored list is more than six hours old. Newly released models are turned on automatically; models you turned off stay off.
- Make the desktop app discoverable from the marketing site. The top navigation and footer link to the download page, Mac visitors get a "Download for Mac" button in the hero in place of "Star on GitHub", a new "Overlay for Mac" section explains what the app adds (threads on your own computer in folders you choose, dictation into any app, chat over any window), and the "Hire your first AI employee" card lists the Mac download beside the web app. Download buttons carry the Apple mark.
- Make the download one click. A "Download for Mac" button opens `/download?start=1`, which starts the download, shows "Your download has started" with the installer's file name, and lists the three install steps. Visitors who are not on a Mac are told the app is for Apple silicon Macs and offered the web app and a link to copy. `/api/latest-release` now also returns the installer's file name and size.
- Keep `/download` reachable on every deployment. It no longer redirects to the home page when `OVERLAY_DESKTOP_DOWNLOADS_ENABLED` is off; it points to the public source repository instead, so the new links work on self-hosted sites too.
- Add the desktop discovery plan and its mockup (`internal/plans/DESKTOP_DISCOVERY_AND_DOWNLOAD_PLAN.md`, `internal/plans/mockups/desktop-discovery.html`), built from the live marketing page.
- Turn on the desktop download page in production. Overlay Desktop 0.3.0 is published as a signed and notarized macOS build on GitHub Releases, so `/download` now offers it instead of redirecting to the home page (`OVERLAY_DESKTOP_DOWNLOADS_ENABLED=1` on Production). The desktop submodule moves to the 0.3.0 release commit, and the comments describing the switch no longer say desktop builds are not public.
- Unblock publishing the Agent Host. The publish workflow runs the local-computer tests before it publishes, and the billing tests among them read a usage-markup setting that only the main test runner supplied, so every publish since that setting was added stopped before reaching npm. The test script now carries the same placeholder value the runner uses. The Agent Host CI job also moves from the retired `macos-14` runner image, which GitHub cancelled before it started, to `macos-15`, so the host's macOS tests run again.
- Replace the thread's computer and folder pickers with **Local and Cloud**. A thread runs on **This computer** (in the desktop app) or in the **Cloud**, and works in one folder or none: a local thread in a folder on the computer or an Overlay Files folder, a cloud thread in an Overlay Files folder or a GitHub repository. Both are chosen before the first message and can't change after it (the server refuses, and the menus become plain labels). In the desktop app, choosing "This computer" or "Choose folder…" connects the computer with no code and no trip to Settings, and folders are picked in the system's folder dialog instead of typed paths. On the web nothing sits under the message box any more: new threads run in the cloud and the folder is chosen from the + menu ("Work in a folder"). A local thread can be read anywhere, but in a browser or on another computer a notice replaces the message box, because it continues only on its own computer. Settings → Desktop → This computer lists the computer's folders with add and remove. The app says "this computer" instead of "Mac" throughout. The "Add a folder" dialog is removed. Remote control, several computers, sync and choosing where local threads are stored are recorded in `todos.md`.
- Split the agent's Computer toolset in two. **Cloud computer** is the existing opt-in paid cloud machine, renamed. **Local computer** is new: it lets an agent use the person's own computer in threads they run on it, is on for new agents and the default agent, and costs nothing. Before, a thread set to "This computer" on an agent without the Computer toolset had no tool that could reach the computer. "This computer" is disabled in Runs on when the agent's Local computer toolset is off. Agents saved before this change don't have it recorded, so a one-time data update (`migrations/grantLocalComputer:run`, run once per Convex deployment after it ships) turns it on for every existing agent that can already edit files and leaves read-only agents alone. Neither toolset is exposed to outside apps or connected agents.
- Make a folder added to a computer usable at once. The Agent Host only learned its approved folders every 15 minutes; it now asks for the current folders when a path is refused and retries. Threads on a computer with no folder work in `~/Overlay Files`, where Overlay Files folders are also copied.
- Extend the desktop bridge contract with `localComputer.chooseFolder` and with the computer's name, environment and work folder in `localComputer.status`. The desktop app needs the matching release.
- Rebuild the `local-and-cloud` mockup from the production components and remove the `thread-folders` mockup it replaces.
- Open the sidebar Help menu (Docs, Submit a bug) only when you click Help. It used to pop open whenever the pointer passed over the button.
- Document that the production GitHub App needs both the `www` and bare-domain callback URLs. Production's app URL is `https://getoverlay.io`, which redirects to `www`, so GitHub sign-in returns to the bare domain; with only the `www` callback registered, connecting GitHub on production failed with "redirect_uri is not associated with this application".
- Add a **Make paid for testing** button to workspace Billing on PR previews only. It gives the workspace the same synthetic paid plan the preview seed uses ($5 of test allowance), so preview QA can run agents without a Stripe checkout or a card. The route returns 404 outside PR previews and the Convex mutation refuses on non-preview deployments, so it can't affect production billing.
- Fix connecting GitHub to agent threads, and make it testable on PR previews. Connecting used to rely on GitHub sending people back after installing the app, which only ever goes to production and never happened when the app was already installed, so the connection was not saved. Connecting is now a GitHub sign-in that returns to the deployment that started it and connects every Overlay Agents installation the person can access; installing is a separate "Install on GitHub" step. PR previews use their own GitHub App (`overlay-agents-preview`) with preview-only credentials, so previews can test the whole flow without holding production credentials. The browser HTTP baseline records the new install-page link, which only opens GitHub in a new tab.
- Show an agent reply's copy, retry, delete, reply and branch buttons only while you hover over or focus the reply, the same as in chats. They were showing permanently under every reply in agent threads.

- Regenerate the Convex API types from the code production now runs: they add the `lib/threadWork` module that the thread-folders change left out, and restore the generator's own ordering and quoting.

- Make agent threads behave like chats instead of channels. Agent replies now have the same action row as chat replies: copy, retry, delete, reply and branch. Reactions, thread replies and the channel toolbar no longer appear in agent threads, and your own messages show just a copy button. Delete removes the whole exchange (your message and the agent's reply) instead of leaving a "Message deleted" row. Retry resends the latest request so the agent answers it again. Branch opens a new thread with the same agent holding the conversation up to that reply. Channels and multi-person DMs are unchanged. Adds the `agents/threads:deleteTurn` and `agents/threads:branch` Convex mutations, with tests.

- Add a proposed design mockup, `internal/plans/mockups/local-cloud-continuity.html`, for Phases 2–4 of the local and cloud continuity plan, built from the real app shell. It shows a project-bound agent thread with where it runs, its folder and branch under the composer, the "Continue in cloud" and "Bring back to Mac" dialogs (snapshot, excluded secret files, context package, needed values and the returned diff), handoff dividers in the transcript, delegated cloud tasks with a Review step, and a Project row in the agent's Details. The mockup builder gains feature builds (`MOCKUP_FEATURE`) that place proposed components into production modules at bundle time without editing them. The canonical app mockup no longer uses the removed connected-agent capabilities or the retired Claude Code harness for Atlas.
- Add two scripts for Phase 0 of the files-on-every-computer plan. `scripts/qa/fuse-mount-check.ts` starts a short-lived Boat computer, mounts a stand-in WebDAV endpoint with rclone over FUSE and checks listing, search, saves, freshness, many small files, a crash and stop/resume. `scripts/qa/boat-stop-control.ts` measures how long a computer takes to stop with and without a mount. They showed the mount works on Boat, but only outside `/overlay` itself, because a mount there blocks the computer from stopping; the plan now mounts under `/tmp` and links `/overlay` to it.
- Fix three ways thread folders could save the wrong thing, found in review. A run now starts from an empty copy of its folder, so files left on the computer by an earlier run (for example ones since deleted in Files) are no longer saved back as new. When a Mac's folder listing comes back cut off, the save no longer treats the missing files as deleted; it saves edits and new files and deletes nothing. GitHub tokens are now kept per repository instead of per owner, so a thread with two repositories from the same owner can push to both. Tests cover each case.
- Let an agent thread choose where it works. Under the message box, a thread now picks the computer that runs it (the agent's cloud computer or one of your own Macs, never a teammate's) and a folder: an Overlay Files folder, a GitHub repository or an approved folder on your Mac. When a run starts, Overlay copies only that folder onto the computer (`~/workspace/<folder>` in the cloud); when it ends, finishes with an error or is stopped, new, changed and deleted files are saved back to Files automatically, with the agent shown as the editor. A file someone changed in the app meanwhile is never overwritten: the agent's version is saved beside it as a copy. A card on the agent's reply lists what went back and any pull requests opened. Dependency folders, build output, `.git` and `.env` files are never copied either way. Repositories are cloned with a short-lived token from Overlay's new GitHub App (installed from the folder picker and verified against your GitHub account), and computer tools now default to the thread's chosen Mac. You can also add a folder to your own Mac or copy a Mac folder into Files from the thread. The owner of a Mac, not only a workspace admin, can now change its approved folders. Documented in `internal/develop/thread-folders.md`; the GitHub App needs the settings listed in `.env.example`. Alongside it: the new routes are in the authorization and API boundary registries, `threadFolderCopies` has a tenancy decision, the tenancy doc drops the removed connected-agent tables, gitleaks skips the four generated mockup bundles (minified code it misreads as keys), the lockfile is regenerated with the pinned npm 11.11.0, and the Agent Host's POSIX shell tests are skipped on Windows, where local tools aren't supported yet. The browser-call and on-prem Convex baselines record the new calls, routes and prune cron, and drop the removed connected-agent calls. A Mac that slept past its 15-minute credential now reconnects on wake instead of needing to be connected again: the credential refresh route alone accepts an expired, unrevoked credential for 30 days, still behind the device-key signature and a single-use nonce. Connecting GitHub now shows a message when it finishes or fails.
- Add `internal/plans/FILES_ON_COMPUTERS_PLAN.md`, a proposed phased plan for the files-on-every-computer design. The first version copies only the folders chosen for a thread onto its computer and saves changes back through the file service, with several Macs as computers, folder pickers and a GitHub App. The `/overlay` mount and Google Drive folders are later items in `todos.md`. Nothing is approved or built yet.
- Add a proposed design for working in folders from Overlay Files, GitHub, Google Drive and your Macs on any computer: an architecture diagram, `internal/plans/mockups/files-on-computers-architecture.html`, and a mockup in the real app shell, `internal/plans/mockups/files-on-computers.html`. The diagram explains why cloud computers mount Overlay Files over a WebDAV endpoint that saves through the file service rather than mounting storage directly, and why code is copied instead. The mockup shows source folders in Files, a computer picker under the composer that lists the cloud computer and every Mac with the desktop app (a Mac folder pins its thread to that Mac, and offline Macs can't be picked), a folder picker, where each of an agent's changes went back to, an add-folder dialog that keeps a Mac folder local or copies it to Files, and a switch between the web and desktop app.
- Remove bring-your-own agents (Codex, Claude Code, Hermes, Eve and other harnesses run over ACP through the Agent Host), so Overlay runs only its own harness. Gone: the desktop "Agent host" settings and harness choices, the agent editor's "connect your own agent" flow, the Agents → Environments and agent accounts settings, the chat slash menu for remote agents, remote run controls in transcripts, agent bindings, remote runs and their commands, events, artifacts, run credentials and drive, the connected-agent MCP server (`/api/agent-mcp`), agent profiles and config import, provider accounts, and about twenty `/api/v1` routes. The Agent Host (now 0.5.0) keeps only enrollment, signed credentials, heartbeat, capabilities, approved folders and local tools; its ACP, acpx, Eve and fake adapters, drive sync, MCP relay, approval bridge and config export are deleted, and its protocol package drops the run, drive, MCP and profile schemas. The `connectedAgents`, `externalAgents` and `cloudAgents` capabilities and their feature flags are removed; connecting a Mac is gated by `features.computers`. Existing connected agents keep their name, threads and history and run on the native harness with the default model; editing one's model switches it to the Overlay harness. In Convex, the connected-agent functions, crons and 21 tables are deleted rather than retired. `migrations/purgeConnectedAgents` deletes their leftover rows and remote-run messages, and `scripts/db/purge-connected-agent-credentials.ts` deletes stored provider credentials from the vault first; neither has run yet. The remote-run fields on `conversationAgentRuns` and message parts stay until a follow-up deploy after the purge. Living docs for connected agents, agent profiles and Codex subscriptions are replaced by `internal/develop/local-computer.md` and `internal/develop/e2b-sandbox.md`.
- Let Overlay agents use your own Mac as a Computer. Every computer tool (shell commands, files, terminal sessions, screenshots, clicking and typing, and a browser) can now run on a connected Mac as well as on a cloud Computer, under the same approvals. Each tool takes an optional `target` (`local` or `cloud`); with none, a cloud Computer is used when one is bound and your Mac otherwise. The Agent Host gains a `--local-tools` mode (package 0.5.0) that serves these tools with no ACP adapter or third-party harness. Overlay sends each tool call over a new signed request/response channel (`local-tool-calls`) that delivers it at most once, and a new `agentLocalToolCalls` table holds calls for up to an hour. The agent loop, model calls and subscriptions stay in Overlay, and cloud gateway credentials are never sent to the Mac. Only the person who approved the Mac can use it, only inside the folders they approved, and never from an automation. Screen control uses built-in macOS tools and needs Overlay's Screen Recording and Accessibility permissions. The browser is a separate Overlay Chrome profile, not your everyday one. Settings → Desktop gains a "This Mac" section: one click connects the Mac, shows the macOS permissions, and can keep the Mac available whenever Overlay is open. The desktop app needs its matching `overlay-desktop` change, and Agent Host 0.5.0 must be published before the pinned connection commands work.
- Add a proposed plan, `internal/plans/LOCAL_CLOUD_CONTINUITY_PLAN.md`, for running Overlay agents on the user's Mac as well as in the cloud. The plan treats the Mac as another Computer whose tools run in the Agent Host, while the agent loop and subscriptions stay in Overlay. It also covers moving projects between the Mac and the cloud through explicit handoffs, without any ACP fallback.
- Redesign notes as one clean, editable Markdown page everywhere, based on the approved `internal/plans/mockups/note-renderer.html`. Notes on the Notes/Files page now sit in a centered reading column with real side margins (a header toggle switches to full width and is remembered on the device), with the title as the page's large first line and a quiet "Edited 2h ago · 1,240 words" line under it. Headings, lists, quotes, code and to-do checkboxes get Notion-like spacing, and tables are a quiet grid with readable columns that scroll sideways instead of squeezing into tall, narrow cells. Hovering any block shows a + and a drag grip in the margin, like Notion: + adds a block below through the block menu, dragging the grip moves the block, and clicking it offers Turn into, Duplicate, Copy as Markdown and Delete. Turning a quote into another block removes the quote from every line in it. The `/` block menu is rebuilt in the app's menu style with an icon, description and Markdown shortcut for each block, and table commands only appear inside a table. Filters can be several words, such as "heading 3" or "to-do list". A note opened from an agent thread now opens in that same editor in the right panel, so it can be edited there with a "Saving… / Saved / Not saved yet" status, instead of the old unstyled read-only text (its styles relied on a Tailwind typography plugin the app does not install). If that note is deleted elsewhere while it has unsaved edits, the panel stays open and keeps the edits so they can be copied. Uploaded `.md` files use the same document styles. The note card in a thread is now a small page preview showing the note's title and opening sections, with "Created just now" or "Updated 5m ago"; it keeps a selected outline while the note is open beside the thread, and three or more files from one reply collapse into one list. Notes are still stored as the same Markdown and saved through the same revision-guarded `/api/v1/notes` path. The canonical app mockup gains a note in Quill's thread and a notes-save fixture so these flows can be checked from disk.

- Split the harness provider-failure classifier into small helpers with no change in behavior. Its last change pushed it over the web complexity limit, which failed CI on main and removed every pull request from the merge queue.

- Say when a model provider, not Overlay, stopped an agent task, and log why. When a provider such as Anthropic sent an error partway through a response, the task ended with "Overlay hit an internal error", and the log line under its diagnostic ID only said the provider reported an error. Seen on Claude Opus 5.5 with a user's own Anthropic key. These errors are now classified from the provider's error type: server errors and unrecognized types are "the model provider could not complete the request" and are retried automatically like other transient provider failures; rate limits, timeouts, account or billing problems and invalid requests get their own messages. The `[harness] task stopped hd_…` log line now includes the provider's error type (for example `api_error`) as `providerType`, still without the provider's message text, so a diagnostic ID shows exactly what the provider reported.

- Stop agent tasks from failing with "The task stopped for an unclassified reason" when they split work into sub-agents, and make every diagnostic ID traceable. Sub-agents no longer have a cap on how many run at once (was 16) or how deeply they nest (was 5): the workspace budget, which stops work under 2% left, is now the only limit. When the backend refuses a sub-agent (for example an unknown plan step, an unmet dependency or tools the parent does not have), the agent now gets that reason back as a tool result and can fix it or do the step itself, instead of the whole task failing; asking for a sub-agent the run never started is handled the same way. Failures that used to be "unclassified" now get a cause: retired or unusable models are "model unavailable", an unreachable task backend is "backend unavailable", missing approvals and expired task deadlines map to their existing messages, and anything else is an "internal error" that says to send the message again. Every diagnostic ID now has a server log line: Convex logs `[harness] diagnostic recorded hd_…` for each one, and a task that stops on an exception also logs `[harness] task stopped hd_…` in Vercel with the error chain (secret-redacted, capped, and without raw provider text or model output). Older stored runs keep their `UNKNOWN` code.

- Prepare the agent backend to drop sub-agent caps and unclassified failures, ahead of the matching web change. Convex now accepts agent runs without the sub-agent count and nesting fields and ignores them when present, no longer refuses a sub-agent because 16 are already running or because it would nest more than 5 deep, and accepts the new failure codes "model unavailable", "backend unavailable" and "internal error". Every diagnostic it records now writes a `[harness] diagnostic recorded hd_…` line to the Convex logs, so each diagnostic ID can be found in the server logs. Existing web behaviour is unchanged until the follow-up web change ships.

- Add queueing and steering to chats, agent threads and automation threads. While a response is running, Enter now queues the message in a tray above the composer, and queued messages send one at a time, in order, once each response finishes. Cmd+Enter (Ctrl+Enter on Windows and Linux) steers: it stops the running response, keeps the partial reply, and sends the message right away. Each queued message can be edited, removed or sent with Steer now. The queue is saved per conversation in the browser, so it survives reloads. Stopping a response by hand pauses the queue until you send something yourself. Only text and mentions can be queued; attachments and quoted replies still send from the composer once the response finishes. Agent-thread composers now also show a Stop button while the agent replies. A queued message waits until the previous reply has fully finished streaming in the browser, so the earlier reply no longer shows up a second time under it. A chat is named from its first message only once, so a later message no longer replaces its title. With the same conversation open in several tabs, each queued message is sent by only one of them.
- Run automations as messages in their own thread. Every automation now belongs to exactly one thread: an agent thread when it was made by an agent, otherwise an ordinary chat named after it. The thread is created with the automation (older automations get theirs on their next run) and is listed in the sidebar like any other thread. Each scheduled run and each Run now posts the saved instructions into that thread as a user message and starts the turn the same way the composer does, so the run uses that thread's normal prompt, persona, tools, memory and approvals; when it asks a question or needs approval it waits in the thread for you. The run ledger now records the posted message and finishes from that turn's result on the next scheduler tick, and the next scheduled run is skipped while a previous one is still working or waiting for you. Personal chats now show the same Working, Needs you and Ready markers as agent threads in the sidebar. Run now opens the thread instead of the run visualization. Editing an automation no longer posts an "Automation updated: …" note into its thread; changes to the schedule, model or instructions simply apply from the next run. In a chat those notes showed up under an "Original message unavailable" placeholder. The old automation run workflow is no longer started; its code, routes and graph fields are removed in a follow-up. Adds `internal/plans/automation-threads-and-connector-return.md` with section 2 progress.

- Draw Mermaid diagrams in chat. When an assistant reply contains a ```` ```mermaid ```` code block, it now shows the diagram instead of the raw source, with a small Diagram | Code switch next to the copy button (copy always copies the source). Diagrams follow the app's light and dark colors, shrink to fit narrow chats and scroll sideways when they are too wide. While a reply is still streaming the block shows its code and only draws once the block is finished, so half-written diagrams never flash errors. If Mermaid cannot draw a block, the code stays visible with a quiet note and the short parse error. Each diagram is drawn inside its own shadow root, so styles a reply writes into a diagram (`classDef`, `style`) cannot change anything else on the page. The `mermaid` library loads only when a reply contains a diagram, so other chats download nothing extra. Adds the approved `mermaid-code-blocks` mockup under `internal/plans/mockups/`.

- Redesign the Extensions catalog (Connectors, Skills, MCPs and Apps) to match the approved mockup in `internal/plans/mockups/extensions-catalog.html`. Each page now has a title and one-line subtitle, a search box and an Add menu, and Browse and Yours tabs (Workspace in the Workspace scope). Browse groups entries into sections such as Popular, New & Noteworthy, Sales & CRM, Small Business, Project Management and Developer Tools, plus an All section for everything else; each section opens a full list with a back button and breadcrumb. Entries are compact two-column rows with the logo, name and a one-line description: + connects or adds, and ⋯ offers Settings and Disconnect (connectors) or Edit and Delete (skills and MCP servers, with a confirmation). Clicking a row opens its details: the existing skill and MCP editors, or a new small connector dialog with its status and Connect or Disconnect. Connector search now asks the provider for its whole catalog instead of filtering only the loaded page, so the long Available list, its Show more buttons and the separate Add Integration dialog are removed. MCPs gain a short catalog of well-known remote servers and Skills a set of starter templates; adding either opens the normal editor filled in, and Skills can also be created by uploading a `SKILL.md`. Links from global search (`?id=`) now open that skill or server. Apps stays locked and shows the same layout with an empty state. One shared page, row, grid, section and empty-state component in `@overlay/modules-react` serves all four types, and the old card grids are deleted.

- Recover agent turns whose model connection drops mid-response instead of failing them with "A model request ended without confirmed usage. Reconcile the outstanding request before retrying it." That happened when a long single generation (for example writing a whole page into one file) lost its stream before the provider reported usage. Nothing could clear the open call, so the turn always ended and showed the error twice. Now the dropped call is recorded as a failed, zero-usage call (it is not charged) and the harness retries it on the same model, up to three times in a row, so each call is still charged at most once. A provider that omits or sends invalid token counts is treated the same way instead of stopping the run. Real causes are reported first: timeouts, rate limits, provider outages, account and invalid-request errors now show their own message instead of the generic one. A permanent cause such as a rejected key is not retried. When the cause is still unknown after retries, the message now reads "The model connection dropped before the response finished. Send your message again." and appears once, as the error card, not also as the message text. Each failed run logs one `[harness] run failed` line with its diagnostic ID, code, stage and error name, so a diagnostic ID can be found in Vercel logs. Convex must be redeployed with this change because it checks the diagnostic wording.
- Make the app's interactions snappier, based on an animation audit and a production profile. Tooltips wait 300 ms instead of 450 ms, and once one is showing its neighbours open instantly with no animation, for both button tooltips and `title` tooltips. The `title` tooltip no longer slides in diagonally, which happened because its zoom animation overrode its positioning. The Cmd+K palette opens and closes with no animation. Opening a thread or loading earlier messages no longer replays the fade-in on every message. Only an exchange added at the end (a message you just sent, or a streaming reply) animates in, and the entrance respects reduced motion. Sidebars collapse instantly instead of animating their width, which re-laid-out the whole chat on every frame. Dragging a sidebar edge updates the width directly each frame and re-renders React only on release. Menus, popovers and selects now grow from their trigger and rise when they open upward. Exchange wrappers transition only opacity and position, not every property, and media action buttons press to 0.97 instead of 0.90. The tooltip interaction QA script now checks the warm-up.

- Remove plans and mockups for work that has already shipped, so `internal/plans/` lists only active work. Deleted the completed admin console, agent files and tool rows, chat AI Elements, CI audit, Convex-native, design-system report, shadcn migration (and Phase 8), gray controls, reliable connected agents and workspace budget-only plans, the release-workflow and shadcn QA evidence folders, about 90 shipped mockup files and the smoke script that only rebuilt one of them. The in-progress paid-first onboarding mockup and the brand generator and approved brand assets stay, and code comments and living docs that pointed at removed files now point at the living docs or git history.
- Replace the hand-built `agent-thread-activity` mockup, whose right sidebar and main framing had drifted from the app, with `internal/plans/mockups/overlay-app.html`: the whole web app in one file that opens from disk. It bundles the real production shell, sidebars, right details panel and every route page (Work list/board/timeline/organization and task detail, agents, the agent editor, chats, channels, DMs, Activity, Notes, Files, Automations, Connectors, Account and Settings) and answers Convex queries and app API calls from sample fixtures, so new mockups start from what production actually renders. Rebuild it with `node internal/plans/mockups/overlay-app/build.mjs`; the mockups README, AGENTS.md and the interface design doc now point at it.
- Let automations run on bring-your-own-key models. The automation turn checked only the server's hosted-model catalog, so every run on a BYOK model (for example a connected Claude subscription) failed with "not allowed by the server model policy". Chat, agents and the harness already authorize those models against the user's own provider connection. Automations now use the same check, and a missing or disabled connection is a terminal refusal.
- Open the most recently active accessible, unarchived thread when entering Agents without a specific conversation URL. Explicit thread URLs remain authoritative, while the existing oldest-thread main designation and legacy-DM/new-thread fallback remain unchanged.
- Fix scheduled automations, which had failed on every run since 2026-10-31 with "Automation run turn binding changed." When the Convex scheduler claimed a run, it stamped a turn ID like `automation-<runId>-<time>`. The run workflow then refused that ID because it only accepts `automation-run:<runId>`. Only manual runs, which take a different start path, still worked. The scheduler now only claims a due run. The run workflow binds the thread and stamps `automationRunTurnId(runId)` from the new `src/shared/automations/run-identity.ts`, and Convex rejects any other turn ID. Convex tests run a cron-claimed run through the workflow's start step.
- Run automations that belong to an agent as that agent. Their tools now save and search the agent's memory and use the agent's computer, and actions are attributed to the agent, not the owner. They already used the agent's instructions, tool grant and approval mode. The agent identity is read from the automation's current agent on every model and tool step and is never taken from saved run state. An automation without an agent still runs as its owner. `create_automation` now tells the creating agent to make the instructions self-contained, because runs do not read the conversation the automation was created in.
- Send automation outcome events again. `automation.succeeded` and `automation.failed` lifecycle events (failure email, audit, metrics) were only published by the old executor paths, so durable runs, which include every scheduled run, never reported failures. `AutomationService.settleRun` now publishes them once a run is settled by its owning workflow. That covers scheduled runs, Run now and `/test`, with the same metadata-only attributes and idempotency keys as before.
- Fix `save_memory` in automation runs, which always failed with "Memory operation failed". Tool calls add `automationId` (and the caller's user ID and token) to every request so the cost is billed to the automation. The memory route passed that whole body to Convex, and Convex `memories:add` rejects fields its validator does not name. The route now forwards only memory fields, so automations can save memory again: to the agent's memory for agent automations, otherwise to the owner's.
- Check the plan of the workspace an automation is created or enabled in, not whichever workspace was last active. Enabling an automation in a workspace that cannot pay now returns the normal 403 "Enabled automations require a paid plan" instead of a 500 "Failed to create automation". Preview QA hit this in a paid team workspace.
- Give every automation run one execution path. Scheduled runs, Run now and `POST /api/v1/automations/test` all start the same durable `automationRunWorkflow` from a run row (`AutomationService.startManualRun` for manual runs). The following are removed: the never-started per-automation sleep() scheduler workflow, the `schedulerWorkflowRunId` field and index (no production rows used them), the no-op `start-scheduler` route and its client method, the unused `/api/v1/automations/execute` callback, and the legacy self-calling executor (`run-act-turn.ts`). For older automations whose thread was stored as the chat they were created in, runs now create the automation's own thread instead of failing admission. `/test` still accepts `{automationId}` and now returns the durable run (`runId`, `workflowRunId`) instead of waiting for the reply.

- Make switching pages and threads feel instant. Chat, Automations, Activity, Notes and Tools no longer wait for a fresh server session check (and show a full-page skeleton) on every switch; they render from the session the app already loaded. Switching between agent threads updates the URL in place instead of doing a server round trip. Clicking an agent in the sidebar now only expands its threads instead of opening its latest thread; opening a thread moves that agent and its threads up below the chief of staff with a short slide animation, right on click. Agents with nothing in progress show just their name, without a "No active tasks" subtitle. In the composer, Backspace at the start of a list point (including the first one) turns it back into plain text or outdents a sub-point, and Tab / Shift+Tab indent and outdent points like Markdown instead of moving focus to the next button; nested points are kept when the prompt is sent.

- Add the generated Convex API entries for the harness diagnostic index and backfill modules that #491 left out, so typed code can reference them and the generated file matches what production deploys.

- Reject invalid native tool inputs before approval or side effects with safe corrective guidance, preserve later confirmed tool outcomes—including real failures—in the final delivery and durable transcript after approval resumes, and add operator-only diagnostic-ID lookup backed by durable diagnostic history, sanitized tool-failure records and an unexecuted bounded legacy backfill. Final transcript reconciliation rereads the current full receipt row on retries, including outcomes omitted from the bounded preview, and returned tool failures retain their corrective guidance in evidence. Native workflow tests cover transcript reconciliation during final delivery.
- Update the Workflow SDK from 5.2.3 to 5.5.0 (with `@workflow/vitest` 5.0.9, `@workflow/world-postgres` 5.2.0 and undici 7.30.2, the version the new Workflow packages require). This brings upstream runtime fixes, including parent runs that wait on a child run suspending instead of polling.
