The new Workflow compiler bundles AI SDK provider code into compiled steps, and those steps then loaded an older shared AI SDK helper package and failed to start. A package override now keeps one shared version of that helper, `@ai-sdk/provider-utils` 5.0.40, and the compiled-workflow test suite passes again. `internal/develop/convex-workflow.md` explains the override and when to raise it.
- Make harness model calls reuse the provider prompt cache, so long agent runs cost much less. Each step's working state (plan, milestones and recent results) used to be appended to the system prompt. That changed the start of every prompt, so providers had to reprocess the whole conversation at full price on every step. The system prompt now holds only the fixed instructions, and the working state goes in one trailing harness message that is replaced each step, never saved to history and never taken for a message from the person. Claude models now also mark the tools, the system prompt and the conversation for caching, which they need before they cache anything. The markers are added inside the model dispatch step. Runs on Gateway models served by several providers now prefer the provider that served most of their steps, keeping their cache warm while still failing over. Tool-choice restrictions for progress updates and final answers are unchanged; those calls still miss the cache. Adds `scripts/experiments/prompt-caching.mts`, a standalone script that measures cache hits through AI Gateway for each prompt layout; results are recorded in the pull request.

- Show the files an agent makes where you are working. An agent thread's Library tab now lists every note, image, video and document the agent creates as it creates it (including files published from its computer and connected-agent uploads, which used to be missing), newest first with thumbnails, and opens each one in a preview inside the panel instead of leaving the thread. Chat replies show created files as compact cards (video posters three across, image thumbnails that open a lightbox, note cards with a preview), and Markdown images no longer fill the whole message width.
- Make tool rows say what each call did, like "Ran `npm test`", "Read auth.ts:1-50", "Searched src for “x”" or "Edited WorkTaskComposer.tsx +20 −6", instead of "Running a command on the computer". Computer file writes now report whether they created or edited a file and how many lines changed, the "Worked for" header totals the lines changed, and secret-looking values typed into commands are hidden in the row.
- Show bullet and number markers in Markdown files opened in Files and in the agent Library preview. Tailwind's base reset strips list markers and indent, and the viewer's `prose` classes had no typography plugin behind them, so lists read as plain lines. The file viewer now restores disc, circle and decimal markers with a normal indent (task lists stay unmarked); chat Markdown is unchanged. A render test covers the marker rules.
- Redesign the Work → Organization chart. Agents are now compact, borderless rows in one grouped panel under the chief of staff, joined by a single line, instead of large outlined cards with connector stubs between grid rows that looked like a chain of command (Desktop → Growth → Scripting) that does not exist.
- Make the chat, agent-thread and Work composers identical in the Mac app and the browser by removing the desktop-only microphone and screenshot buttons. They squeezed the footer and showed a wrapping "Could not start dictation" error beside the send button; desktop dictation still works through its global shortcut.
- Fit the Work timeline to the page width: days now stretch to fill the available space (never narrower than 7 px, and the day letters hide below 14 px), so the timeline no longer scrolls sideways or shows a horizontal scrollbar unless a very long date range genuinely cannot fit. Resize grips on a bar scale with it (at most a quarter of its width each), so even a short task can still be dragged to move it.
- Keep bare numeric knowledge citations unlinked when a turn also has successful web sources, so ambiguous references are not attached to unrelated memories or files.
- Add a Preview-only personal conversation with 120 deterministic completed messages for pagination QA, without changing the existing performance histories or starting execution.
- Deduplicate ambient server-component session reads within each React request, while keeping explicit-request reads and refresh operations uncached. Session and authorization data are never cached across users or requests.
- Update the Workflow runtime (`workflow` 5.1.0 → 5.2.3) and its test plugin for upstream stream-transport fixes. Streamed chat output is now written to Vercel Workflow over WebSocket by default instead of HTTP, and HTTP appends that hit a confirmed rate limit (429) are retried with the full body instead of failing. Adds loopback retry coverage and a bounded Preview-only synthetic Workflow probe. Model retry and billing policies remain unchanged.

- Publish the app theme as the page's theme colour. The Mac app now uses it so its window follows the Overlay theme rather than the macOS appearance, which removes the black outline drawn around the light theme when macOS is in Dark mode.
- Fix the app flickering and loading forever when several Overlay tabs were open on different Personal/Workspace scopes. Each tab saved its own URL scope again whenever another tab changed the remembered one, so the tabs overwrote each other through browser storage many times a second, and any page whose address named no scope (such as `/app/work`, where opening getoverlay.io lands) kept remounting between Personal and Workspace. A tab now saves its scope only when its own address changes.
- Skip layout and paint for off-screen messages in long DMs and channels, so scrolling stays smooth with many messages loaded. Messages stay in the page, so Find and text selection keep working, and jumping to a pinned or linked message stays centred as off-screen rows finish layout instead of being pulled back to the latest message. Messages with an open picker or author card render normally so nothing is clipped. Loading older history keeps you on the message you were reading: the position is restored from that message's measured place and held while the newly loaded rows finish layout, instead of from an estimated height change that could move you thousands of pixels.
- Open agent destinations before resolving their main thread, with late-result guards that preserve newer navigation. Separate chat directory subscriptions from active transcript/execution updates so streaming does not notify sidebar and archived-list consumers unnecessarily.
- Scrolling a DM or channel marks it read once per transcript state instead of sending a read mutation on every scroll event, which re-rendered every loaded message. New messages, a different room, returning to the tab and jumping to latest still mark read.
- Reuse the room-message time formatter instead of rebuilding locale formatting state for every row render in long DMs and channels. Keep the existing time labels and invalid-date fallback.
- Redesign Settings → Desktop in the Mac app: clear Dictation, Local models, Floating panels, Agent host and About sections; shortcuts shown as key caps you click to re-record; one Open button per panel with docking in a menu; an Agent host row with its status and start controls together instead of a divided form; and a laptop icon for Desktop in the settings sidebar instead of the generic gear.

- Make invited onboarding workspace-aware and wait for live billing before showing the conversation. Paid workspaces skip subscription offers; free-workspace members can ask owners and admins to subscribe or open Work with their first task safely saved as a draft.

- Preview workspace invitations before signing in, see the inviter and workspace billing context, create an account with the invited email, or switch mismatched accounts. Email verification now signs new accounts in automatically when possible, and invitation emails name the inviter. Email/password sign-in loads the sanitized redirect as a fresh page so invitations immediately reflect the signed-in account.
- Refuse paid requests from a workspace without billing with `402 workspace_billing_required` (or `403 workspace_wallet_forbidden` for a member who may not spend the wallet) instead of a generic 500. Cloud dictation in a workspace that had not set up billing failed with "Failed to transcribe audio"; it now says billing is required, and the shared API wrapper applies the same answer to any paid route that lets this error escape.
- Commit the generated Convex API type entry for `lib/threadPullRequests`, which PR 458 added without regenerated bindings, so production backend deploys no longer leave that file changed. Types only; nothing changes at runtime.
- Keep only Archive in sidebar agent-thread rows, with fixed space beside pull-request indicators. Delete archived items immediately from the Archived page without a confirmation dialog.

- Show Jump to latest whenever messages remain below the visible conversation, even with no unread messages or while an agent turn is anchored. Jumping releases the turn anchor and scrolls to the bottom.

- Record last-observed GitHub pull-request associations from successful, server-authorized agent tool results and include them in authenticated workspace-thread projections.

- Align agent-thread progress icons beneath the agent avatar and remove curved indentation. Show linked GitHub pull requests only in sidebar threads, with lifecycle icons and last-observed details from successful integration actions. Keep response loading dots beneath the agent name only until the first displayed token. Start new agent exchanges near the viewport top without removing scrollable history. Center Help with the other rail controls and move Work task settings into a popover that does not expand the composer.

- Commit the generated Convex function index entry for the Preview-only performance fixtures (`dev/performanceQa`) that PR 448 added, so production deploys no longer leave that file changed, and remove a duplicated older changelog entry for the native deployment-feature projection.
- Keep unchanged completed personal-chat messages stable during streamed updates, while refreshing message actions, permissions and live plans when they change.
- Add an opt-in, Preview-only completed code-block fixture for first-use syntax-highlighting checks, without model execution or changes to existing histories.
- Load the syntax-highlighting engine only when a supported code block needs it, instead of including it in the initial app JavaScript. Keep code readable while highlighting loads and preserve streamed highlighting state.
- Performance measurements retain slow keystrokes that share a browser frame during streaming, rather than rejecting them when another character arrives before the frame is observed.
- Keep the Work composer controls on one row in narrow panels, with compact assignee, schedule and dependency icons, accessible labels, tooltips and count badges.

- Start personal-chat message subscriptions alongside summary loading instead of waiting for a second network round trip. Keep rows hidden until the current summary is authorized, and let opted-in route reads return an empty page for unavailable conversations without weakening authentication, workspace or capability checks.

- Fix a cancelled ChatGPT/Codex sign-in staying in the provider list. Removing a subscription connection failed with "Subscription cleanup is pending" when a sign-in check was still running, so the connection was never deleted. That check now hands its lock back as soon as it sees the removal, and removal waits for it (at most a minute) before deleting the stored sign-in data and the connection. This changes Convex subscription mutations.
- Let people cancel the ChatGPT/Codex sign-in dialog even when cleaning up the pending sign-in fails. Cancel used to show "Could not cancel this connection. Try again before closing." and keep the dialog open; it now closes at once and removes the pending sign-in in the background (fixes #454).
- Stop shutting down a computer that is being used just because its two-hour session ran out. A long build, an open terminal session or an agent run on a computer used to be stopped about two hours after the last computer action, even while it was busy. The session now keeps extending while the computer is in use (recent activity, an open action or a live run), and a computer stops on its own only when it is idle or the workspace budget is under 2%. A deadline a person sets on an agent task can now be longer than 7 days (the 30-second minimum stays). Docs no longer describe a "24-hour hard cap" on machines.
- Remove arbitrary limits that refused, stopped, cut short or degraded real work; the workspace budget (stop under 2% left) stays the only automatic spending limit. Agents now work in the model's whole context window instead of 32,000 tokens (200,000 when the catalog does not know the model) and get 32,000 output tokens by default instead of 8,192. A very large step no longer fails the run: its stored copy is shortened to fit Convex and the full step stays in file storage. Runs are no longer paused as "stuck" after six segments without visible progress (the recovery hint stays). Automation runs are no longer failed after 15 minutes; only a run that never started within 24 hours expires. Subscription connections accept images (other files are still replaced with a note) and requests up to 32 MiB instead of 2 MB; the subscription bridge changed to match. MCP tool results are shortened past 400,000 characters instead of 50,000. Agents asking agents: the 500,000-token chain limit is gone, and the limits are 50 questions and 50 posts per turn, 200 of each per person's message, 10 hops and 20 mentions. Sub-agents: 16 at once and 5 levels deep instead of 3 and 2. Connected agents: paid runs are no longer stopped after 24 hours (a 30-day credential deadline remains), a machine has 15 minutes instead of 2 to pick up a turn, one message can start 20 agents instead of 5, and each room message keeps 50,000 characters of context instead of 2,000 (the whole context is still bounded by the 128 KiB start-command size installed hosts accept). Earlier tool outputs read back by a model keep 20,000 characters instead of 1,000. Chat attachments allow 500 uploads an hour per person (1,000 per IP) instead of 20 (40). The notebook agent can take 200 steps instead of 20. The daily caps on memory extraction (120) and indexing (200 chunks, 5 MB) are gone; that work is charged to the workspace budget. Kept: security and abuse limits, cycle checks, per-file storage limits, provider hard limits, explicit deadlines and spend limits people set, and free-plan connected-agent limits.
- Make the workspace budget the only automatic spending limit. Overlay no longer estimates, quotes or holds money before a paid call: chat, agents, automations, tools, images, videos, transcription, embeddings, memory work and computers now check the real balance first and charge the actual cost after each call (images and videos when delivered; a cancelled connected-agent run is charged the tokens it used). Work is refused, or stops after its current step, only when the workspace has less than 2% of its budget left and one automatic top-up attempt cannot bring it back. Removed with the estimates: the image "known context limit" refusal, the $0.05 web search hold, the 10-minute computer hold, the $1 computer floor and $15 per-run cap, the per-task spend limit, the "grant more spend" card and its `POST /api/v1/conversations/run/resources` endpoint, and the pause for funding. Admin spend limits per member or agent, explicit deadlines and rate limits still apply. Charges use the cost known at the time (provider receipt or catalog price); the later exact-cost correction is gone. Paused runs from before can only be cancelled.
- Delete the hold machinery behind the old estimates (reserve/finalize/release, the expiry and stale-hold sweeps, the reconciliation queue and its audit script, the released-cost recorder, the margin report and sandbox settlement markers) and add a one-time Convex migration, `billing/retireHolds:releaseRetiredHoldsInternal`, that releases every hold still open, so that money is available again. It charges nothing, is safe to rerun and also clears old machine-lease holds. The `budgetReservations` table keeps its history until a later change removes it.

- Return the latest task revision after an agent posts a comment, and give revision conflicts the current task and safe retry instructions, so a progress comment does not leave an otherwise completed task stuck before review.

- Fix agents on a subscription or bring-your-own-key model still stopping with "The task stopped for an unclassified reason" right after taking a computer screenshot. Before each model call, the harness refused any request containing an image unless the model had a known context size, which these models don't report. They cost Overlay nothing to reserve, so their requests now use a size estimate and the run continues. Priced models still need a known context size before sending media.
- Explain why an agent task stopped when the model provider refused the request, instead of "The task stopped for an unclassified reason". A refused request (HTTP 400, 404 or 413, such as content the model cannot accept) now says to choose a model that supports it or remove the attachment. A rejected account, key, access or billing (HTTP 401, 402 or 403) now says to reconnect or check the provider in Settings. This adds two diagnostic codes to the Convex harness validators.
- Add the phased UI responsiveness plan and opt-in performance baseline tooling. Reject incomplete navigation/streaming samples and empty scenario selections, verify loaded DM history before scroll measurement, and report fixed-name failure stages without private diagnostic payloads. Add Preview-only fixed histories and a repeatable public-stream fixture without model calls.
- Fix agents on a subscription model (such as a connected Claude account) stopping with "The task stopped for an unclassified reason" after a tool returned an image, for example a screenshot attached to a GitHub issue. Subscriptions accept text only, so the next model request was rejected. The image is now replaced with a note telling the agent it could not see it, and the run continues.
- Keep the composer at the bottom in new chats and new automations, matching tasks and agent threads. The greeting stays centred with suggestions just above the composer, so the input no longer jumps from the middle of the screen to the bottom when you send the first message.
- Clear a failed or cancelled agent thread's red icon and "failed" count once you open it, the same way a reviewed completion loses its green check. Threads that need your input keep their indicator until answered.
- Turn on a computer by default when creating an agent in a workspace that has computers, so new agents can use a desktop without an extra step. The toggle still lets you create one without it.
- Show your first message in a new chat as soon as you press Enter. It used to stay hidden until the server finished creating the conversation.
- Reuse canonical workspace handles already resolved by the live authorized directory, avoiding a second lookup when opening or navigating that workspace. Aliases and unverified handles still use the membership-checked resolver.
- Standardize the approved Overlay wordmark and icon across loading, sidebar, email and metadata. The loader fades in the brand, then changes from the tagline to the workspace status with animated eyes. Nested streaming fallbacks keep the brand settled instead of replaying its entrance.
- Add activity-only sidebar filters for chats, channels, agents and their threads, preserving ordinary rows and using the simple "No activity yet" empty state. Remove the redundant Activity dropdown in favor of the bell.
- Align Computer availability, automation persona, memory, generated-UI, citation, and formatting guidance across chat, agent and automation prompts. Durable automations now include the bound agent's name and stored instructions.
- Run the Notebook assistant as an ordinary personal chat with one conversation per note, so it uses the same durable harness, billing, streaming and Stop as chat. Its prompt names the open note and it edits the note directly with the chat note tools. The editor shows each saved change as an inline diff that you can accept or reject one at a time, or all at once; rejecting restores the original text. Its message box is the chat composer, text-only, with the same send and stop button. The @ mention list now renders at the page level, so it can no longer be clipped by, or scroll, the panel it opens from. Notebook note tools stay available when a durable step rebuilds the chat's tools, so an edit no longer fails with an unknown tool outcome when the message itself did not ask for note tools. The Notebook assistant is also told to make every requested change through a note tool and to say a change was made only after that tool succeeded. Its tool rows are the same plain lines as chat, without the Overlay mark, and Accept all / Reject all sit below the latest response instead of in the panel header. The assistant starts on chat's default model (DeepSeek V4 Flash) and remembers a model picked in Notebook separately from chat. The separate `/api/v1/notebook-agent` route is removed.
- Add a Help menu with hover, touch and keyboard access to Docs and a bug-report dialog. Signed-in users can send a description and up to 5 MB of attachments to GitHub, with a Resend notification to the configured owner.

- Keep a live agent sentence ahead of the tool it starts. Before the step settled, a newly started tool row briefly appeared above the sentence that introduced it, grouped with the previous tool. Live text now goes before tools the running call has started.

- Fix agent-thread replies showing only the latest sentence while the agent worked. Each model call's text was dropped because the person's message and the agent's reply carry different turn ids. The reply now keeps every step's text in order, between collapsed tool rows, as chat does.
- Remove the muted check from reviewed agent threads in the sidebar. Only an unreviewed result shows the green check; once opened, the thread reads as an ordinary row.
- Show one avatar per streaming agent reply. The avatar beside the agent's name moves its eyes while it works, replacing the pulsing dots under the reply.
- Fix a type error in the agent-thread review and streaming mockup source that failed main's typecheck and the Docker image build.
- Add the approved design mockup for two agent-thread changes. A thread loses its check once its result has been reviewed. Agent replies stream as one continuous response, with text and collapsed tool lines in order, as chat does.

- Agent rules now put every new mockup in `internal/plans/mockups/` without asking where it should go. Gitignored `artifacts/` is used only when the owner asks for it.

- Explain why an agent turn did not start when the workspace has no paid plan, an unconfirmed payment, a used-up budget or billing that needs attention, and say who can fix it. Previously the reply read "could not start this turn" followed by "No failure reason is available". Every start failure now records a short reason on the failed reply.

- Correct the release note on blocked deploys: the BLOCKED Vercel status on a merged commit comes from the intentionally blocked merge-queue branch Preview, not from the commit's author email. Integration now checks that main's own production deployment exists and is ready.

- Align sidebar activity with native Convex authentication and shared thread visibility checks, removing legacy token arguments, backend-capability gating, and silent query-error fallbacks without changing the sidebar design.

- Render idle sidebar threads as text only, without reserving an empty status-icon slot.

- Honor the selected single or double sidebar layout in mobile and narrow-screen drawers instead of substituting the older mobile navigator.

- Show live thread status icons beside single-line sidebar titles, preserve the curved thread indentation on desktop and mobile, and summarize working, ready-to-review, and needs-you threads beneath each agent.

- Add the missing `lib/workDependencies` entry to the committed Convex API types (`convex/_generated/api.d.ts`). The Work dependency helper landed without regenerated bindings, so every production backend deploy rewrote the file. Types only; nothing changes at runtime.
- Fix pages that loaded forever after one failed sign-in token request. Convex asks for its token once, so a single network error, timeout or server error left every live list (Work's tasks and sidebar sections, among others) waiting until a reload. A failed request now retries after 1s, 2s, 4s and so on, capped at 30s, while the person stays signed in. A signed-out Work page shows "Sign in to see your tasks" instead of endless skeletons, and opening a task from the other scope no longer produces a `?scope=personal?task=…` link that dropped the task.
- Fix the Work page flickering between Personal and Workspace on open. Every part of the app now reads one shared remembered scope, and the page waits for it instead of guessing Personal first, so a link without a scope no longer redirects to Personal, the breadcrumb and sidebar toggle always agree, and the task lists no longer subscribe to the wrong scope and then drop it.
- Tidy the Work page: remove the header's New task button (the composer starts tasks), make the task title the task panel's header, size side-panel header buttons at 32px like other headers and line side-panel headers up with the main and sidebar headers, and shrink the Work composer to the same box as chat and agent threads. The empty list now points to the composer below instead of above.
- Link a task to the agent thread doing it: the task panel shows a Thread row with the thread's title and whether it is working, finished or stopped, and opens the thread in one click. A new `work/browse:linkedThread` query serves it only to the member who owns the thread.
- Fix agents that could not read the Work task they were assigned. The assignment message tells the agent to read the task with `get_task`, move it to In progress and Review with `update_task`, and comment with `comment_on_task`, but those tools came only from the agent's Tasks permission. An agent created without it (including the Preview QA fixture agents) answered that it could not access the task and never updated it. In the thread the assignment opened, the assigned agent now always gets those three tools, limited to that one task, and without its own Tasks permission it can change only the task's status and blocked flag. Workspace policy and the assigner's Work permissions still apply. Adds tests for the task lookup and the scoping.

- Add a bottom-pinned Work composer with searchable assignees and dependencies, Start/Due dates, context, status and priority. Agent assignments start immediately unless dependencies are unfinished, then start automatically when the last blocker is done.

- Stop a live room read-marker feedback loop that repeatedly fetched the conversation directory during onboarding and could exhaust browser resources before Subscribe reached checkout.

- Open Chats as a fresh personal draft, including desktop rail re-clicks, without creating an empty saved conversation or collapsing its sidebar. Keep onboarding consent checked and suppress the leave-site warning for the saved checkout redirect. After setup, retain the onboarding composer over Work until the loaded selected-task panel has painted and its entrance has finished, without a blank frame, delayed navigation tour or onboarding returning over later pages.

- Keep long answers intact when they are saved. A reply's main text could be saved at up to 80,000 characters, while all the reply's text together is allowed 180,000. Now that a long answer is written in fewer, larger model calls and continued calls are joined into one text part, answers longer than 80,000 characters were cut off with a "[truncated … chars for storage]" note, sometimes right where a call had stopped. The main answer can now use the whole 180,000-character budget; the total limit is unchanged. The continuation instruction also asks the model to start with the space or punctuation that comes next when the cut fell after a complete word, so words are not run together ("acknowledgesno").
- Fix long answers that restarted after running out of output tokens. When one model call hit its output cap, the next call never saw the text it had already written: the agent framework (`@ai-sdk/workflow`) drops a cut-off response from its conversation history, and the harness's working state carried only the response's first 8,000 characters. The model resumed from the end of that excerpt, so a 160-item list cut near item 117 continued with "I'll continue… starting from item 17" and later calls restarted the list. The harness now adds the cut-off response back as the last assistant message, records where it really ends in the working state instead of its opening, and asks the next call to continue from the exact character where it stopped with no preamble. A regression test covers a long cut-off list.
- Raise how much one model call can write in personal chat and workspace agents. Every harness call was capped at 8,000 tokens because the limit was taken as a quarter of the 32,000-token working-context budget. Reasoning models such as Claude Sonnet 5.5 count their thinking against that same limit and could use all of it on thinking, writing no text while the run kept continuing. Calls now request 32,000 tokens. Compaction keeps its working-budget reserve, and the reply is bounded only by the model's own maximum and context window. Longer replies still continue in the next call. Each call reserves more spend up front, and whatever it doesn't use is released when the call settles. If a call still uses its whole limit on thinking and writes nothing, the next call now runs with thinking off so it produces the answer, instead of every continuation starting to think again from scratch. Adds tests for the new limits and the thinking fallback.
- Fix long answers that broke apart where one model call ran out of output tokens and the next call carried on. The two calls' text was joined with a blank line, so a numbered list cut at "23" read "23", blank line, ". The Tagus…" and every later item rendered as one run-on paragraph. Overlay now records when a call stopped at its output cap and joins the continuation directly, in the saved message, while it is still streaming, and in workspace-agent and room replies, so the list stays intact. Text before and after a tool call still gets its blank line. Earlier messages are unchanged. Adds regression tests for the saved, live and final-delivery paths.

- Turn on unified durable streaming for every production workspace that exists today (owner decision after the #424 release): new personal chat, workspace agent and automation runs in those workspaces publish through the persisted stream, so output survives reloads and Stop keeps the partial answer. Enrollment is an exact-ID production setting, so workspaces created later stay on the existing path until added; rollback is removing the IDs and redeploying, and runs already started keep their path. Also refresh the generated Convex API types for the stream reader module that #424 added.

- Restore the Convex-native migration plan (`internal/plans/CONVEX_NATIVE_PR_PLAN.md`) at the owner's request as the completed record: every card from CN-01 to CN-37 is marked merged and live, with the closing PRs (#426 to #428) and the follow-up fixes (#419, #425, #429, #430) in its table, the HTTP reads kept on purpose and an outcome summary.

- Fix starting a direct message with someone you had a DM with before but had left, deleted for yourself or archived: it now reopens that DM for you. Before, it returned the old conversation without re-adding you, so it opened with "0 people", every action failed with an access error and it never appeared in the sidebar.

- Finish the Convex-native migration: the browser now reads everything Convex already serves from Convex. Settings → Agent accounts lists your Claude Code and Codex accounts live (a connect, reconnect or removal in another tab shows at once, and credential references never leave Convex). The agent editor, the bring-your-own-agent connection form and onboarding read connected-agent environments, bindings and whether the viewer may connect their own agents (owners and admins, when the deployment and the workspace's rollout allow it) live. Opening a chat link checks its type and scope with Convex instead of a conversation request, and the notebook loads a note's text from Convex. Removes an unused free-plan hook. The native-architecture guard now also counts client calls written with type arguments, which had let three reads go uncounted. The reads that stay on HTTP each have a recorded server reason: provider catalogs and connections, sharing disclosure before a share change, the participant check inside an archived-room delete, and the workspace list on the OAuth consent page. Deletes the finished Convex-native plans.

- Fix opening an archived chat from a link (a pasted URL, a reload, a new tab). Links from Settings → Archived and from the chat sidebar's Activity list now name their workspace (`/w/<handle>/archived?id=…`), so they open in the right workspace even when another one is active. Before, `/app/archived?id=` used whichever workspace was active: the chat could not be found there, and the page showed "Nothing archived" or quietly opened that workspace's newest archived chat instead. An id that is not archived in the current workspace now says "Archived chat not found" with a link to Settings → Archived, instead of opening a different chat. An archived personal AI chat now ends with "This chat is archived." and a Restore button, which restores it and opens it as a normal chat. Before, it showed "Thread safety checks are temporarily unavailable" with a Retry button that could never succeed, because archived chats are deliberately not loaded for sending.

- Add a CI guard that keeps the app Convex-native, and remove leftovers from the migration (CN-37). `npm run check:native-architecture` now runs with the lint and boundary checks. It rejects the retired database-provider capability names and the old browser login token. It also keeps a list of every browser HTTP call through `overlayAppClient`: a new one fails unless it is made native or added on purpose, and one that disappears must be removed from the list. Opening an archived chat (`/app/archived?id=`) now reads archived channels and DMs live from Convex instead of downloading every archived conversation over HTTP, and the always-true "native personal chat configured" flag and its fallback branches are gone. Deletes 13 API routes that nothing calls: profile sync, the native subscription check, the two old search routes, notification preferences, scheduler cancel, and the collaboration routes for message edit and delete, pins, presence, reactions, thread follow, notifications and saved messages, whose browser callers moved to Convex earlier. Also deletes the 16 API-client methods, 10 repository methods and 9 server-only Convex functions behind them. Fixes two phase test scripts that listed a deleted test and removes a smoke script whose file no longer exists. A chat link with a malformed `?id=` now shows the chat as not found instead of failing the chat list (the id was sent to a Convex query that rejects it).

- Fix workspace operational metrics and audit export for owners and admins. Both read the operator-wide audit log, which the customer web lost when the operator console moved to its own app, so every request failed with a 500 and Settings → Workspace → Sharing & links never showed the metrics block. Audit rows now carry the workspace they belong to (indexed), and the governance service reads only that workspace's events through a new server query; members still get the owner/admin denial. Audit export now reads the workspace's events oldest first, page by page, so an export larger than one page no longer drops its oldest events while claiming to cover the whole window. When a window holds more events than the requested `limit`, the export ends on a whole millisecond and returns `x-overlay-audit-complete: false` and `x-overlay-audit-next-from`, so the next request resumes with no gaps or repeats. A one-off backfill (`migrations/backfillAuditWorkspaceIds:run`) scopes events written before this change; new tests cover it continuing past its first 200-row batch and two workspaces never seeing each other's events. The web keeps failing these two reads until customer Convex is synchronized after the web release.

- Remove the old browser login token for Convex (CN-35). Every browser query and mutation already signed in to Convex natively with the session's verified identity token, so the second, HMAC-signed token minted by `/api/auth/convex-token`, its 4-minute refresh loop in the browser and its verifier in Convex had no remaining caller. They are deleted, and Convex now rejects that token type even when it is correctly signed, so it can't quietly come back. `/api/auth/convex-token` now always returns the native identity token, the release smoke test checks realtime sign-in natively (the signed-in account sees its own identity; a connection without a token sees none), and `INTERNAL_API_SECRET` stays for server-to-Convex calls.

- Remove the "which database provider" layer (CN-36, first part). Convex is the only backend, but the server still computed a capability set (`provider: 'convex'`, `supportsNotes: true` and twenty more always-true flags), passed it through every API route, sent it to the browser, and checked it before loading BYOK models, titling chats and creating file-ingestion jobs. That set, its helpers, two placeholder repositories that threw "not implemented", the lazy repository proxies, and the always-"convex" provider rows in workspace governance metrics are deleted. Domain HTTP services now construct their Convex repositories directly. `/api/v1/capabilities` and `/api/v1/bootstrap` no longer return `appDataCapabilities`; no web, desktop or mobile client read it. Three repository interfaces that nothing implemented are deleted too. The remaining repository interfaces each have one Convex implementation and stay as the seams services and their tests use; they are not a provider switch.

- Open a new channel or direct message right away: after Create channel or starting a DM, the conversation pane now switches to the new room at the same moment as the URL and sidebar, instead of showing the previous room or "Select a channel" for a while. Creating a room now uses the same in-place navigation as picking one from the sidebar, and the conversation pane re-reads the address after every navigation, so a plain link to another room can no longer leave it on a stale one.

- Fix automations failing with "Automation agent permissions have been revoked" before the model ran. The mid-run permission check required every admitted tool to be named by the automation's grant, but web search, generated UI and the connected-app router tools are admitted by other rules and no grant names them, so any automation with those tools failed. The check now judges each tool by the rule that admitted it: account tools by the current account and automation grants, connected-app tools by an unchanged connector grant, and web search, MCP and connected-app tools by a bound agent's capabilities (shared with the agent tool filter). Revoking any of these mid-run still stops the run.

- Fix Stop failing on the unified streaming path while text was arriving quickly: the cancel step read the live stream rows the executor rewrites several times a second, lost every automatic retry and returned an error, so the response kept running. Stop now cancels the run without touching those rows (which also turns away the executor's next write) and copies the text streamed so far into the response in a follow-up step, so the partial answer and the "interrupted" note are kept.

- Fix workspace-scope chats on the unified streaming path briefly showing a step's text twice (the settled copy and the live copy) while the response streamed. Those chats now settle each step into the stream checkpoint like personal chats, which retires the live copy in the same write.

- Add a versioned durable public-stream policy for explicitly allowlisted workspace IDs. A single executor publisher stores filtered response text in ordered 8 KiB UTF-8 segments, capped at 160,000 UTF-16 characters with an explicit truncation marker. Canonical transcript checkpoints retire matching output by run, generation, receipt and invocation; terminal messages never retain active overlays, and bounded cleanup removes segments after settlement, failure or cancellation. Versioned API SSE clients receive persisted text and settled tool-part metadata, while legacy runs retain their original writer. Browser timing is labeled `receive_to_next_frame_ms` (not paint); no cross-host timing subtraction or rollout performance claim is made without observed telemetry.

- Record in the Convex-native plan that Phase G (native search and pickers, live Settings → Archived, the live channel and DM sidebar, and the retired browser bootstrap reads) is merged and live in production, so Phase H is next.


- Make the channel and DM sidebar list live: the list comes from one Convex subscription, so a new message, a new room, a rename, joining or leaving, or archiving in another tab or by a teammate updates every open sidebar at once. The list loads 50 rooms at a time with "Load more", after weighing every room the member belongs to, so a recently active room is never pushed out by older ones or by agent threads. Removes the browser's chat-list cache with its retry loop and version-triggered refetch, the list-version query, and the sidebar refresh counter. The DM and channel unread badges now count straight from the live notifications instead of downloading 100 conversations whenever something is unread, and choosing Direct Messages or Channels picks the room to open from the live list instead of an HTTP request. The browser also stops calling `/api/v1/bootstrap`: the Integrations page reads its connector registry from the app's static config, and the model pickers' plan limits are live even when billing is turned off (they then show the member's own entitlements, which is what the server enforces). The bootstrap and conversation-list routes stay for API, desktop and mobile clients. Also fixes four regressions in the Phase G native search and archive views: @-mentions find channels and DMs the member belongs to again, and match skills, automations and MCP servers by description as well as name. Archived agent threads appear once in Settings → Archived (under their agent) instead of also as a direct message. A markdown link is left unresolved, rather than guessed, when its folder has more files than one read covers.
- Remove more polling from the app shell: a remote agent's status in a channel ("Running a tool · 12s") now comes from a Convex subscription instead of a request every five seconds, and the app's capabilities come only from the server-rendered page instead of a fallback request. Deletes an unused 15-second reconcile helper and an unused server loader for memories. The channel and DM sidebar list keeps its version-triggered refresh for now; making it native is the next step (CN-34b).

- Make Settings → Archived live: archived channels and DMs, files, folders and notes, skills, MCP servers, automations, tasks, agents and agent threads come from Convex subscriptions, each with its own access rules, instead of seven HTTP loads that refreshed only after your own actions or a workspace switch. Something archived in another tab or by a teammate appears at once, and restored or deleted items disappear on their own. Archived files and rooms are selected by their archive state before any limit, so many newer active items can never hide an older archive. Restore and permanent delete still go through the server, which keeps the audit log, removes stored files and stops automations.

- Make @-mentions, Cmd-K and the context pickers native: the chat composer, the notebook editor, Cmd-K search and Work's "add file, note, chat or link" search one indexed, permission-checked Convex query instead of downloading up to six 100-item lists per workspace or calling the old mention-search route. Results respect each person's access: items shared with the workspace are included; private, archived and deleted items, and categories the person can't use, never appear. Only the newest answer is shown while typing, Cmd-K tasks come from the live task list, and notes picked in Work are now typed as notes. Relative links between markdown files resolve through a native folder listing, indexed by folder so files elsewhere never crowd out a folder's own, instead of paging the file API. Connectors still come from the integration provider; a failed connector lookup is retried on the next search instead of hiding connectors until reload, and a good list is reused for a minute. Removes `/api/v1/mention-search` and the legacy Convex search function.
- Make Slack import progress live: the Slack import panel reads whether Slack is connected and every import job's progress from a Convex subscription. A running import keeps showing its real progress after a reload or in another tab, finishing Slack sign-in in another tab is noticed at once, and the 3-second job poll and the OAuth connection poll are gone. This also closes a gap where any signed-in account could list another workspace's import jobs: the old job-list query had no membership check and is removed, along with two unused job queries. The runner's job-listing queries are now internal-only. Listing Slack channels and users, and starting or cancelling an import, still go through the server. Pressing Connect Slack now keeps waiting for the sign-in for five minutes instead of ending the wait immediately.

- Make webhooks live: Settings → Webhooks shows your endpoints and their recent deliveries from Convex subscriptions. Each delivery's move from pending to delivered, failed or dead-lettered appears as the delivery runner records it, and endpoints changed in another tab update at once. The manual refresh button is gone. Signing secrets and payloads are never sent to the list. The webhooks permission now also requires webhooks to be turned on for the deployment, matching the settings route. Creating and editing endpoints, which checks their addresses, and rotating secrets still go through the server.

- Settings → Connected apps now names each connection's workspace from the live workspace list instead of loading the list over HTTP, so a renamed workspace shows its new name at once. Issuing tokens and disconnecting apps still go through the server, which seals tokens and records the audit trail.

- Make billing live: Workspace → Billing, the onboarding payment step, the chat composer's budget gates, model pickers and the sidebar usage meters read the workspace's plan and balance from Convex subscriptions. A Stripe-confirmed payment, a top-up, a charge or a plan change by another admin shows everywhere at once. Removes the sidebar's 60-second poll, the subscription refetch after every chat turn and the 3-second poll after checkout. The live views and the billing routes share one summary builder, so they always agree, and Stripe customer and subscription IDs never reach the browser. Checkout, top-ups, the portal and automatic top-ups still run on the server.

- Fix four edge cases in the live workspace and settings data found in review: accepting an invitation no longer leaves "Workspace unavailable" on screen if the live list catches up a moment later, a page open at a workspace handle follows a handle change made in another tab instead of stalling, the Slack import waits for the member list before marking Slack users as new, and a refused settings save shows the saved value again instead of the unsaved one. An older settings save can no longer replace newer settings, a background workspace check never pulls you back to a page you left, and the Slack import shows its loading state while members load instead of a false "no access" message.

- Make account settings and your name and photo live: theme, default models, the send key and other app settings, and your account name and photo now come from Convex subscriptions, so a change in another tab or on another device applies without a reload or the old 4-minute profile refresh. A change you just made is not undone by an older snapshot, and after switching accounts the previous account's name is never shown. Saves still go through the server, which validates them. Removes the unused account-profile read (`GET /api/v1/account/profile`), the unused identity-update browser event, and two placeholder server repositories for onboarding and settings that nothing used. Onboarding stays on the server.

- Make workspace members and invitations live: Settings → Workspace (people, guests, teams, roles and agents), chat @-mentions, the new channel and direct-message pickers, the memories owner filter and the Slack import read members from a Convex subscription. A role change, removal, new invitation, accepted invitation or renamed member now shows in every open tab, and someone removed from a workspace loses these lists at once. Pending invitations and guest grants are still shown only to owners and admins. Inviting and resending (which send email) and every other change still go through the server; the browser no longer loads these lists over HTTP.

- Make the workspace list and workspace policy live: the workspace switcher, the account page and Settings → Workspace read your workspaces, their names, icons, handles and member counts, your pending invitations and the workspace's sharing policy from Convex subscriptions. A rename, a new invitation, an admin's policy change (and the New buttons it governs) or losing access now reach every open tab without a reload, and a tab keeps the workspace its address names even when another tab switches. `/w/<handle>` links resolve natively. The HTTP workspace-list read (`GET /api/v1/account/workspaces`) and the server's received-invitations lookup are removed; creating, switching, renaming and policy changes still go through the server.

- Make agent surfaces live: an agent's Slack connection, its channel bindings and whether you may bind it now come from a Convex subscription, so a channel added or removed in another tab, or a Slack install degrading, shows without reloading. Listing channels, binding and Slack OAuth stay on the server, which talks to Slack. The extension host needed no change: it renders on the server and has no browser data loads.

- Make Computers and connected-agent environments live: Settings → Computers, the agent panel and sidebar, Settings → Environments, agent options and machine enrollment read from Convex subscriptions instead of five separate polls (every 2.5 to 15 seconds). Provisioning, start, stop, control, enrollment, approval and heartbeats now show as they happen in every view, and an environment whose heartbeat goes stale turns offline on a local clock without any request. Provider references, public keys and credential hashes are never sent to the browser. Starting, stopping and provisioning stay on the server, which calls the provider and checks billing.

- Make model-provider connections live: Settings → Providers and every model picker read your connections from a Convex subscription, so adding, testing, enabling or removing a provider in another tab, or a teammate sharing a subscription with the workspace, updates model availability at once. This replaces a request every 60 seconds and on every window focus. API-key vault references are never sent to the browser. App integrations still read their connected status from the integration provider, which is the source of truth for it.

- Make MCP servers and connected apps live: the MCP page shows configuration and connection state from a Convex subscription, so a server connected through OAuth in another tab, or changed by a teammate, updates without a reload or the old refresh-on-focus. Settings → Connected apps updates when an app is connected or revoked anywhere. Credentials, tokens and OAuth secrets are never sent, and members who cannot edit a shared server see its address without any key in it. Adding, editing, testing and OAuth stay on the server, which encrypts credentials and checks addresses.
- Open a shared MCP server you cannot change as view-only: its settings show but are disabled, and Save, Delete, Test, Connect and the move/archive controls are gone. Before, the dialog looked editable and the server refused the change only after you saved.

- Keep unsaved note text when the note is deleted or archived elsewhere: the editor now stays open with a notice so the text can be copied, and returns to Files by itself only when nothing is unsaved. Opening an automation from a chat now reads it natively instead of over HTTP, and the Files page drops a memories data loader it never used.

- Fix two problems found by testing with a second workspace member: a skill someone else owns now opens view-only for members who cannot edit it, instead of offering Save and Delete that the server then refused; and saving an automation whose form was opened before someone else changed it is now refused with a message, instead of silently undoing their change.

- Make skills live and native: the Skills page reads from a Convex subscription, and creating, editing, enabling or deleting a skill applies at once in every open tab. Editing a skill that changed elsewhere since the form opened is refused instead of overwriting the newer version. Removes the skills, MCP and extensions change events and the archive-restore refresh hook, which nothing listened to, and two unused file and note event constants.
- Fix the committed Convex API types, which had drifted from the generated ones.

- Make automations live: the sidebar list, the automation editor and its run history read from Convex subscriptions, so a schedule, name or enabled change made in another tab or by an agent appears at once, and runs move through queued, running, retried and finished without pressing Refresh. The editor follows remote changes only while it has no unsaved edits. Saving no longer reloads the automation, and opening one from the sidebar no longer fetches it first. Removes the automations refresh event, the sidebar's paged HTTP list and the runs Refresh button. Saving, enabling, running, cancelling and retrying stay on the server, which checks billing and manages the scheduler workflow.

- Make Work board moves native: dragging a task, moving it with the keyboard or approving and sending back a review applies at once in every open view and reaches other tabs live. The server still ranks the task between its new neighbours, so two people moving tasks at the same time end with one agreed order, and a refused move rolls back only that task instead of reloading the board.

- Make Work live: the Work page, its sidebar sections, the task panel and task links read tasks, people and activity from Convex subscriptions, so tasks and comments added or changed in another tab, by a teammate or by an agent appear everywhere without a reload. Edits, new tasks and comments are native and show at once; a refused edit rolls back by itself without undoing later ones. A task opened by link outside the list stays live too. Giving a task to an agent and moving it between scopes still go through the server, which checks the budget and starts the agent. Removes the provider's load, pending and refresh bookkeeping, the sidebar's separate load and publish channel, the Work refresh event, and the reload error notice.

- Make Memories live: Settings → Memories and an agent's memory section now read from one Convex query, so memories you add or delete, and memories extracted from chats and agent turns, appear without a reload in every open tab. Owner-only memories stay visible only to their author, and a memory written by an agent you cannot see is shown without that agent's name. The member filter now takes its agent list from the live agent directory instead of an HTTP request, and filtering by a member or agent no longer reloads the page's data.

- Keep open notes current: a note open in one tab now updates when it is saved in another tab, by an agent or by an outside app, as long as you have no unsaved edits. With unsaved edits your draft stays, and its save is refused with a conflict notice instead of overwriting the newer version. A note deleted or archived elsewhere closes and returns you to Files. The editor also stops downloading the whole notes list each time it opens, since the web app never shows that list. Saves still go through the server, which canonicalizes and sanitizes note Markdown.

- Make an agent thread's Library tab live: files an agent publishes or a person attaches in the thread now appear while the panel is open, instead of only after reopening it. Removes the outputs tab's Refresh button and its unused refresh state, since outputs are part of the live file list.

- Make Files live and open them instantly: the Files page and sidebar now read files, folders, notes and outputs from one Convex subscription per scope, so creating, renaming, moving, archiving or deleting anything shows up in every open tab without a reload. Clicking a file opens its viewer at once from the list while its text loads, an open file follows remote renames and closes if it is deleted or you lose access, and rename and move apply instantly everywhere. Removes the file change events, their reconciliation and retry machinery, the cross-tab event bridge nobody used, and an unused list controller. The live list carries no file text or storage keys, and the knowledge surface contract check now requires it and rejects the old events.

- Make Activity and read state native: notifications and unread badges come from one live Convex query, marking read updates every open client at once, and a notification that arrives while you are marking others read stays unread. Opening a notification goes straight to its room without an extra lookup. Removes the local read maps, two unused refresh events and the obsolete personal list-version subscription.

- Make room details live: participants, reactions, pins, saved messages, thread follows, mute and presence now come from one native Convex query per room, so they match in every open client without refetching. Reactions, pins, saves and follows update instantly and roll back by themselves if the server refuses them, and removing someone from a room closes it for them at once. Presence and typing now use native heartbeats instead of a polling fallback.

- Make agent threads live and immediate: the sidebar's thread lists update in every open client, opening an agent you have already expanded goes straight to its thread with no request, and creating, archiving, restoring or deleting a thread updates everywhere at once. Opening a thread no longer re-downloads the agent's thread list to check access, and a link to someone else's thread is still refused.

- Load DM and channel messages natively: rooms open from a live Convex query instead of waiting on an HTTP load, older messages and reply threads page in from an index (fixing threads and roots older than the newest 500 messages that could not load at all), and edits and deletes appear in every open client at once without a reload. Whether a room or thread accepts new messages is also read live, so opening a room or switching windows no longer makes a request that heavy tab switching could rate-limit.

- Make the agent directory live: the sidebar, agent picker, organization chart, automations, search, computer settings and room identities now read agents from one Convex subscription, so creating, renaming or archiving an agent shows up everywhere at once without a reload or refresh event. The agent editor follows changes made elsewhere while it has no unsaved edits, and never overwrites edits in progress.

- Fix automation builder chat, which had failed every send since Act stopped accepting the retired `automationMode` flag, and fix failed first turns in any chat, which spun forever with no error instead of ending and showing what went wrong.

- Move workspace and automation chats onto the same live Convex path as personal chats. Their lists, messages, run state, renames, archives, turn deletion, branching and draft edits now update across clients, while reducing chat code by about 1,900 lines and removing the old HTTP history loader, message patching, polling fallback and duplicate chat lists. Branching is now one atomic operation, so a failure cannot leave a half-copied chat.

- Require agents to update a plan's status and progress whenever they implement one of its phases, and add a route-by-route checklist to the Convex-native plan, so progress and remaining work can be verified from the repository.

- Make Convex-native personal chat unconditional: native browser sign-in is now required for every deployment, and the auth flag, backend readiness probe and revision markers are gone. Convex is the only supported backend, so these only added startup calls and extra code paths.

- Make new personal chats reload-safe: the URL, title and selected sidebar row now appear as soon as the chat is saved instead of after the whole reply finishes. A chat interrupted during its first turn now gets its generated title when reopened, opening a personal chat no longer blanks and remounts the chat surface while an unneeded HTTP scope check runs, and the composer and a false "thread safety unavailable" notice no longer flicker when switching chats.

- Require each Convex-native migration domain to delete its old path before the next surface starts, and move the migration to one implementer and one surface at a time, so old and new paths stop accumulating side by side.

- Add the Convex-native migration architecture plan and its phased PR plan under `internal/plans/`, with current card status, so agents and reviewers can reference the same scope, dependencies and acceptance gates from the repository.

- Land CN-05 personal history/sending and CN-06 durable execution/progress together on the main-targeted integration branch.

- Reconcile a sender's streamed native reply with its canonical row even when the local stream lacks the recorded model, so the sender no longer shows a phantom second response version until reload.

- Keep personal chat working during web-first releases and backend rollback by enabling native list ownership only when the backend advertises the matching revision. Validate all native personal-chat response shapes explicitly, prevent Undo from reconstructing a record already known to be missing, and preserve concurrent title changes while archive and Undo are both pending.

- Subscribe native personal chats directly to scoped execution state for lifecycle, safety and exact-turn commands, while retaining existing BFF execution and a fail-closed readiness rollback gate.

- Make native personal-chat transcript pages, preferences and user-turn admission authoritative in the browser while retaining the existing scoped HTTP execution, memory, billing and streaming path.

- Enable native personal history and sending only when the backend advertises compatible transcript support, preserving legacy ownership during web-first upgrades and backend rollback. Keep model preferences in the validated native summary contract.

- Add private, opt-in two-client chat and navigation baselines that distinguish query readiness, local feedback, HTTP acknowledgement, content visibility, and remote convergence without collecting chat or file content. Correlate acknowledgements to the exact mutation and sanitize setup failures.

- Preserve unsent drafts and source-page state during pending workspace activation, while keeping that page hidden and discarding it when the route or scope changes.

- Keep the chat composer visible while workspace access is being verified after navigation or rollback, without mistaking pending or failed entitlement reads for a billing denial; sending and the model picker remain blocked until access is confirmed.

- Keep URL-driven chat hydration read-only so remounting the previous chat cannot replace a pending workspace navigation with its old URL.

- Keep native personal-chat title synchronization field-scoped so live summary updates cannot reset model selection and repeatedly trigger metadata saves that exhaust the shared API rate limit.

- Add a server-controlled, fail-closed deployment-feature projection for native Convex domains. Policies are backend-bound, refreshed from the actual web configuration, fenced against stale callbacks (including delayed publication) and expired reactively; browser calls cannot publish policy. Existing conversation CRUD remains independent of deployment-feature mappings. Privileged agent Computer/credential cleanup stays server-owned.

- Move browser personal-chat list, title, archive/restore and basic unread ownership to native Convex with additive indexes and no backfill. Persisted `conversations.read` / `conversations.edit` capabilities are enforced even when the legacy BFF is in observe mode; native calls accept no caller policy flags or operator bypass. Optimistic writes rebase by row, bounded pages may be empty while filtered results remain, and verified-user writes are capped at 300 per 10 minutes because native calls have no BFF IP context. Legacy HTTP remains for native-off installations and non-pilot workspace chats, DMs, channels and other resources; transcript/history/streaming and deletion retain their existing server boundaries. Roll back web ownership first and leave backend fields/indexes in place.

- Add native Convex viewer and personal-chat scope guards that derive identity from authentication and recheck workspace membership, ownership and participant access on every call, excluding legacy automation-linked conversations even without their old flag and validating the minimal scope projections returned to callers.

- Clear native-agent, room and thread-reply composers as soon as the optimistic user message appears, instead of waiting for the save response. Restore failed submissions even after the rich editor resynchronizes, without overwriting newer drafts or another conversation.

- Remove the unused kimi.ai subscription variant from the bridge, API, configuration and database validators after confirming there are no production connections or pending authorizations. Kimi remains available through kimi.com, and the living adapter documentation lists only the supported providers.

- Enable Claude, Grok Build, Meta Muse, Kimi and Devin alongside ChatGPT/Codex in Production and shared Preview. Offer one Kimi choice using kimi.com and render the actual Meta and Devin logos instead of letter placeholders.

- Establish native Convex browser authentication with identity-isolated clients and refresh handling, preserving legacy subscriptions and allowing private providers to opt in after their backend upgrade; preflight hosted Preview builds with WorkOS on the exact claimed PR backend.

- Document subscription callback completion and workspace sharing in the API catalog, including active-member use and owner-only management.

- Keep subscription device sign-in polling after retryable provider failures, so a temporary outage does not silently stall authorization.

- Show each subscription connection's saved Personal or Workspace scope in Settings instead of always labeling it Personal.

- Add immutable personal/workspace subscription scope, active-member authorization, owner-only lifecycle management, and workspace-aware picker caching. The approved scope selector defaults to Personal, supports explicit Workspace sharing, and preserves scope on reconnect.

- Tighten subscription auth error classification, callback retry/fencing and provider identity checks; local fixtures remain distinct from hosted and live-account acceptance.

- Extend the private pinned subscription bridge and fenced control plane to Claude, xAI, Meta, Kimi and Devin, including callback/device authorization and provider-bound catalogs/inference. Added providers remain disabled in production until their individual rollout checks pass.
- Resume waiting runs when a person skips a single optional questionnaire question, instead of silently leaving the run paused, with regression coverage for skipped optional and blank required replies.

- Keep late model-cost corrections within unreserved credit and payer limits, and prevent old corrections from changing a renewed wallet while retaining the provider's full reported cost.

- Upgrade Better Auth and its SSO integration to 1.7.16, including the November 3 security fixes for private deployments. Hosted WorkOS authentication and the dependency cooldown policy are unchanged.

- Load complete public Harness text and tool records when expanding a response, with authenticated trace retrieval and separate full-payload storage. Compact chat snapshots no longer determine trace length; previously truncated historical payloads remain marked as legacy.

- Show the user's photo, name, and email above the workspace list, keep sidebar Settings and sign out while removing duplicate menu rows, and allow owned Harness continuations waiting on input, approval, children, or resources to finish usage admission.

- Recover personal chat responses after a dropped connection. Saved updates for these chats were ignored, so the text froze and the finished answer stayed hidden until a reload; they now appear while a run is followed, without blanking text already shown.
- Speed up agent runs by no longer waiting for exact billing data after each model call, which could hold a run for up to 16 seconds per call. Calls now settle immediately from token counts and catalog prices, and a background job replaces the estimate with the exact cost once it is available, charging or refunding the difference once. Calls with no catalog price still wait for the exact cost. Each model call also renews its authorization once instead of twice, and runs no longer make a separate progress-update call before starting work, which answered simple questions early and left the real reply with nothing to say.

- Keep text and tool calls in the order they happened when a response has source citations. Saved responses previously merged all their text into one block ahead of every tool call; responses already saved stay merged.

- Show an agent's plan with live progress at the end of its response. Once the response ends with every step finished, the plan moves into the "Worked for" dropdown, and unfinished plans stay visible. Finished personal replies now show only the final answer, with earlier narration, tool calls and the plan inside that dropdown. Internal plan and question tool calls no longer appear as rows.

- Replace the one-box agent input prompt with a questionnaire, so agents ask real questions with choices, written answers and optional skips. Empty prompts such as "placeholder" are sent back to the model instead of reaching people, and stuck-run prompts now ask a plain question.

- Remove harness-level checking of agent work: the final-answer verifier, its paid extra model call and retries, milestone evidence requirements, and the rewrite of unconfirmed note claims. This stops rejected drafts from repeating in responses. A step is now done when the model marks it done, so a model can report work that did not happen; people, or a verifier agent they add, check the result.

- Stop forcing agents with write tools to make a plan. Simple questions now get a direct answer, and the model plans only when a task is long and needs structure.

- Refresh subscription rollout status and remove the superseded in-process adapter plan so future agents follow the private bridge architecture.

- Update YAML parsing used by development and repository tooling. (#329)

- Update asynchronous OpenTelemetry context propagation. (#332)

- Update Word document conversion and remove its obsolete Bluebird dependency. (#333)

- Update the development CSS selector parser. (#338)

- Update the development regular-expression engine while retaining Node 22 compatibility. (#343)

- Update CodeQL initialization, analysis and SARIF upload together. (#334)

- Update lint and compiler cache actions, including read-only cache handling. (#373)

- Update the Semgrep engine used by the existing security scan. (#374)

- Improve artifact upload/download retry handling when GitHub rate-limits CI. (#375)

- Update container registry login handling for image publication. (#376)

- Refresh Python setup support and diagnostics for security scans. (#377)

- Preserve Codex event-stream framing and optional function-tool arguments so connected subscription responses can complete in chats and native agents.

- Recognize Codex Pro Lite/Max catalogs using explicit pinned plan membership, and preserve newly authorized credentials when catalog discovery is unavailable while continuing to block unknown-plan inference.

- Keep provider sign-in dialogs scrollable within short viewports so the connection action remains reachable.

- Use the shared reasoning-enabled model picker in General settings, reusing workspace-scoped new-chat preferences without changing existing threads.
- Add per-model reasoning preferences to chats and native agents, and expose only discovered Codex subscription reasoning capabilities.
- Route the first Codex subscription cutover through a private, pinned CLIProxyAPI bridge; keep credentials in Overlay Vault, refresh near-expiry credentials under Overlay's fenced pre-dispatch lease (never inside Go inference), and preserve saved model selections through reconnect failures. Keep isolated Preview backend provisioning in the web service build.
- Give each subscription its own model-picker group below Recommended, with a plug badge, scope label, independent favorites and reconnect action. Load the same connections in chat and agent pickers while preserving paid-access requirements, without silently replacing unavailable subscription selections.
- Keep agent progress visible with brief metered updates and a durable, sanitized transcript that survives workflow handoffs and failures. Uncertain tool outcomes stop further actions and show a safe diagnostic instead of automatically repeating work. Empty progress-only replies cannot abort otherwise valid work. Existing paid-workspace admission, selected models, funding waits and renewable execution leases remain enforced.
- Simplify agent Computer actions and correctly show the current operator and stopped state, retain document attachment chips after reloading a thread, and hide internal agent bookkeeping from saved responses. Computer artifact upload permissions now expire within 15 minutes independently of the task lease.
- Establish the approved agent-thread mockup as the canonical workspace-shell reference, remove ten conflicting workspace mockups and the superseded single-sidebar plan, keep mobile detail panels opaque, and document strict reuse of production sidebar components and styles.

- Keep the user-owned agent setup entry hidden when the deployment’s connected-agent rollout is disabled, while native agents and Computers remain available.

- Keep retired hosted-machine cleanup pending until a fresh provider lookup confirms deletion, preventing asynchronous delete acknowledgements from discarding cleanup ownership.

- Preserve private exact-origin hosting access for user-owned Agent Hosts, so retiring hosted Cloud runtimes does not break enrollment, signed API calls, or MCP tools on protected PR previews.

- Retire hosted third-party Claude Code/Codex runtimes in Overlay Cloud. Cloud creation, restart, run credentials, profile import and desktop launch are blocked; legacy agents remain readable and cleanup is lease-reaper managed. Preserve BYOM Agent Host, native Computers, provider connections, billing settlement, and historical records. Deployment is gated on an operator inventory, drain/export, and provider-deletion verification; completion is not established.

- Keep answer-correction instructions consistent with the available evidence-reading controls, so a verifier rejection does not also tell the agent to avoid those tools.

- Start Harness answer corrections as explicit continuation turns for providers that reject assistant prefill, and keep evidence-reading controls available without replaying completed external actions.

- Allow authorized artifact reads to satisfy explicitly declared artifact-reading milestones, while keeping reopened excerpts ineligible as proof of a new write.

- Preserve bounded, source-revision-linked observations when reopening private Harness artifacts, so final verification can inspect retrieved excerpts without treating them as new writes or independent verification.

- Preserve private Harness artifacts across authorized workflow handoffs while rejecting stale workers, and retain summary/verification holds until measured usage settles without restoring free-model execution.

- Keep Harness model-port, verification, compaction, artifact-prompt, and reducer validation paths focused and maintainable without changing their behavior or limits.
- Allow isolated Preview QA to lower the test owner's Harness working-context budget to exercise compaction, without changing production or other users' budgets.
- Keep Harness continuations grounded in persisted evidence, page omitted observations, verify nontrivial final answers, preserve context in private chunk-transport artifacts, and recover from failed compaction or verification without releasing paid or unknown provider holds.
- Keep owned funding-paused Harness tasks waiting while payment verification is pending, without renewing execution authority or bypassing paid admission; revoked and stale runs still fail closed.

- Preserve the pending response when context summarization pauses for workspace funding, including after durable workflow serialization; foreign pause receipts cannot resume the task.

- Reconcile durable Harness continuation with paid-workspace admission: retain the selected model on funding failure and require current verified payment before creating automatic top-up claims. Existing subscriptions and markup are unchanged.

- Preserve fractional-cent funding balances through durable Harness pauses and prevent workspace-funded task errors from falling back to a different model instead of waiting for funds.

- Seed funding QA agents with valid editable avatar values so saving a model does not fail unrelated avatar validation. Existing fixtures and balances remain unchanged on reseed.

- Preserve generation fencing, authorization pauses, and lease renewal when combining durable continuation with the streamlined personal Harness startup path.
