Storage limits, the automations paid check and the paid/free answer behind the model picker and zero-data-retention setting now follow the plan of the workspace you are working in (chat, agent and generation runs already did). Plans go from $8 to $500 a month (was $200). `/pricing` is the new page: free workspaces (Auto model with weekly limits, notes, chats, files, knowledge, 10 MB; a person can own up to three), paid from $8 a month as that month's credit, unused credit does not roll over. The on-demand (hard stop or automatic top-ups) and itemized-bill sections are on `/pricing/preview` until those features ship. The six existing paid personal plans were moved onto their owners' home workspaces with the plan-conversion tool (same Stripe subscription and credit). Plan: `docs/plans/WORKSPACE_PRICING_PLAN.md`.
- **Home page: a Discord link in the nav, and the title no longer stacks words when you scroll fast.** The Discord icon sits between the light/dark toggle and GitHub. The changing part of the section title now fades the old words out before the new ones arrive, and keeps at most one leaving word, so a quick scroll through several scenes no longer piles them on top of each other.
- **The home page is a scroll story, with a working miniature of the app on top.** `/home` now opens on "The open source multiplayer cloud control plane for AI employees" and a small, clickable copy of Overlay (agents, chats, files, extensions, automations; type a message and it answers). Scrolling folds the five underlined phrases into a left rail and walks through AI employees (memory, tools, a computer), the control plane (create, manage, deploy, bring your own agents), cloud, multiplayer and open source, each with its own animated graphic and a pair of agents that slide in from the screen edges; the last screen lists where to get Overlay (web, desktop, mobile, MCP, API). The rail and sidebar items jump straight to a scene, and the footer's `/home#agents`, `#platforms`, `#computers` and `#automations` links land on the matching scene. The footer is unchanged. Brand logos are self-hosted in `public/assets/svg/landing`. The old overview page, `LandingShowcase`, `landing.css` and `landing-toast` are removed. iOS and Android currently link to `/download` (no store pages exist yet), and MCP and API link to the API reference in the docs.
- **An agent's settings load all at once, and you can pick any color for its avatar.** Opening an agent's settings used to show the default Overlay form and then fill in the machine, access, model, connection, memories and reachability one after another as each loaded, with the layout jumping each time. The settings now show one loading state until everything has loaded, then appear together (the machine's access and model reuse the agent that was already loaded instead of fetching it a second time). If a part never answers, the form shows anyway after 10 seconds. The avatar picker has a rainbow swatch after the preset colors that opens a small picker (a square for saturation and brightness, a hue strip, and a hex field; no opacity); any six-digit color is saved.
- **One Archived page in Settings; no more Archived row in every sidebar.** Settings → Archived lists everything you have archived: chats, files, notes and folders, agents, agent threads, skills, MCP servers and automations. A dropdown (All, Chats, Files & notes, Agents, Extensions, Automations) and a search narrow it; each row says whether it came from Personal or Workspace. Restore puts an item back where it came from. **Delete forever** removes it for good, one at a time or many at once: tick several rows (shift-click for a range), then Restore or Delete forever from the bar that appears; every delete asks first, and a partial failure (an item you may not change) is reported rather than hidden. Archiving anything now shows a note with **Undo** and **View archived**. Agent threads are kept apart from their agents: archiving a thread archives only that thread, the agent stays live and in its list, and opening it goes to its next active thread (a new one if none is left); deleting an archived thread deletes only that thread, even if it was the agent's only one. Archived agents are their own rows, and deleting one also deletes its threads (`DELETE /api/v1/agents/[agentId]/permanent`; only archived agents; the default Overlay agent is refused). Old `?scope=archived` links and `/app/archived` open the new page (an archived chat still opens in its own reader). Connectors are not listed (they were not in the old Archived view either). Each source lists up to 100 rows.
- **A machine's desktop is open to whoever may use its agent; the transcript stops at a permission card; `archived` is gone from the list tools.** Control (and the desktop route) is no longer limited to owners and admins: a personal agent's machine is its creator's alone (an owner or admin gets nothing extra), a workspace agent's is open to the workspace's members (not guests). When Claude Code or Codex asks permission, what it produced after asking is no longer shown above the card; the transcript resumes in order once you answer. `list_notes`, `list_files`, `list_skills`, and `list_automations` no longer take `scope: archived` (archived items are a UI view, not something agents list).
- **Connected agents' finished tool calls keep their descriptions; a Control button opens the machine's desktop.** A Claude Code or Codex action named itself when it started ("git clone https://github.com/…") but ended with a generic "Tool call", because the agent sends no name when it finishes and the update replaced the old one. A finished action now keeps the name it ran under, its detail too. Terminal, file-edit, and plan rows are named for what they are ("Terminal output", "Edited src/app.ts", "Plan") instead of "Remote Terminal / Diff / Plan". Actions already recorded keep the generic name they were saved with. On an agent's Machine card, **Control** (owners and admins, while the machine is ready) opens its live desktop in a new tab, waiting for the stream to be ready if the machine had been paused.
- **Fixed: a Claude Code (or any connected) agent could get stuck rejecting every message after a run was cancelled; the "Waiting for <computer>" row is now a shimmering tool line; the agent settings panel loses its dead space at the bottom.** Cancelling a run marked the run's commands that the machine had not received yet as cancelled, which left a hole in the numbered stream the machine reads; the machine then rejected every later command ("expected command sequence N") and nothing could start until the machine was replaced. Cancelled-but-undelivered commands are now delivered as harmless shutdowns (the way finished runs already were), so the stream has no holes. A rejected command now says why in the chat row (for example "(command_sequence_gap: expected command sequence 6)"). A repair, `agents/connectedAgents:reopenUndeliveredCommands`, unwedged the one affected production machine. While a connected agent's computer is waking up the chat shows "Connecting to computer" as a running tool line with the shimmer instead of "Waiting for overlay-cloud-…"; Cancel and Retry are unchanged. The agent settings panel no longer has about 130px of empty space under Cancel.
- **Agents in a room others can read can no longer search or list the summoner's private items (Tool scoping T4, second slice).** In a channel anyone in the workspace can read, or a room with more than one person, an agent's searches (knowledge, memory, messages) return only what the workspace shares, its file, note, skill, and automation lists are limited to the Workspace scope, the tools that read the person's own chats and file text are not offered, and it can use only the workspace's connected accounts and shared MCP servers. This applies to Overlay agents and to Claude Code / Codex agents connected over MCP; a chat between one person and an agent is unchanged. Not yet covered: reading an item by id, the write tools, computer and browser tools, and agents asking agents; there is no per-message "include this" yet. Also made the memory backfill: the 1,729 existing people's memories (and their indexed chunks) are now private to whoever saved them; agents' own memories stay shared. See `docs/develop/tool-catalog.md`.
- **Agents answering in a room others can read no longer bring in the summoner's private material (Tool scoping T4, first slice).** When someone mentions an agent in a channel anyone can read, or in a room with more than one person, the context loaded for it automatically (the person's memories and memory profile, retrieval over their files, notes, and memories, and their personal skills) now leaves out what is private to them; what the workspace shares is still loaded. A chat between one person and an agent, and a private channel with one person in it, are unchanged. Not covered yet: the agent can still call search and list tools as the person during a turn (the next slice), and a person can still ask for a specific private item. See `docs/plans/TOOL_SCOPING_PLAN.md`.
- **New memories are private to the person by default (Tool scoping T0, memory).** A memory an assistant saves about someone in a chat, or that they add themselves, was shared with everyone in the workspace unless marked otherwise, so a teammate's search could recall it. New memories now default to the person's own; saving one as shared still works, and an agent's own memories remain workspace knowledge. Memories saved before this were private-by-backfill on 2026-10-06 (1,729 people's memories and 1,586 chunks); agents' own stay shared. See `docs/plans/TOOL_SCOPING_PLAN.md`.
- **A personal plan can become a workspace's plan, on request (Workspace scoping, Phase 5b step B4; operator-run, nothing converted).** For a person who pays for a personal plan, an operator can move that plan onto one of their workspaces so every member draws from the same credits: the same billing account (and its Stripe subscription, balance, and history) becomes the workspace's, the person's own plan becomes Free, and Stripe renewal and payment events follow the plan to the workspace. A read-only preview lists what would block it and what it would change; the move is one all-or-nothing step and can be reverted. It is refused while auto top-up is on or workspace billing is not enabled for the workspace. No account has been converted, and there is no button for it yet. See `docs/develop/billing-plan-conversion.md`.
- **Fixed: an account-level top-up on a personal account was granted and then silently erased.** A personal account's balance lives in the person's subscription row, and the account-level copy is rewritten from it after every billing change, so a top-up, subscription change, or refund written only to the copy disappeared on the next change. Those account-level writes (meant for workspace accounts) now refuse a personal account with `billing_account_balance_is_legacy_synced`, and the sync refuses to touch a workspace account. Normal personal top-ups were never affected (they use the personal path); the only live way to hit it was a manual ops grant. This was the blocker for converting a personal plan into a workspace plan.
- **Any workspace can have its own plan (Workspace scoping, Phase 5b step B3; off until workspace wallets are switched on).** Workspace billing is no longer limited to organization workspaces: owners and admins of any workspace, including a first one, can add a plan from Settings → Workspace → Billing (Free, with an **Add a plan** button, then the usual subscribe/top-up/manage controls). A workspace with a paid plan draws every member's usage from its shared credits; without one it stays on the free allowance, so setting up a wallet does not bypass the free limits, and an organization with wallets on but none set up is free instead of failing. Workspaces with a plan do not count toward the three-workspace limit. Production behavior is unchanged until `OVERLAY_FEATURE_WORKSPACE_WALLETS` and its rollout stage are turned on. A first workspace can still not be archived or given away. See `docs/plans/UNIFIED_SCOPES_PLAN.md`.
- **The free allowance belongs to the workspace (Workspace scoping, Phase 5b step B2).** On the free plan, the weekly count of chat turns (ask, write, agent) and transcription seconds is now shared by everyone working in a workspace, instead of being per person, and a person has a separate allowance in each workspace. Paid plans are unchanged. A person can own up to 3 active workspaces (archive one to create another), so creating workspaces cannot multiply the free allowance. Counters start fresh per workspace; nothing is migrated. See `docs/plans/UNIFIED_SCOPES_PLAN.md`.
- **A workspace that is just you is simpler (Workspace scoping, Phase 5b step B1).** When you are the only person in a workspace, the side panels drop the Personal/Workspace split: Files, Extensions, Chats, Agents, and Automations list their own rows (for example All, Notes, Files, Outputs) with Archived below; Chats shows Chats and Channels only (no person-to-person Direct Messages or Activity); Agents lists every agent, including the default Overlay agent; and Move to Workspace and the Personal/Workspace tags are hidden. Anything you create stays private to you, so inviting someone later exposes nothing. The full interface appears when a second person joins. This is derived from how many people are in the workspace, not a setting. Also fixed: the "N members" label and the first-run workspace naming counted the default Overlay agent as a member, so a workspace with one person read "2 members" and the naming step was skipped for new people; both now count people (`humanMemberCount` on the workspace summary). See `docs/plans/UNIFIED_SCOPES_PLAN.md`.
- **Fixed: teammates could search other people's personal chats and DMs.** Every chat message was indexed as visible to the whole workspace, so in a workspace with more than one person `search_messages` and `search_knowledge` could return text from someone else's personal chat or a DM they were not in. Only public-channel messages are shared now; personal chats, DMs, and private channels stay with their author, and retrieval re-checks each foreign message against its conversation so nothing indexed under the old rule can leak while it waits to be re-stamped. Existing message chunks were re-stamped (no re-embedding). Search inside a DM now finds your own messages, not the other person's (you can still open the chat). See `docs/plans/TOOL_SCOPING_PLAN.md`.
- **Workspaces stop looking special (Workspace scoping, Phase 5a).** Every workspace now has a name that says whose it is ("Maya’s workspace"), not "Personal": the 249 existing first workspaces that still had a stand-in name were renamed, and new people name their workspace before the tour. Owners and admins can rename any workspace from Workspace settings (new `PATCH /api/v1/workspaces/{id}/lifecycle`). One avatar and one "N members" label for every workspace. Billing is unchanged; workspace-level billing (5b) and a plan for scoping tools and search (`docs/plans/TOOL_SCOPING_PLAN.md`) are written up for approval.
- **Workspace connectors, Move/Archive everywhere, and tidier expanded rows (Workspace scoping follow-up).** Extensions → Workspace → Connectors now shows the same connectors, but connecting one links the workspace's own account (shared by every member, separate from personal accounts, unaffected when someone leaves); one per connector, removable by whoever connected it or an owner/admin, and who may connect follows the "anyone can share skills, MCP servers, and connectors" setting. In a workspace, agents get a second connector tool set (`workspace_…`) that acts through those accounts. Not yet exercised with a live OAuth link. Move to Workspace/Personal and Archive are now also in the note editor, the file viewer, and card-layout files and folders. What an expansion reveals (a scope's sub-rows, threads) is no longer indented under its parent; the rule is in `internal/develop/interface-design.md`.
- **Move, archive, and restore from the app; agents use what the workspace shares; usage top-ups follow the workspace setting (Workspace scoping, Phase 4).** Hover a file, note, or automation row, or open a skill or MCP server, to move it between Personal and Workspace or archive it (select several files to do it in bulk); restore from Archived. Buttons appear only where you are allowed, and moves disappear for members when an admin turns moving off. Skills and MCP servers shared with the workspace are available to every member's agents (an MCP server runs with its creator's credentials on Overlay's side; members never receive them). Mentions find shared items. The list tools (`list_notes`, `list_files`, `list_skills`, `list_automations`) take an optional `scope`. Members may add workspace usage only when "Anyone can add usage" is on. Moves and archives are written to the audit log. Archived files now show the right Personal/Workspace tag. Connector sharing is not included yet. See `docs/plans/UNIFIED_SCOPES_PLAN.md`.
- **The prerendered shell's two inline scripts are allowed by hash under the nonce CSP.** With `SECURITY_CSP_NONCE=true`, the static shell shipped two inline scripts with no nonce, which the browser blocked on every `/app/*` load (two console errors): React's `$RT` timer and the theme initializer, so the saved theme was applied only after hydration. Both are now allowed by hash (`INLINE_SCRIPT_HASHES` in `src/proxy.ts`, with tests that fail if React or the theme script changes).
- **One secondary panel for Chats, Files, Extensions, Automations, and Agents: Personal, Workspace, Archived (Workspace scoping, Phase 3).** Each page's side panel now starts with the same three rows; the selected one opens that page's sub-rows beneath it (Files: All/Notes/Files/Outputs; Chats → Workspace: Direct Messages/Channels/Activity; Extensions: Connectors/Skills/MCPs/Apps), followed by New and search, then the list. Your choice follows you between pages and is in the URL as `?scope=`. New is hidden in Archived, says "in workspace" in Workspace, and is hidden for members when an admin has restricted who can create there. Archived shows everything archived from either scope with a Personal/Workspace tag and a restore button. Notes, uploads, skills, MCP servers, and automations are created in the scope you are viewing. Moving and archiving items from the UI arrives in Phase 4. See `docs/plans/UNIFIED_SCOPES_PLAN.md`.
- **Personal and Workspace scopes, and Archive, for files/notes/outputs, skills, MCP servers, connectors, and automations (Workspace scoping, Phase 2; backend only, no visible change yet).** Every one of these can now be Personal (only you, within the workspace) or Workspace (shared with its members), and archived from either. New workspace settings, all defaulting to "anyone in the workspace": who can create or edit workspace skills/MCP servers/connectors, who can create or edit workspace notes/files/outputs/automations, whether members may move items between Personal and Workspace, and who can add usage (stored; enforced later). Only the creator can move an item; owners and admins can edit and archive other people's workspace items but never move them back to Personal. Lists take `?view=personal|workspace|archived`, creates take `scope`, and new `POST /api/v1/scope` moves, archives, or restores. Workspace memories now hide other people's owner-only entries. Existing items are all Personal, so nothing changes for anyone until something is moved. See `docs/plans/UNIFIED_SCOPES_PLAN.md`.
- **Knowledge bases, projects, and the legacy notes/outputs tables are removed (Workspace scoping, Phase 1).** The unused knowledge-base and project tables and every `projectId` field are gone from the schema. Notes and outputs live only in `files`; the 33 live legacy notes and 18 completed outputs that had never been copied were migrated first, so some people will see old notes and outputs reappear in Files. Old note and output ids still resolve. See `docs/plans/UNIFIED_SCOPES_PLAN.md`.
- **UI polish: agent threads, the agent panel, and the people picker.** In the sidebar, an agent's threads and "New thread" now line up under its avatar and name, and the archive and delete buttons on a hovered thread sit on a solid background instead of being hidden by the title. The agent side panel now looks like the new-agent dialog (centered avatar, then Name and Description), with the Config buttons side by side ("From your computer", "Upload folder") instead of stacked. The picker for adding people to a DM or channel shows each agent's own avatar and description instead of a generic icon.
- **Agent replies fold their working details; Settings → Agents.** In rooms and agent conversations, a finished agent reply shows only its final answer (the text after the last tool call), with the tool calls and the text written between them behind "Worked for…, called N tools"; expanding shows everything in order. A reply with no tool calls shows all its text. Agents are asked (native agents' system prompt, and a RESPONSE_STYLE line for Claude Code / Codex) to end with a short summary. "Settings → Agent accounts" is now **Settings → Agents**, with a new "Collapse working details in responses" toggle (default on; new `collapseAgentWork` setting) above the Agent accounts list. Actions an agent did not name show as "Tool call" instead of "tool call". Personal chats are unchanged.
- **Codex agents on a ChatGPT sign-in accept the model and reasoning choice** (host 0.3.10, image `overlay-agent-v5`). Choosing a model such as `gpt-6-luna[high]` made every run fail ("the ACP agent did not advertise that model") because a ChatGPT sign-in offers plain names; the host now applies the name and sets the reasoning level separately.
- **An agent whose computer was deleted can get a new one.** When credit ran out the machine and its lease were deleted, but the agent kept its old environment and a "ready" record, so messages failed with "could not start this turn" and provisioning again was skipped as already done. The agent page now shows "Machine stopped" with a **Start a new machine** button; provisioning a gone machine revokes the old environment and boots a fresh one (`reviveIfMachineGone`), and a message sent to such an agent says what to do. The status response now includes the agent's `adapterId`. Also: the reply placeholder with the agent's avatar now stays below your sent message instead of jumping above it.
- **Settings and dialog polish.** The unused API keys section is removed from Settings → Account (the `/api/v1/api-keys` routes stay for programmatic access). Providers and Computers now have their own icons in the settings sidebar instead of repeating General's. Info tooltips (the ⓘ in the new-agent dialog and elsewhere) now show above dialogs and menus instead of behind them.
- **A machine stopped for lack of credit now finishes its chat rows.** The credit-stop path cancelled the run but left the agent's message "generating" forever (and its remote session open, which also counted as "a run is going"); it now closes the message with a note, closes unfinished actions, and ends the session.
- **Agents can read chats.** New tools `list_chats` (find a chat by title) and `read_chat` (its messages, oldest first, labeled by speaker, newest 40 by default) are in the memory tool group, the outside-app Read only level, and personal chat's base set, so "summarize @some chat" works for native agents, Claude Code and Codex on Overlay Cloud, and outside MCP apps. Agents saved earlier gain them through `LATER_GROUP_MEMBERS`.
- **Stopping a Claude Code / Codex run really stops it.** The generic Stop (`/api/v1/conversations/stop`) used to mark a connected agent's run cancelled in the database without telling its machine, so the agent kept working; it now cancels such runs through the control plane (cancel command to the host, billing settled).
- **A reply placeholder with the agent's avatar appears the moment you send a message to an agent**, instead of nothing for a couple of seconds. The header's "Attach" button and its dialog (which did nothing) are removed.
- **The idle sweep's fallback runs every 10 minutes instead of every minute** (the per-minute pass still meters billing and supervises runs; the event-based idle timer is the primary stop).
- **Agent machines stop exactly 10 minutes after their last run ends.** The idle stop is now event-based: a run's end schedules a check for 10 minutes later (Convex scheduled function, cancelled by any newer message through a token on the lease), which stops the machine unless something happened or a run is going. The per-minute meter pass remains as the fallback: it starts a missing timer and stops a machine whose timer was lost, 2 minutes after the window. "A run is going" is now a real check of the environment's remote runs instead of the run's 24-hour maximum, so a run that never settles no longer keeps a machine up. See `docs/develop/agent-machine-idle.md`.
- **Choose the model of a Claude Code or Codex agent, and connect its account while creating it (Overlay Cloud agents).** The new-agent dialog and the agent page have a Model field: Claude Code (Default, Opus 5.5, Fable 5.1, Sonnet 5, Haiku 4.5) and Codex (GPT-6 and GPT-5.x models, with a Reasoning level), read from the agents themselves. The choice is stored after `byo/<adapter>#` in the agent's `modelId`, sent to the host as `metadata.model`, and applied through ACP `session/set_model`; a model the agent does not offer fails the run with the agent's own message. In the dialog, an agent with no account shows a Connect button (ChatGPT sign-in for Codex) and the account is saved under Settings → Agent accounts. A connected agent's actions now show the title the agent gave them instead of "Remote Action", and its text and actions stay in the order they happened instead of all actions collecting at the bottom. Agent Host 0.3.9, image `overlay-agent-v4`.
- **Cloud agent machines stop after 10 idle minutes (was 15), and the connected-agent run UI is simpler.** A running connected agent is stopped with the Stop button in the message toolbar (it cancels the run); the separate "Cancel run" text button is gone. The second, avatar-less "responding" indicator under the messages is removed, leaving the one with the agent's avatar.
- **Codex on a ChatGPT subscription, experimental agents, and E2B (Phase 6).** Settings → Agent accounts can sign in to Codex with ChatGPT (device code). Overlay keeps the rotating refresh token itself, refreshes it under a lock, and gives each run a short-lived `auth.json` with a placeholder refresh token, so several Codex agents can share one account. OpenCode, Hermes and Cursor agents can be created on an OpenRouter or Cursor key and are marked experimental. Self-hosters can run machines on E2B (`OVERLAY_MANAGED_SANDBOX_PROVIDER=e2b`). Agent Host and bridge protocol 0.3.8. New route `POST /api/v1/provider-accounts/codex-sign-in`. OpenCode was run on production (cheap default model `deepseek/deepseek-v4-flash`); Hermes is held back until its install works on Boat; the agent image is now `overlay-agent-v3`; live ChatGPT verification is pending. See `docs/develop/codex-subscription.md`.
- **Overlay Cloud agent machines (Phase 0, behind `OVERLAY_FEATURE_OVERLAY_CLOUD_ENVIRONMENTS`).** `POST /api/v1/agent-environments/cloud` provisions a Boat machine for an agent from the new Overlay agent image (`overlay-agent-v1`, built by `infra/agent-image`), starts the Agent Host on it with Claude Code or Codex, approves the environment with a fixed workspace root, and binds the agent. Idle machines are stopped by the lease meter and resumed when a turn arrives. The Agent Host gains an acpx engine (`--engine acpx`, acpx 0.19.2) and an `image-check` command; on-your-machine hosts are unchanged. No UI yet. Verified end to end against real Boat machines: provisioning in 12–15 s, an @mention answered by Claude Code on the machine, and a stopped machine woken by the next message. The machine's server URL comes from the configured app URL rather than the request origin, and every Overlay Cloud turn checks (and if needed wakes) its machine.

- **Import your Claude Code or Codex config into an Overlay Cloud agent (Phase 4).** The agent page gets a Config section: run one command on your computer (`export-config`, from the Agent Host package) or pick your `.claude` / `.codex` folder, review what was found (skills, commands, subagents, output styles, prompts, MCP servers, instructions) and what was left out and why, then apply it to the agent's machine. Credentials, history, and plugins are never copied; MCP server tokens become named values you enter once (stored encrypted, given to the agent only while it runs); hooks stay off until you turn them on. Versions are kept and an earlier one can be restored. New `agentProfiles`, `agentProfileChunks`, `agentSecrets` Convex tables, `GET/POST /api/v1/agents/{id}/profile`, and public one-time-code `POST /api/v1/agent-profiles/upload`. See `docs/develop/agent-profiles.md`.
- **Agents can ask each other (Phase 5 of Overlay Cloud agents).** A new tool group, "Ask other agents", gives an agent `list_agents`, `ask_agent`, and `read_agent_reply`, for Overlay agents and for Claude Code / Codex agents on Overlay Cloud alike. An agent asks another by name; the question appears as a message from the asking agent in a conversation of you and both agents, the asked agent answers with your access, and the answer comes back to the asker. Questions can be passed on at most 3 times, an agent can never be asked twice in one chain, one turn may ask 3 questions, and one message of yours may cause 12 in total. Existing agents with the full toolbox get the tools; narrower ones do not. New `agentAskCounters` table and `POST /api/v1/agent-asks`. Agents can also post into rooms they are in and mention other agents there (`post_message`); a chain stops asking once its asked agents have used 500,000 tokens; each question links back to where it came from; ChatGPT, Claude, Cursor and other MCP apps at Everything can ask an agent as you (30 an hour); and a direct link to an agents' group conversation now opens in the room view. See `docs/develop/agent-to-agent.md`.
- **Cloud agents wake reliably after being idle.** A machine idle for more than 15 minutes could not wake: its host's credential had expired and could not refresh, so the agent never answered again. An expired credential may now refresh itself for an Overlay Cloud machine (for up to 7 days, still signed by the machine's key). A separate bug could leave the host rejecting every command after one was skipped or cancelled while it was offline; skipped commands now go out as harmless shutdowns. Pausing or restarting a machine now fails the run it interrupted at once, with a message, instead of blocking the next message for 15 minutes. Imported Claude Code config is now actually loaded by the agent (the host starts with `ACPX_CLAUDE_INCLUDE_USER_SETTINGS=1`), imported permission modes that skip or deny Overlay's approval prompts are dropped, and applying a profile removes folders it empties.
- **Connect other AI apps to Overlay over MCP (Phase 3 of Overlay Cloud agents).** ChatGPT, Claude (web and desktop), Cursor, Claude Code, Codex, and any MCP client can now use a person's workspace: add the address from Settings → Connected apps, sign in to Overlay, and choose the workspace (personal or team) and what the app may do (Read only, Read and write, or Everything). The server is `/api/mcp` with OAuth 2.1 (dynamic registration, PKCE, rotating refresh tokens) and a consent page at `/oauth/authorize`; local agents can use a personal token instead. Each connection is listed in Settings → Connected apps and can be disconnected at once. Tools run through the same pipeline, policy, and billing as an Overlay agent, acting as the person; computers, agent editing, and conversation-only tools are never offered. Fixed `list_notes`, which returned an error (and would have capped at 20 notes) because the notes API now answers lists with a page envelope. New `mcpGrants` Convex table. See `docs/develop/mcp-access.md`.
- **Stuck usage holds are settled automatically.** Failed or overrun provider calls used to leave their budget hold in place forever, so remaining credit could read far below allowance minus spend (one account: $11.66 held of $20). Holds older than 6 hours are now settled hourly: finished-but-overran calls are charged what was held, failed calls are released. See `docs/develop/billing-holds-and-machine-metering.md`.
- **Overlay Cloud agents get Overlay access.** An agent created from the dialog now holds an Overlay access level (default Everything: notes, files, memory, knowledge, automations, connected apps, your MCP servers), changeable on its page under Overlay access; before, its MCP server exposed no tools. Creating a machine needs enough credit to run it (otherwise it would be deleted within a minute), and the agent page says when a machine is gone. Fixed the create dialog hanging on "Starting", and machines failing to reach Overlay when the app URL redirects (`OVERLAY_AGENT_PUBLIC_URL`). Verified on production with a real Claude Code subscription.
- **Connected agents can ask for approval of an Overlay tool.** A tool call that needs approval (for example a tool on one of your MCP servers) now shows an approval card in the conversation and runs once you allow it; before, it was refused. Overlay skills are also offered to MCP clients as prompts (Claude shows them as slash commands).
- **Fixed the automation tools.** `update_automation`, `pause_automation`, and `delete_automation` failed in every agent and chat because the tool's own automation id was overwritten by an empty one when the request was built (found by calling every tool the new MCP server offers).
- **Other agents on Overlay Cloud, in the new-agent dialog and on the agent page (Phase 2).** Where Overlay Cloud agents are enabled, "Other agent" in the new-agent dialog creates a Claude Code or Codex agent on its own machine: pick the agent, the account, and a machine size, watch it start, and chat once it is ready. The agent page gets a Machine section with its state, Pause / Resume / Restart, and a Reconnect button when its account needs signing in again; archiving the agent deletes its machine. Creating a machine (`POST /api/v1/agent-environments/cloud`) now returns 202 and provisions in the background, with a durable startup record behind `GET /api/v1/agents/{agentId}/machine` (also `POST` for pause/resume/restart and `DELETE`). Paid plans only. New `cloudAgents` capability and `cloudAgentProvisions` Convex table.
- **Agent accounts (Phase 1 of Overlay Cloud agents).** Settings → Agent accounts connects the Claude Code and Codex credentials an Overlay Cloud agent runs on: a `claude setup-token` token or Anthropic API key, or an OpenAI API key. Secrets go to the credential vault; only metadata and an opaque reference are stored, and no API response or log includes a credential. A cloud agent is bound to one of its creator's accounts, and the host fetches the credentials for each run (new `POST /api/v1/agent-environments/{id}/run-credentials`, Overlay Cloud environments only) and hands them to the agent process in memory. An agent that rejects its credentials ends the run with `auth_required` and flags the account "Needs reconnecting" until it is reconnected. `POST /api/v1/agent-environments/cloud` now requires `providerAccountId`. The agent image is now `overlay-agent-v2` (the bridge protocol gained the run-credentials method); `OVERLAY_CLOUD_AGENT_IMAGE` defaults to it.

### Fixed

- Harden durable remote-run integration: preserve legacy host polling, validate fresh recovery commands, expose confirmed fresh starts, require stop evidence before cancellation settlement, fence old callbacks and keep replacement health reporting active.

- Keep standalone admin navigation accessible when mobile labels collapse to icons.

- **Deleting an account now deletes its stored credentials from the vault.** Account deletion removed the Convex rows for model-provider keys but left the secrets in the credential vault, now also true for agent accounts. It lists every vault reference the person stored and deletes each before the rows are removed; if any cannot be deleted, the deletion stops with an error and can be retried, so no secret is orphaned. Needs the new `listCredentialRefsByServer` Convex queries deployed first.
- **Sandbox commands honour their own environment.** The Boat adapter applied only the machine-wide environment and silently dropped a command's `environment`.

### Security

- Updated the MCP SDK, sharp, proxy-addr, and source-map-js to patched versions required by the production dependency audit. MCP OAuth credentials now preserve their authorization-server binding; older unbound credentials require reconnecting.

- **`devalue` 5.9.4** is pinned past three advisories (GHSA-j22f-vq7h-c4qm, GHSA-mcm9-63f2-9j32, GHSA-x5rw-q4pp-hg5g) that failed the production audit.

### Changed

- **Site metadata repositioned to "Overlay: The control plane for AI employees".** Root title (now a `%s | Overlay` template), descriptions, keywords, Open Graph, and Twitter cards (now `summary_large_image`) updated; every marketing and legal page now carries its own title, description, and canonical. Added Organization/WebSite/SoftwareApplication JSON-LD on marketing pages, a `FAQPage` block behind a new landing FAQ section, `public/llms.txt` for answer engines, explicit AI-crawler rules in `robots.txt`, and the missing `/download`, `/self-hosting`, `/blog`, `/changelog` routes with `lastModified` in `sitemap.xml`. `/` now issues a real redirect to `/home` for confirmed guests instead of a client-side navigation, and the visible tagline ("The control plane for AI employees.") is consistent across the hero, footer, and auth pages.
- **`DelayedTooltip` now runs on a shadcn/Radix tooltip.** New canonical `components/ui/tooltip` in `packages/overlay-ui` (portal, collision-aware positioning, arrow, delayed-open animations); the existing `DelayedTooltip` keeps the same `{label, side}` props, so every callsite upgrades with no consumer changes. 450 ms show delay and fine-pointer hover gating are preserved. Third step of the shadcn/ui migration. Keyboard focus associates the tooltip with the actual control, preserves existing descriptions, and cleans up on dismissal; pointer-capability changes update hover gating immediately.

- **`@overlay/ui` dialogs now run on shadcn/Radix primitives.** New canonical `components/ui/dialog` + `alert-dialog` in `packages/overlay-ui` (Radix focus trap, scroll lock, portal, `role`/`aria` wiring, Escape dismissal); the existing `DialogFrame` and `ConfirmDialog` re-export the same props on top, so all 16 callsites upgrade with no consumer changes. Adds `radix-ui`/`clsx`/`tailwind-merge`/`class-variance-authority`/`tw-animate-css`, a real `cn()`, and repo `components.json` pointing into the package. No visual changes intended. Second step of the shadcn/ui migration (`docs/plans/SHADCN_MIGRATION_PLAN.md`). Controlled dialogs restore focus to their external opener; asynchronous confirmations remain open while busy, invoke each callback once, and block repeated Enter/Escape dismissal.

- **Tailwind CSS upgraded to v4.** The app now builds on `tailwindcss@4` via `@tailwindcss/postcss` with CSS-first configuration (`@theme`, `@custom-variant`, `@source`). `dark:` utilities now follow the app's `[data-theme]` attribute instead of the OS color scheme — previously 61 `dark:` classes ignored the in-app theme toggle. The base palette has a single canonical definition in `@overlay/ui` styles; `globals.css` keeps only app-specific variables. No visual changes intended. First step of the shadcn/ui migration (`docs/plans/SHADCN_MIGRATION_PLAN.md`). Inline note-diff markers and action buttons retain valid `flex-shrink` declarations.

- **Computers moved to Boat's API.** Box renamed itself Boat, and the legacy `ascii.dev/api/box/v1` endpoints advertise a 2026-10-31 sunset. The sandbox adapter now calls `https://boat.dev/api/v1` (`/sandboxes/*`, `sandbox` envelopes, `sandboxId` in named snapshots) and still reads legacy `box` envelopes. `BOAT_API_KEY` is preferred; the existing `BOX_API_KEY` keeps working, so no secret change is needed to deploy. `BOAT_API_BASE_URL` can override the base. The internal provider id stays `box`, so leases, prices, and stored references are unchanged. Verified with the live conformance and desktop suites against Boat.
- **New agent is a short dialog.** "New agent" now opens one small dialog instead of creating an "Untitled agent" and opening the full editor: avatar (click to pick a shape and colour), name, description (the agent's instructions), type, a Computer toggle (off by default, provisions the machine on create), access (Only me by default), and a collapsed Advanced section with the model and tools (every tool on by default, with Everything / Standard / Read only presets). Nothing is created until **Create agent**, so cancelling no longer archives a draft. "Other agent" is shown but disabled until connected and hosted agents are rebuilt. The avatar palette grows to 11 colours and the editor uses the same avatar picker.

### Removed

- **Vercel Sandbox is removed.** Hosted harness agents (Claude Code, Codex, OpenCode, Pi, Hermes in an Overlay sandbox) are gone: the AI SDK `HarnessAgent` turn workflow, harness registry and bridge, the Vercel sandbox adapter, `POST /api/v1/sandbox/run` and its orphan sweeper, Vercel sandbox pricing, the `@ai-sdk/harness*`, `@ai-sdk/workflow-harness`, `@ai-sdk/sandbox-vercel`, and `@vercel/sandbox` dependencies, `POST /api/v1/agent-environments/managed`, `POST …/reset-harness`, the editor's hosted-runtime picker, and the `managedHarnessAgents` capability. The `./overlay` file sync built for harness sandboxes went with them. Agents that were bound to a hosted runtime stay read-only in the editor, and messaging one posts a reply explaining it must be recreated. The Overlay MCP server for connected agents is unchanged (its tool builder moved to `agent-mcp-tools`). The sandbox lease meter now prices Box (and Daytona until it is removed); leases for `vercel` are refused. `OVERLAY_VERCEL_SANDBOX_*`, `OVERLAY_MANAGED_SANDBOX_PROVIDER`, `OVERLAY_EXEC_SANDBOX_PROVIDER`, `OVERLAY_EPHEMERAL_SANDBOX_STALE_MS`, `OVERLAY_HARNESS_*`, and `OVERLAY_AGENT_HOST_IMAGE` no longer do anything and can be unset. `providers.sandbox.provider: vercel` is still accepted so existing configs validate; the default is now `box`.

- **Creating agents on Overlay Cloud is disabled.** The runtime picker for hosted Claude Code, Codex, OpenCode, Pi, and Hermes no longer appears, the control plane refuses new managed-harness bindings for every workspace regardless of flags, and `POST /api/v1/agent-environments/managed` returns 410. These runtimes are being rebuilt from the ground up (Box in the cloud, E2B for self-hosting); Vercel Sandbox and Daytona are being removed. Existing agents, Computers, and connected (on-your-machine) agents are unaffected. Plan: `docs/plans/SANDBOX_PROVIDER_CONSOLIDATION_PLAN.md`.

### Added

- **Agent Host 0.3.6** (`@layernorm/overlay-agent-host`, `@layernorm/overlay-agent-bridge-protocol`): connected agents receive Overlay's MCP server for workspace tools. New enrollments pin 0.3.6; existing hosts keep working without it and pick up the tools when upgraded.
- **Workspace files in every managed harness sandbox** (since removed with Vercel Sandbox, see Removed): before a hosted Claude Code, Codex, OpenCode, Pi, or Hermes turn, the summoning person's workspace files are mirrored into `./overlay` in the agent's working directory (notes as `.md`). After the turn, files the agent changed or created there are saved back to Overlay. Edits update the original guarded by the mirrored revision, an edit that raced a change in Overlay is saved as an "(agent copy)", and deleting a mirrored file never deletes it in Overlay. The mirror is rebuilt each turn, needs the agent's `read_file` grant (saving back needs `write_file`), and is limited to 2,000 files and 100 MB. `OVERLAY_HARNESS_FILE_SYNC=0` turns it off. The sync runs host-side through the same file and note services as the app, so no storage credentials enter the sandbox.
- **Large text files live in object storage**: text over the Convex document limit (~850 KB) is now stored as one R2 object with a searchable inline prefix (`files.textInObjectStore`), rather than being split into `(part N of M)` rows. `read_file` and `GET /api/v1/files?fileId=…&fullText=true` return the full text. Edits move text between inline and object storage as its size changes. Agents' `write_file` accepts up to 10 MB. The Files editor shows these files read-only, since it only loads the prefix. `list_files` now pages past the list endpoint's 100-row cap.

### Fixed

- **Files and folders created through `/api/v1/files` land in the caller's workspace.** `FileService` received the request's workspace but dropped it on every create path: new folders, text files, R2 uploads, and ingested documents. The rows had no `workspaceId`, so workspace-scoped listings (the Files page, `list_files`) never showed them. Background ingestion now carries the job's workspace too. Rows created before this fix still have no workspace; `migrations/backfillWorkspaceIds` can assign them.

### Security

- **Next.js 16.3.8** fixes a remote-code-execution advisory in `next/og` `ImageResponse` (GHSA-vcvr-r3jv-pc5j), which the share pages' Open Graph images use. `axios` (1.20.0) and `@grpc/grpc-js` (1.14.5) are pinned past advisories published 2026-09-30 that failed the required production audit on every commit.

### Added

- **Overlay workspace tools for every harness agent**: hosted harness agents (Claude Code, Codex, OpenCode, Pi, Hermes in an Overlay sandbox) and connected agents on a user's own machine now get the same workspace tools as an Overlay agent with the same grant: notes, files, memory, knowledge search, automations, connected apps, and MCP servers. Hosted harnesses receive them as host-executed tools, so each call runs on Overlay's servers and the sandbox gets no token or extra egress. Connected agents receive an MCP server (`POST /api/agent-mcp`) through the Agent Host, authenticated by a per-run token that stops working when the run ends. It needs a host release (the metadata key is ignored by older hosts). Agents also get file tools (`list_files`, `read_file`, `write_file`, `create_folder`, `move_file`). Reads join the Knowledge group and writes form a new File editing group; revision-guarded writes return 409 on a concurrent change. Grants saved before a group grew now hold the tools that joined it, which also fixes agents created before the patch-style note tools not receiving them. Fixed: every tool call in an agent turn shared one idempotency key, so a second write to the same route in one turn was rejected with 409; keys are now derived per tool call and per request, which keeps replays idempotent.

- **Notes are Markdown, with patch-style agent tools**: notes are stored as GitHub-flavored Markdown with `$…$`/`$$…$$` math. Before, the editor saved TipTap HTML and agents saved Markdown, and the loader guessed the format with a regex. Editor features Markdown can't express (underline, highlight, sub/superscript, alignment, YouTube embeds, merged or sized table cells) are kept as small sanitized inline HTML. One converter, `@overlay/app-core/note-markdown`, serves the editor (Markdown ↔ TipTap HTML at load and save), the server, the share page, and the backfill. `NoteService` stores Markdown even when older desktop clients send HTML, and reads legacy HTML rows as Markdown. Agents get `append_to_note`, `replace_note_section`, and `edit_note` (exact find/replace), so a change no longer means regenerating the whole note. `get_note` returns a `revision` and a heading outline, and every agent write is guarded by the revision it read, so an agent edit can no longer silently overwrite what someone is typing. Note tags are now persisted (Convex `files.tags`), blank note titles are stored as "Untitled", and the share page renders legacy notes, math, and inline formatting. Agent note tools now write through `/api/v1/notes` instead of `/api/v1/files`. Release step: after the Convex deploy, run `npx convex run migrations/notesToMarkdown:run '{"dryRun": true}'` and then without `dryRun` to rewrite stored HTML notes. It's idempotent, keeps `updatedAt`, and skips rows edited mid-run.

- **Several agents per Slack channel**: any number of workspace agents can now be bound to the same Slack channel. People address one with `@Overlay <agent name> …`; follow-ups in a thread stay with the agent that owns it, a single-agent channel works as before, and an unaddressed mention in a shared channel gets a reply listing the agents. Each reply posts under the answering agent's name. Routing reads Slack's raw event text, because the SDK's normalized text renders the bot mention as "@Overlay", which would otherwise match an agent named Overlay. Bindings are now unique per channel and agent (Convex `surfaces/surfaces:listBindingsByChannel`; `findBindingByChannel` stays for older web deployments).

- **Agents on Slack (surfaces)**: a workspace can connect Slack and bind agents to channels from the agent editor's **Reachable on** section. Slack events arrive at `POST /api/v1/webhooks/slack` through the Chat SDK adapter (signature-verified, deduped), each Slack thread maps to one Overlay conversation, and the turn runs through the durable automation workflow with the agent's instructions, model, tools, and memory, billed to `surface:slack:<bindingId>` under the agent creator. Surface threads appear in Chats as read-only rooms tagged with the Slack icon. Uninstall and token-revocation events mark the connection `uninstalled`/`degraded`. This work was built on `staging` (phases 0–5) and later overwritten there; it is recovered here on the Convex-only backend, with Chat SDK state (installations, encrypted bot tokens, subscriptions, locks, dedupe keys) in a new Convex `surfaceChatState` table so serverless instances share it. Surfaces stay off until `SLACK_CLIENT_ID`, `SLACK_CLIENT_SECRET`, `SLACK_SIGNING_SECRET`, and `SLACK_ENCRYPTION_KEY` are set; production also needs the Convex schema deploy (`surfaceConnections`, `surfaceBindings`, `surfaceChatState`, conversation surface fields). Setup: `docs/develop/surfaces-slack-app.md`.

- **Full test suite in CI** (`npm run test:all`, `scripts/ci/run-all-tests.mjs`): every tracked `*.test.*` file now runs in the required Quality and Security Gates job, each with the runner it needs (tsx, vitest for Convex and unit suites, `node --test` for `.mjs`, and `npm test` for the published agent-host packages). Previously CI ran only route characterization and release-safety slices, and 43 failures had accumulated unseen. Stale tests were updated to intentional behavior changes, tests for features reverted in `e02a88843` are kept as `todo` with the revert SHA, and tests for removed Projects code were deleted.

- **Low-balance AI Gateway fallback**: when the global `AI_GATEWAY_API_KEY` credit balance drops below $5 (`AI_GATEWAY_LOW_CREDIT_THRESHOLD_USD` override), chat requests lead with free models — the OpenRouter free router (`openrouter/free`) first, then the quality-ordered free catalog — with the selected model and its paid fallbacks kept as a last resort. The balance is read via the gateway's `GET /v1/credits`, cached for 60s, and any `402` gateway response marks the key low until refresh. Every gateway-routed chat path gets the same protection — Act turns reorder their attempt list, and all other `getLanguageModel` callers (automations, workspace agents, notebook agent, titles, suggestions) resolve to a free model while credit is low. Convex memory extraction/profile compilation switch paid jobs to `openrouter/free` under the same threshold. `onlyAllowZdrModels` workspaces are never silently substituted onto a non-ZDR model, and BYOK keys are unaffected.

- **Agentic memory retrieval (M4)**: agents with the memory tool group now also get `search_messages` — a verbatim search over the raw conversation record for when distilled memories aren't enough (exact wording, dates, who said what). `search_memory` covers curated facts; `search_messages` covers what was literally said, and each hit arrives citation-ready (`YYYY-MM-DD · Speaker`). It inherits the same memory on/off and capability gates as `search_memory`, and legacy write-only memory grants pick it up automatically. The knowledge indexing path also accepts a trusted-internal flag so server-secret callers (benchmarks, internal backfills) bypass the per-user daily indexing-chunk cap, and a per-ingest reservation nonce so purge→re-ingest of identical content re-embeds instead of being idempotently skipped; ordinary user caps and idempotency are unchanged.
- **Memory write-path + read-path upgrade (M1+M2)**: extracted memories now carry lifecycle fields (`supersededBy`, `supersededAt`, `expiresAt`, `eventAt`, `visibility`) and are semantically deduplicated at write time — each candidate is embedded and compared against existing memories (auto-skip on near-identical hits, an LLM decision for `add`/`noop`/`update`/`supersede` in the ambiguous band), so repeated facts refresh `updatedAt` instead of duplicating rows. Tool writes through `POST /api/v1/memory` get the same dedup gate. On the read path, raw conversation messages are now indexed as searchable chunks (`sourceKind: 'message'`) alongside memory and file chunks — vector + lexical retrieval merges all three kinds with reciprocal rank fusion, memory results get a 30-day recency half-life (messages and files don't decay), and superseded/expired/other-owner memories are filtered out. A compiled per-owner `memoryProfiles` summary (identity, preferences, standing instructions, active projects, constraints) is generated after extraction writes and injected as `User profile:`/`Agent profile:` context, replacing the query-blind top-memories list when present. Conversation and message deletion purges the associated message chunks; a paginated backfill action covers pre-existing conversations.
- **Agent memory surface (M3)**: the Memories page member filter now includes workspace agents (labeled `· agent`), and `memberPrincipalId` on `GET /api/v1/memory` resolves agent principals to their memory owner id, so an agent's memory is viewable like any member's. The agent editor gains a "Memories" section (edit mode) showing a preview of what the agent remembers with per-row delete and a "View in Memories" link that deep-links the filtered page (`?owner=<principalId>`).
- **Memory consolidation surface (Stage 2, bench-gated)**: the `memories` table gains `inferred`/`derivedFrom` fields so a consolidation pass can write cross-memory inferences linked to their supporting facts. The bench harness runs the derivation (digest of recent stated memories → bounded LLM call → same dedup gate restricted to add/noop → `inferred` rows) behind `BENCH_CONSOLIDATION=1`; production wiring and cron scheduling stay off until the flagged runs show it helps.
- **Agent threads and nested agent resources**: workspace agents now own multiple chat threads (`conversations.agentId`) that nest under the agent in the sidebar instead of appearing in Chats, plus any automations drafted inside those threads (`automations.agentId`), which disappear from the standalone Automations page and run under the agent's instructions, tools, and environment. The sidebar gains Personal/Workspace/Archived tabs above New agent; expanding an agent lazy-loads its threads and automations. Threads are created via `POST /api/v1/agents/[agentId]/threads` (fresh thread every time — the legacy singleton DM is adopted as the main thread by `POST .../threads/resolve`), archived per-participant via `PATCH .../threads/[threadId]`, and deleted via `DELETE .../threads/[threadId]` — deletion of an agent's last surviving thread is refused (`AGENT_LAST_THREAD`) so an agent always keeps a main thread, derived as the oldest survivor. Archiving an agent preserves its thread participation so the Archived view renders full history; `POST .../restore` re-activates it, and agent-owned automations refuse to run (scheduled or manual) while their agent is archived. One-to-one DMs opened with an agent principal bind to the agent automatically and route to the agents surface. A two-phase migration (`convex/migrations/backfillAgentThreads.ts`, server-secret gated, paginated) stamps `conversations.agentId` on existing agent DMs then `automations.agentId` via `sourceConversationId`.
- **Agent gateway for in-box CLIs (metaharness v1)**: `POST /api/agent-gateway/{anthropic|openai}/v1/*` is a metered model proxy — `computer_exec` injects `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`/`OPENAI_BASE_URL` plus a short-lived scoped token (`OVERLAY_AGENT_GATEWAY_SECRET` or `INTERNAL_API_SECRET`) into every command, so agent CLIs inside a box (`claude`, `codex`, `opencode`, `pi`, `hermes`, …) authenticate through Overlay and never see a real provider key. Requests reserve budget before forwarding (real keys resolved from WorkOS Vault `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`/`OPENAI_API_KEY` with env fallback), SSE streams are tapped for provider-reported token usage, and spend finalizes through the existing reserve/finalize pipeline billed to `agent:<id>`. Unpriced models bill a conservative fallback (`OVERLAY_AGENT_GATEWAY_FALLBACK_USD`) rather than going unmetered.
- **Environment machines unify with computers**: an Overlay Cloud environment's sandbox is now also its bound agent's machine — computer tools (`computer_exec`, file read/write/list, `computer_open_url`) resolve the environment's box when no computer row exists, `POST /api/v1/agent-environments/{id}/desktop` issues a live desktop ticket on it, and Settings → Computers lists environment machines alongside computer rows. Agent creation skips provisioning a second computer when the agent binds to a managed environment.
- **SQL read mirror for self-hosted deployments** (`examples/customer-deployment/mirror/` + `docker-compose.mirror.yml`): a sidecar that streams the supported Data Sync endpoint (`POST /api/v1/data/sync`) and materializes every Convex table as `mirror.<table>(id, creation_time, ts, doc jsonb, synced_at)` in customer-controlled Postgres — the supported relational surface for BI tools and data lakes (never query Convex's internal `documents`/`indexes`). Durable opaque cursor in `mirror._meta` with per-page transactions, so restarts resume and mid-page crashes replay idempotently; `truncates` events `TRUNCATE` mirror tables in-transaction so bulk replaces can't drift it; component tables prefixed (`stripe_*`); nanosecond `ts` preserved losslessly; optional `MIRROR_TABLES` allowlist and `MIRROR_ADMIN_URL` auto-provisioning. Verified live against a deployed stack (115 tables snapshot, live inserts/replaces replicating in seconds). `npm run test:mirror` runs unit + integration suites (integration needs `MIRROR_TEST_DATABASE_URL`).

### Changed

- **Web search now routes through ELO + TinyFish instead of the Vercel AI Gateway tool hop**: `web_search` (ELO → Tavily; quick lookups, news, batched queries, domain/recency filters), `deep_search` (ELO → Parallel `advanced` mode; long excerpts, source policy, effort knob), and `web_fetch` (TinyFish Fetch API; clean markdown for up to 10 known URLs) replace `perplexity_search`/`parallel_search`. Executors are direct `fetch` calls — the inner proxy-model `generateText` hop (and its token cost, ~5k tokens/search) is deleted, cutting deep-search latency ~6× (12.4s → ~2.1s in benchmark). Provider cost to Overlay is ~$0; user-facing metering stays at $0.005/provider call. Exact-entity lookups still auto-filter and deep-augment inside `web_search`. `ELO_API_KEY` and `TINYFISH_API_KEY` resolve through WorkOS Vault with env fallback; free tier keeps gated stubs; legacy `perplexity_search`/`parallel_search` names remain mapped for transcript rendering and leaked-call repair. Enterprise config: `providers.webSearch.provider` now defaults to `elo` (`ai-gateway`, `perplexity`, `tavily` declarations fail validation with guidance; `none` unchanged).
- **Default chat model is now GLM 5.3 Flash** (`z-ai/glm-5.3-flash`, upstream `zai/glm-5.3-flash` on the AI Gateway) — `PAID_TIER_DEFAULT_MODEL_ID` moved off `moonshotai/kimi-k2.6`, which remains selectable; the new model joins the curated catalog and quality-priority ordering above GLM 5.1. Free-tier "Auto" is unchanged.
- **Sidebar agent rows no longer double-highlight**: when an agent thread is selected, only the thread row shows the active state — the parent agent row highlights only on the bare agent view.
- **Memory retrieval is now date-aware and corroboration-aware**: questions naming a time window ("last week", "in March", "on Tuesday") get a temporal candidate list fused into hybrid search — message and memory chunks carry an effective `eventAt` (the fact's event date when the extractor knows it, else write time) indexed per owner, so dated evidence surfaces even when vector/lexical blends miss it. Repeatedly confirmed memories rank higher: dedup "seen this before" hits now increment a `sourceCount` on the memory row and its chunks, applying a log-scaled lift. `hybridSearch` also accepts `includeProvenance` — memory hits can carry up to 2 verbatim message chunks from the source turn (`knowledgeChunks.turnId` links them) so a distilled fact arrives with the raw wording behind it.
- **Managed HarnessAgents are grandfathered**: `OVERLAY_MANAGED_HARNESS_ROLLOUT_STAGE` now gates *creation* only — turn dispatch uses a separate run gate (feature flag + workspace policy) so existing harness agents keep running with the stage at `off`. `upsertBinding` refuses new (agent, environment) harness pairs when creation is gated but always allows re-saving an existing pair; the picker returns bound harnesses as `legacy: true` entries (404 only when none exist); the editor filters legacy entries out of the create-mode selector and rebinds grandfathered agents on their existing environment instead of dropping the binding on save. The `managedHarnessAgents` feature flag remains the emergency kill switch for the whole surface.
- **Overlay Cloud agent environments default to boat boxes** (`OVERLAY_MANAGED_SANDBOX_PROVIDER` now defaults to `box` when `BOX_API_KEY` is set; `vercel`/`daytona` remain selectable). Persistent agent hosts run on the provider's own dollar-accurate usage accounting — `GET /boxes/{id}/usage` returns multiplier-adjusted billable seconds and list-price `dollars`, which the lease meter debits as deltas (provider-reported spend wins over the seconds rate card; the card is only a fallback when the endpoint omits dollars, tunable via `OVERLAY_BOX_SECONDS_PER_DOLLAR`). Fleet-level alerting: when any box lease is active, each meter sweep probes `GET /limits` and warns when `canStart` is false or the account's machine-second balance drops under `OVERLAY_BOX_LOW_REMAINING_SECONDS` (2h default). Boxes have no custom-image or egress-allowlist primitives, so hosts bootstrap via `npx @layernorm/overlay-agent-host` (`OVERLAY_AGENT_HOST_PACKAGE`) and run `allow_all` — safe because the box holds nothing sensitive (single-use enrollment code; model keys stay server-side behind the gateway). Harness-mode provisioning still requires an egress-allowlist provider and rejects `box` loudly.
- **Ephemeral sandbox execution is pinned to its own provider** (`OVERLAY_EXEC_SANDBOX_PROVIDER`, default `vercel`) so chat/automation `sandbox.run` calls can never follow the persistent-agent provider to a provider without deny-all egress. The run handler now `stop()`s, reads final usage, finalizes billing, **and deletes the sandbox** in `finally` — a delete failure is logged and swept rather than masking the run result. The reconcile route (already cron-driven every minute) additionally runs an orphan sweep (`ephemeral-sweeper.ts`) that deletes any `overlay-sandbox-*`-prefixed non-persistent Vercel sandbox older than `OVERLAY_EPHEMERAL_SANDBOX_STALE_MS` (15m default), covering sandboxes orphaned by aborted requests; sweep results are reported in the reconcile response.

### Fixed

- **Production dependency audit is green again**: new `undici` and `brace-expansion` advisories (including a TLS certificate-validation bypass) failed the CI audit. The Workflow runtime's pinned `undici` override moves to 7.29.1 and `brace-expansion` to 5.0.12. `npm run security:audit` now runs `scripts/ci/security-audit.mjs`, which still fails on any high or critical advisory except three reviewed, expiring (2026-10-14) exceptions for install paths overrides cannot reach: `pi-coding-agent`'s shrinkwrapped `undici`/`brace-expansion` (fixed by the pending `@ai-sdk/harness` family bump) and `eve`'s pinned `undici` (agent-host CLI only, not the web runtime).

- **Slack replies render formatting**: agent replies are standard Markdown, which Slack showed literally (`**pong**`). Replies now go out as Slack's native `markdown_text` (the same path the Chat SDK uses); replies over Slack's 12,000-character `markdown_text` limit fall back to converted Slack mrkdwn.

- **Route authorization inventory is complete**: 38 API route methods (computers, agent threads, agent bundles, workspace billing, provider connections, file ingest jobs, Slack import, mention search, sandbox run, and others) were missing from `authorization-route-policy.ts`; each now records its actual access model, and the two internal-secret callbacks are marked `internal`.
- **Environment desktop streams require a workspace owner or admin**: `POST /api/v1/agent-environments/{id}/desktop` issued live desktop tickets to any workspace member; it now carries the same manager gate as the other environment operations.
- **Withheld connectors are enforced on the integrations route**: connectors an administrator withholds in the catalog were already filtered out of chat and automation turns, but still appeared in the integrations search and connected lists and could be connected. They are now hidden there and a direct connect returns `403`. Batched catalog filtering also resolves the capability, owner, and wildcard grants once per list instead of once per resource.

- **User-facing lifecycle emails redesigned to the Overlay brand**: all eight transactional templates (`src/shared/email/lifecycle-email.ts`) now render the standardized metal orb + serif wordmark lockup, a white card on the paper background, serif headings, sentence-case copy, per-event feature cues (quick-start rows on welcome, a status pill on billing changes, a run card naming the automation on failures, workspace/chat cards on invites, mentions, and DMs), and black pill CTAs that deep-link into the app. Notification-type emails (mention, DM, automation failure) gain a "Manage email preferences" link; footers state why each email was sent. `public/assets/overlay-logo.png` is regenerated to the current mark for email clients that strip SVG, and the `convex/email/deliveryRunner.ts` allowlist now accepts `workspace.mention` and `workspace.dm_received` so those emails actually deliver instead of dead-lettering. Automation lifecycle events carry `automationName` so failure emails name the automation.

- **Agent threads auto-name on the first message**: one-to-one agent threads (including the main thread) generate a title from the first human message, matching personal chats — the sidebar thread row swaps the placeholder title for the generated one instead of staying "Untitled agent".
- **Mention chips no longer render "@ @"**: mention pills in transcripts dropped the literal `@` prefix (the type icon already signals a mention), and agent mentions now show the agent's creature avatar instead of the generic glyph.
- **Automations no longer hijack the conversation they were created in**: `sourceConversationId` is now provenance only — never a run target, never hidden from Chats, never deleted with the automation. Every automation executes in its own owned thread (`conversationId`, `isAutomation`), created on first run or on the first message in the automation surface, and every run appends to it. Previously, creating an automation inside a chat/agent thread made runs write into that thread, hid it from Chats, and (for drafts) deleted it with the automation — which also bounced an open view back to the chat list. Automation-run messages now carry the automation's name as the author instead of "Someone". A one-off migration (`convex/migrations/backfillAutomationConversations.ts`, server-secret gated, paginated) clears `conversationId` values that point at non-`isAutomation` conversations, restoring hijacked threads to their owners.
- `bin/convex-bootstrap.sh` rejected admin keys for non-default `INSTANCE_NAME` — the key prefix is `<instance-name>|`, not hardcoded `convex-self-hosted|`. Also `convex.env` (which holds real secrets) is now gitignored, as is `examples/*/node_modules/`.
- **Orphaned knowledge chunks could stay retrievable after their source was deleted**: deleting a chat turn (`deleteTurn`) or tearing down an archived workspace hard-deleted `conversationMessages` rows without purging their `knowledgeChunks`/`knowledgeChunkEmbeddings`, so verbatim fragments of deleted messages kept surfacing in memory search. Both paths now purge chunks atomically in the same mutation via a shared `deleteChunksForSource` helper, and a daily 04:30 UTC cron (`sweepOrphanedChunks`, plus a server-secret `sweepOrphanedKnowledgeChunks` action for manual remediation) scans the table and deletes any remaining provable orphans — chunks whose source row is gone or whose memory is tombstoned; unverifiable sources are conservatively kept.

### Removed

- **Free tier is now just the Auto router**: Step 3.5 Flash (`stepfun-ai/step-3.5-flash`) and Nemotron 3 Super 120B (`openrouter/nvidia/nemotron-3-super-120b-a12b:free`) are removed from the free model list — free-tier chat is routed entirely through `openrouter/free` ("Free Router"). Saved preferences pointing at the retired ids resolve to the free router via the legacy-model alias map, and the dedicated NVIDIA NIM fallback path (`nvidia-nim-openai.ts`, low-balance NIM fallback) is deleted with them. Users BYOK-ing their own NVIDIA NIM endpoint are unaffected.

- **Bespoke Postgres application-data path**: Overlay is now Convex-only for application data. Deleted the `OVERLAY_PROVIDER_DATABASE` selector, all `Postgres*` repositories, the Drizzle app-data schema/migrations (`migrations/`, `drizzle.app-data.config.ts`), the app-data worker/scheduler (`scripts/db/app-data-worker.ts`, `app-db:*` npm scripts, `docker/docker-compose.app-data.yml`), the Postgres contract/parity suites, and the Postgres-only Next.js Stripe webhook (`/api/webhooks/stripe` — the `@convex-dev/stripe` component endpoint is the single webhook). Client-side Postgres long-polling was removed from chat/collaboration providers (Convex subscriptions only). Postgres remains in exactly two infrastructure roles: Better Auth's separate `BETTER_AUTH_DATABASE_URL` database (`pg` driver + `better-auth:db:*` scripts + compose service unchanged), and `@workflow/world-postgres` for durable workflow runs. Self-hosted/enterprise deployments run the official `convex-backend` (see `examples/customer-deployment/`); its persistence engine may itself be Postgres, which is a Convex-backend concern, not an application-data provider. `drizzle-orm`/`drizzle-kit` dependencies removed; `pg` retained for Better Auth.

### Changed

- Enterprise runtime packaging follows the Convex-only topology: `docker/Dockerfile.enterprise-runtime` no longer bundles the app-data worker and now accepts the `NEXT_PUBLIC_CONVEX_URL` build arg (Next.js inlines it at build time, so self-hosted images must be built knowing the customer's Convex URL); `deploy/ec2/docker-compose.yml` drops the worker/scheduler/app-data-migration services, adds the official `convex-backend` service, and runs the web container on host networking so one loopback Convex URL serves both browser (SSH tunnel) and server traffic.

### Changed

- Branch workflow: `main` and `staging` no longer require parity — either may be ahead, and post-merge staging realignment/host commits now happen only on explicit owner request rather than as bookkeeping (documented in `AGENTS.md` and `docs/develop/`).
- **Landing page polish and docs restyle**: nav links now center on the page (not just within the bar), headline reads "The control plane for your workspace", Windsurf is removed from the agent visuals and Devin and Hermes join the roster, the section visuals no longer sit inside gray boxes, and the hero demo stays intact at narrow widths (horizontal scroll with the agents panel collapsing below 560px). The Mintlify docs match the landing design language — Libre Baskerville headings, the grayscale palette with a light/dark theme toggle, the metal-orb lockup, and grouped sidebar navigation for the Desktop, Develop, and Deploy & Operate sections.
- Redesigned the auth screens (`/auth/sign-in`, `/auth/sign-up`, `/auth/forgot-password`, `/auth/reset-password`) around a split layout — a quiet brand panel with the orb mark and tagline on desktop, a compact brand mark above the form on mobile — replacing the floating card. Auth options and the SSO capability flag are now resolved server-side during page render instead of two client fetches (`/api/auth/options`, `/api/v1/capabilities`), so the full form paints on first load rather than popping in after the heading. Sign-up's explicit Terms/Privacy toggle is now standard implicit-consent fine print ("By continuing, you agree to…"); the legal acceptance payload with document versions is still recorded identically at click time. The column scrolls safely when the form exceeds viewport height. The brand mark renders the canonical inline SVG `OverlayMark` at `MARKETING_LOGO_SIZE` — identical to the landing header — instead of the raster `overlay-logo.png`, and every auth loading surface (`auth/loading.tsx` plus the `LandingAuthBoundary`/reset-password Suspense fallbacks) now renders a shared `AuthPageSkeleton` that mirrors the split layout's real component positions rather than a top-left spinner.

### Fixed

- Managed sandboxes no longer burn provider budget while idle. Vercel sandboxes have no native idle-stop — they run until the 24-hour `timeout` — so every managed lease provisioned ~96 GB-hours/day of memory whether or not the agent was used. The meter now enforces the lease's idle window itself: a sandbox with no acquire/settle activity for `idleTimeoutMs` (15 minutes) is stopped while the lease stays resumable, turn-slice acquires heartbeat `usage.lastActiveAt` so working sandboxes never cool mid-turn, and `running` leases past their 24-hour `reservedUntil` are reaped instead of lingering forever.

- The reconciliation sweep no longer emits recurring `offline_environment` warnings for managed (`overlay_cloud`) environments. Those environments have no heartbeat host — they read offline whenever their sandbox is idle-stopped, which is normal — so the BYO-oriented stale-environment pass now skips them in both the Convex and Postgres implementations. Genuinely stale BYO environments still alert.

### Changed

- Managed sandbox billing now meters the lease, not the turn. Agent-environment turns previously reserved a worst-case 24-hour sandbox cost (~$15) at dispatch, which declined turns outright on wallets that could have run for hours. `ManagedAgentSandboxBilling.reserve` now takes only a ~$1.50 bootstrap hold (low-balance floor plus a short coverage window), and a per-lease meter debits actual provider usage once a minute: each tick reads `instance.usage()`, diffs it against a cursor persisted on the lease (`usage.meteredUsage`/`meterVersion`), and commits the charge plus cursor atomically so retried or concurrent ticks can never double-charge. When the payer's remaining balance falls under $1 (`OVERLAY_SANDBOX_LOW_BALANCE_CUTOFF_CENTS`) or a tick debit is declined, the lease flips to `stopping`, in-flight commands and queued/running turns are cancelled with a `usage_limited` terminal error, and the provider sandbox is deleted by a reaper pass that also retries `cleanup_failed` leases with backoff — closing the gap where `stopping` leases were never reaped. Turn settle is now just the final meter tick plus bootstrap-hold release, and the missing `agentSandboxSettlements` marker on managed-harness settles logs at info instead of throwing `MANAGED_SANDBOX_SETTLEMENT_MARKER_MISSING` into reconciliation. The meter runs from the BFF `agent-environments/operations/reconcile` route (already called by the Convex cron) and the Postgres `app-data.maintenance` job; `OVERLAY_SANDBOX_METER_DISABLED=true` disables it. Provider usage reads never resume an idle-stopped sandbox (`reconnect(reference, {resume:false})` on every runtime adapter), and because providers such as Vercel publish cumulative counters only once a session stops, a running sandbox bills the elapsed window against a virtual wall-time cursor while reap/reset paths stop the instance, take one final meter read, then delete — so session usage is captured rather than lost at teardown. The 24-hour runtime hard limit, free-tier shape (30-minute cap, zero egress), and the standalone sandbox route's worst-case reservation are unchanged.

### Added

- Self-hosted Convex backend packaging for enterprise deployments (`examples/customer-deployment/docker-compose.convex.yml` + `convex.env.example` + `bin/convex-bootstrap.sh`, with the operations runbook at `docs/deploy-operate/self-hosted-convex.mdx`). The stack runs the official open-source Convex backend on PostgreSQL persistence and S3-compatible object storage with full feature parity to Convex Cloud — validated end-to-end against Overlay's real code path (full `convex/` deploy, 18 crons, storage upload/serve round trip, WorkOS/HS256 auth, AI Gateway chat). The architecture decision and proof results are recorded in `docs/develop/convex-only-self-hosting.md`.

- Managed harness providers, approvals, and BYOK (phase 4 of `docs/plans/MANAGED_HARNESS_AGENTS_PLAN.md`): Daytona is now a selectable managed-sandbox provider (`DAYTONA_API_KEY`; provisioned via a new `createOverlaySandboxProvider` bridge in `@overlay/sandbox-runtime` that maps `SandboxInstance` onto the AI SDK's `HarnessV1SandboxProvider`, with private preview links authenticated through `SandboxPort.headers`). Box remains deliberately unavailable for harnesses — it lacks the egress allowlist and credential-forwarding guarantees required to keep customer keys out of the sandbox. Approval-gated tool calls now park the durable workflow on a hook (up to 20 cycles per turn): the run row flips to `waiting_for_approval`, the existing approval card renders each requested tool, and resolving it resumes the workflow with synthesized `tool-approval-response` messages — no fallback to ACP. Managed harnesses can also run on a workspace member's own provider connection: the editor's **Model access** picker offers connections compatible with the harness's `byokProviders` (Vercel AI Gateway for Claude Code/Codex/OpenCode/Pi, OpenRouter for Hermes), the binding records `modelBilling:'byok'` + the connection and its owner, and the turn slice reads the key from the credential vault at execution time — the key never enters binding config, workflow state, or the Daytona sandbox environment.
