BYOK is restricted to the Vercel provider, whose request transformations inject the key at the sandbox boundary; other providers fail closed.

- Managed harness creation UX (phase 3 of `docs/plans/MANAGED_HARNESS_AGENTS_PLAN.md`): the agent editor's **Hosted on Overlay Cloud** branch now offers a runtime picker — the native Overlay agent plus every managed HarnessAgent this workspace may run (Claude Code, Codex, OpenCode, Pi, Hermes) — served by `GET /api/v1/agent-environments/managed` so the feature flag, `OVERLAY_MANAGED_HARNESS_ROLLOUT_STAGE`, workspace `allowedAgentHarnesses` policy, and Vercel Sandbox credentials all gate server-side (404 leaves Overlay as the only hosted runtime). Picking a managed runtime swaps the model/tool-group section for harness config — a per-harness model select (Overlay-funded; the harness-native alias lands on the binding's `adapterConfig.model` while the priced gateway model becomes `agent.modelId`), a fixed Vercel Sandbox provider row, and `/workspace`. Saving creates the agent, provisions the sandbox environment, then upserts the `protocolAdapter:'harness'` binding — a mid-flow failure lands on the edit page so retry never duplicates the agent. Editing a managed agent shows its runtime, live sandbox status, and a manager-gated **Reset session** action (`POST .../reset-harness` — clears `agentHarnessSessions` and destroys the sandbox; the next message rebuilds both). Switching runtimes provisions a fresh environment and resets the detached one; dispatch re-checks availability so a retired harness fails closed rather than turning on a stale binding. BYO agents keep the existing harness/environment/connect flow unchanged.

- Managed harness dispatch + durable turns (phase 2 of `docs/plans/MANAGED_HARNESS_AGENTS_PLAN.md`): messages to a `protocolAdapter:'harness'` binding now run through `managedHarnessAgentTurnWorkflow` instead of the ACP command queue. Each workflow step executes one time-sliced `@ai-sdk/workflow-harness` turn inside the lease's Vercel Sandbox — reconnecting or recreating it from the lease record — while a transcript writable projects stream chunks into the same generating reply row hosted agents use. `agentHarnessSessions.resumeState` carries the native session across user turns, and cancellation destroys the session, clears resume state, and settles sandbox billing. v1 runs built-in tools under `allow-all` inside the sandbox boundary with elicitation disabled; interactive tool approvals are a later phase.

- Managed harness provisioning (phase 1 of `docs/plans/MANAGED_HARNESS_AGENTS_PLAN.md`): `POST /api/v1/agent-environments/managed` now accepts `{mode:'harness', harnessId, provider?}` — it creates the Vercel sandbox (bridge port + allowlist egress with denied private/metadata CIDRs), writes an already-approved `overlay_cloud` environment with a fixed `/workspace` grant, and records the lease — no enrollment ceremony or Agent Host image. Bindings on those environments write `protocolAdapter:'harness'` (`AGENT_PROTOCOL_ADAPTERS` and `WORKSPACE_AGENT_HARNESSES` extended), and a new durable `agentHarnessSessions` store (Convex table + Postgres migration 0076 + both repositories) persists per-binding+conversation HarnessAgent `resumeState`. Live API verification is pending `VERCEL_*` env credentials — provider selection fails closed without them.

- Managed harness foundation (phase 0 of `docs/plans/MANAGED_HARNESS_AGENTS_PLAN.md`): Overlay Cloud can now describe and load AI SDK HarnessAgent runtimes — a shared `src/shared/agents/harness-catalog.ts` catalog + `MANAGED_HARNESS_IDS` contract cover Claude Code, Codex, OpenCode, Pi, and Hermes (via generic ACP), and `src/server/agents/harnesses/` resolves each id to its adapter with lazy dynamic imports (`registry.ts`) and selects the sandbox provider (`sandbox-providers.ts`, Vercel-only in v1; Box/Daytona arrive via the Phase 4 bridge). No routes or UI yet. A live smoke run verified a `claude-code` HarnessAgent bootstraps and streams inside a real Vercel Sandbox. Dependency floor: `ai` pinned to `7.0.93` with `@ai-sdk/react@4.0.96`, `@ai-sdk/workflow@2.0.24`, and `@ai-sdk/otel@1.0.93` moved to the same lockstep batch to keep one deduped `ai`/`provider-utils` tree.

- New marketing routes `/blog`, `/changelog`, and `/self-hosting` (shared `MarketingPlaceholderPage` scaffold) so every navbar and footer destination resolves to a real page instead of 404ing.

- Agent DMs now surface the agent's computer in the conversation header: when a one-to-one agent has a usable bound machine (`ready` or `stopped`), a subtle **Desktop** button with a status dot (green running, amber stopped) issues a desktop stream ticket and opens the live view in a new tab — stopped machines resume on ticket issue automatically. Hidden when the `computers` capability is off or the agent has no machine.
- The agent editor's **Computer** tool toggle now *is* the computer toggle: enabling it grants the `computer_*` tools and shows the size picker underneath, and saving provisions the machine; an existing machine renders its card (Open desktop, Stop/Start, Delete) under the row, and toggling off warns that saving destroys the disk. A bound machine auto-marks the group on load so the two can never disagree, and deleting a machine immediately persists the grant removal — agents can no longer be left with computer tools but no computer.
- The agent editor's dialog-vs-side-panel presentation now persists in localStorage — the settings gear reopens whichever shape it was left in instead of always opening the dialog.
- The agent editor dialog and docked panel now render one uniform surface — the form body no longer paints a darker background inside the elevated panel frame.
- Agent identity edits now preview live: typing a name or picking a shape/color updates the sidebar roster row, the conversation header, and the editor's own title before saving; cancelling or closing clears the preview.

- Cancelling the agent editor on a never-saved agent now abandons creation — the agent is archived, its greeting DM is removed, and the view returns to the most recently used agent (or the agents index). Saving or explicitly archiving clears the abandon marker first, so a saved agent is never deleted by a later close.
- Saving the agent editor now closes the dialog and docked panel instead of leaving it open on a saved flash; the standalone editor page still stays open.
- New agents now default to every tool group except **Computer** — agents are fully capable out of the box, while the computer group stays opt-in because enabling it provisions a persistent paid machine on save.
- Generating agent responses in DMs now show a stop button to the left of the reaction control — it cancels just that run (`POST /api/v1/conversations/stop` with the message id), finalizes the partial reply with an interrupted marker, and disappears once the message settles.

- Automation runs now execute their agent loop as a durable in-workflow agent loop (`workflows/automation-agent-turn.ts` + `automations/automation-turn-runner.ts`) instead of the durable workflow shelling out to `/api/v1/conversations/act` over self-HTTP. Model calls, tool calls, message persistence, and billing run as individually durable and retryable workflow steps, so a process restart resumes at the failing step rather than losing the entire turn. Entitlement gating, catalog authorization, workflow-step metering, usage reservations, and run-status settlement are all re-evaluated inside steps under service auth; approval-gated tools are auto-denied with an explicit reason since unattended runs have no approval surface. The loop is manual rather than the SDK's `WorkflowAgent` specifically so the model resolves via `getLanguageModel` inside the step — **all providers work durably**, including BYOK keys (fetched from the vault at call time, never persisted in the workflow log), OpenRouter, and NVIDIA NIM.

- Computer agent tools (wiring phase 5.5): a new opt-in **Computer** tool group lets agents drive their bound persistent desktop — `computer_exec` (shell commands, 5m cap), `computer_read_file`/`computer_write_file`/`computer_list_files`, and `computer_open_url` (opens in the machine's real browser; the desktop stream ticket never enters the transcript — users watch via Open desktop). Tools resolve the executing owner's bound computer through `ComputerService` — never a model-supplied id — resume stopped machines, and are withheld when the `computers` capability is off. The agent editor's provisioned computer row also gained inline Stop/Start and Delete (confirm) controls, and the Computer tool toggle hides when the capability is off.

- Settled assistant turns now fold all work — tool calls, reasoning, and browser sessions — behind one expandable **"Worked for {duration} — {n} tool calls"** row at the top of the reply, so the collapsed message reads as uninterrupted text. The row is a mode toggle, not a container: expanding it switches the message to the raw timeline with every work segment back at its true position between the texts. Deliverables (draft cards, gated callouts, generated files/UI) stay inline in both modes, and streaming still shows every step live in order. Implemented via a shared `AssistantSegmentItem` renderer + `planAssistantWorkCollapse`, deduplicating the transcript/room render paths.

- Agent editor Computer section (wiring phase 5): Overlay agents can now own a persistent cloud desktop from their editor — an enable toggle plus size picker (small/default/large) provisions the computer right after the agent is created, edit mode shows the bound machine with an Open desktop action (ready and stopped computers), and turning the toggle off deletes the computer behind a disk-destruction confirm. The section only appears for Overlay agents when the `computers` capability is on; agent machines remain manageable under Settings → Computers.

- Computers settings UI (wiring phase 4): Settings → Computers lists every computer the caller can see — personal computers, workspace agents' machines, and creator-only agents' machines for their creator — with status, size, and last-active; actions cover create (personal), open-desktop (ticket → new tab), stop/start, and delete. The section only appears when the `computers` capability is on.

- Computers provider registry (wiring phase 3): `OVERLAY_COMPUTER_PROVIDER` (default `box`) + an admin-scoped `BOX_API_KEY` now resolve a cached `BoxSandboxRuntime` per computer row, and `features.computers` / `OVERLAY_FEATURE_COMPUTERS` gates the `computers` capability. The capability reports on only when the flag is set AND the configured provider resolves, so self-host deployments without credentials keep the routes hidden (403 `capability_disabled`); provisioning now reaches the provider when both are configured. Still no UI — the Settings section lands in phase 4.

- Computers API surface (wiring phase 2): seven authenticated `/api/v1/computers` routes — list, provision (idempotent per owner), get, destroy, desktop-ticket (bearer-secret response excluded from idempotency persistence), stop, start — plus `computerService` on the server context and a typed `ComputersClient` in `@overlay/api-client`. All routes are gated behind the new `computers` capability, which stays off until the provider-registry phase wires `features.computers` + `BOX_API_KEY`; provision then fails closed as `provider_unavailable` until a runtime is configured.

- Computers (persistent cloud desktops) storage foundation landed: shared `Computer` types in `@overlay/workspace-contracts`, a `ComputerRepository` port implemented on both backends — Postgres migration `0075_computers` (unique `(workspace_id, owner_type, owner_id)` owner binding, size/status checks, `created_by` → `users` cascade) plus the matching Convex `computers` table and server-secret-gated handlers — registered in `AppDataRepositories` and the parity matrix (`computers` domain). Also fixes migration `0072_project_archival` to add `archived_at` with `IF NOT EXISTS` (it collided with `0022`'s guarded add on replay). No API routes or UI yet — those follow in wiring phases 2–4.

- Entry routing is agent-first: signed-out visitors to the root land on the marketing home, while signed-in users go straight to their agents. Try Overlay and all marketing CTAs open the agents surface (previously chats), and the demo shell now opens on a showcase agent conversation instead of the chat demo — the retired tile directory is deleted. Requires the Convex `avatarShape` validators from the creature release on deploy.

- Agent avatars are now anthropomorphic creatures instead of metallic orbs: a solid-color body in one of eight shapes (circle, blob, squircle, pill, triangle, hexagon, cloud, droplet) with blinking eyes that go dark-on-light and light-on-dark automatically. Shape and color are chosen in the agent editor's Identity section (live creature preview, shape grid, color grid) and persist per agent across the sidebar roster, directory tiles, DM headers, and message avatars. Opening the Agents page now resolves the most recently used agent's conversation directly behind a loading indicator — the blank intermediate page only appears when the workspace has no agents. Requires Postgres migration `0074_agent_avatar_shape` (nullable column, circle default) and the additive Convex schema field on deploy.

- Marketing and auth pages now render outside the application shell: home, manifesto, pricing, legal pages, download, and sign-in/sign-up/recovery open on a standalone marketing frame (navbar + content + footer) with no sidebar, no session resolution, and no demo state. The old `/app/home`, `/app/manifesto`, and `/app/pricing` demo addresses redirect to their canonical pages, and the sidebar showcase links point at the top-level routes. Docs were already served outside the shell via the Mintlify proxy.

- The Overlay logo is now a resizable SVG orb component (`OverlayMark`) instead of a PNG: sidebar, marketing nav/footer/showcase, sign-in popover, notebook, and loaders render it crisply at any size. Agents are orbs too (`AgentOrb`): the Overlay master agent renders as the grey metallic mark while every other agent tints the same sphere from its avatar color — in the sidebar roster, directory tiles, DM headers, message avatars (animated while streaming), and the editor, whose color picker now previews orb colorways. Lifecycle emails and favicons intentionally keep the PNG raster.
- Offline Bring-Your-Own-Agent environments now explain themselves in Settings → Environments: why the machine went quiet (asleep or connector closed), that Overlay cannot wake it remotely, and — on macOS — a one-click copy of the persistent-service install command so the connector survives Terminal restarts and logins. The enrollment setup prompt now nudges toward the persistent service for the same reason.
- Workspace agents now have an access mode: **Only me** (`visibility: 'creator'`) or **Everyone in workspace** (`visibility: 'workspace'`, the default). Creator-only agents are hidden from the agents directory, workspace search, and direct reads for everyone but their creator (reported as not found, so their existence does not leak); only the creator can edit them, while workspace managers keep archive access as a safety valve. The agent editor has a matching Access control and directory tiles show an "Only me" badge. Backed by Postgres migration `0073_agent_visibility` (nullable column; existing agents stay workspace-visible) with no Convex migration needed.
- Creator-only agents are now also gated on every invocation path: DMs with one by anyone but its creator return 404, @-mentions of one by non-creators silently produce no agent run (including in pre-existing DMs after an Everyone → Only me flip), and new creator-only agents no longer auto-join public channels. Flipping Only me → Everyone grants no retroactive channel joins.
- Agent creation and editing moved from a dialog to dedicated full-page routes (`/app/agents/new` and `/app/agents/:id`) with Identity, Behavior, Access, Connection, and Danger-zone sections, a sticky save bar, and a post-create "Say hello" handoff that opens a DM with the new agent. Directory tiles now attribute each agent to its creator ("by {name}").
- Added the chat-platform bot seam: `WorkspaceGovernanceService.resolvePlatformActor` maps a linked Slack/Teams user to its workspace principal (manager-gated linking, uniform not-found for unmapped identities), and the new `PlatformAgentAccess` routes bot list/DM requests through the same visibility-enforced services first-party clients use. No new API routes; the Chat SDK bot processes, platform OAuth, and install storage remain future work.

- Connected agents that advertise slash commands over ACP now power a composer slash menu in their DMs: the Agent Host forwards `available_commands_update`, the transcript stores it as a stable part, and typing `/` lists the agent's commands with descriptions. Agents that do not advertise commands are unaffected; activating this in production requires the next Agent Host package release.

### Changed

- Removed the Projects and Knowledge Bases features end to end: the `/app/projects` and `/app/knowledge` routes, sidebar/global-search/mention entries, `features.projects`/`capabilities.projects` config flags, `knowledge.*`/`projects.*`/`governance.*` authorization capabilities, the `/api/v1/projects*` and `/api/v1/knowledge-bases*` route families (including admin knowledge-bases defaults and admin governance policies/reviews/export), `src/server/projects/`, `src/server/knowledge-bases/`, the admin `GovernanceService`, the matching Convex domain folders, the `@overlay/modules-react` projects export, and every `projectId`/`knowledgeBaseId` thread through services, repositories, stores, and tests. The `projects`, `knowledgeBases`, `projectKnowledgeBases`, `governancePolicies`, and `governanceAccessReviews` tables — plus the dormant `projectId` columns on files/conversations/mcpServers/skills/memories — stay in both schemas so existing rows remain readable; queries now simply filter them out. Memories, knowledge search (`/api/v1/knowledge/search` → `files.read`), file indexing, the Files page, and workspace governance are unchanged.

- The `/home` landing page was rebuilt into a Linear-style product showcase: the hero pairs the control-panel pitch with a floating field of Overlay creature agents and bring-your-own harness pills, followed by a 16:9 recreation of the real app shell — the actual icon rail, agents secondary panel (New agent, search, creature rows, YOUR AGENTS section), and a live agent DM with a working composer, typing indicator, "Worked for {duration}, called N tools" row, and clickable rail panels. Below it: a lowercase-serif section system with SVG figures, an agents roster table, layered platform cards (Slack, iMessage, Telegram), a computer desktop mock, an automations card, governance controls, and logo clouds for supported agent harnesses and model providers (svgl.app assets with light/dark variants). Agent avatars render the real `Creature` component — `Creature.module.css` now also adapts under `data-landing-theme="dark"`. Navigation moved off section anchors to substantive destinations (Product, Docs, Blog, Pricing, Changelog, Self-hosting, GitHub, light/dark toggle, Get Started), every "Try Overlay" is now "Get Started", and the footer gained a column grid linking all pages — including the previously orphaned `/dpa`, `/subprocessors`, `/refunds`, `/commercial-license`, `/dmca`, `/download`, and `/manifesto`.

- The `/home` landing page now leads with the agent control-panel story — "The control panel for your AI workforce" — with a new Agents-directory product demo (Overlay agents beside bring-your-own harnesses like Codex, Claude Code, and Hermes, each showing where it's reachable) and new sections for the create/deploy/manage loop, bring-your-own agents, connecting agents to platforms like Slack, Telegram, and iMessage, and the complete-solo / optional-multiplayer model. The navbar's dead `open-source` anchor was replaced by real `agents`, `platforms`, and `organizations` section anchors.

- The Overlay orb mark moved into `@overlay/ui` (`components/orb/Orb.tsx` + `ovorb-*` styles, loaded app-wide via `globals.css`), with `src/components/orb/Orb.tsx` kept as a re-export shim. Every raster-logo consumer in the chat surface — tool-call rails, the reasoning row, the streaming tail marker, exchange loading states, and the notebook agent panel/editor — now renders the SVG component instead of a background-image PNG. `chat-react-config`/`toolLogoUrl` (whose only purpose was the logo URL override), `OVERLAY_LOGO_SRC`, the `--overlay-mark-logo-url` vars, and the stray root `overlay-logo.png` are gone; `public/assets/overlay-logo.png` stays for lifecycle emails and favicons, which require raster. Requires desktop `d5bedcd` (SUBNAV 3), which no longer consumes the removed config.

- The ESLint boundary registry now covers every feature domain — `workspaces`, `agents`, `settings`, `showcase`, `admin`, and `knowledge-bases` are registered — and the workspace primitives all domains share moved out of the feature: `useWorkspace`/`WorkspaceProvider` → `@/contexts/WorkspaceContext`, routing helpers → `@/shared/workspaces/routing`, workspace types → `@/shared/workspaces/types`, and `useWorkspaceChanged` → the new `@/hooks` layer. The `showcase ↔ workspaces` import cycle is gone (the app layer injects the showcase workspace client), and `SERVER_DOMAINS` now covers all server domain directories with `config`/`database`/`env`/`idempotency`/`shared` as leaf infra; existing server cross-wiring is now enumerable warn-only debt.

- File ingestion jobs moved behind a real repository: `FileIngestionJobRepository` (Convex impl, Postgres `unsupportedRepository` sentinel) replaces the inline `lazyConvex` calls in the ingest-jobs routes, so `check-files-route-boundary` is enforced green again. `ActConversationRepository` now exposes a branded `ConversationId` — the BFF no longer imports `convex/_generated` for conversation ids, and the brand propagates through the conversation services and API routes.

- `ChatExperience` decomposition started: the mentions cluster (state, workspace category fetch, person-mention confirmation) extracted to `src/features/chat/components/use-chat-mentions.ts` — `useChatMentions({activeWorkspaceId, isPublicShowcase})` returns the state bag. Component drops ~90 LOC; the audit records the remaining extraction seams.

- `math-markdown-normalize` duplicate resolved: the `src/shared/markdown/` mirror was dead code (zero importers, unwired test) — the `packages/overlay-chat-react` copy is now canonical and its test moved alongside it under the package's own test runner.

- The 1,520-line `conversations/act` route is now a 16-line wrapper: the turn orchestration lives in `src/server/app-api/v1/conversations/act/ActTurnOrchestrationService.ts` (`executeActTurn`), which the route delegates to. Behavior unchanged — this is a relocation, and the route-characterization suite stays green.

- Root cleanup continued: `docker/` now holds both Dockerfiles and all six compose files (build contexts + `env_file` paths repointed; `package.json` db scripts, `docker-publish.yml` matrix, and deploy docs updated — also fixed the publish workflow's stale `context: installer` entry). `BUILDER_AGENT_PROMPT.md`/`INTEGRATION_AGENT_PROMPT.md` moved to `docs/develop/`. `LICENSE.md` is gone — its policy text was fully covered by `docs/legal/licensing.mdx`, so `LICENSE` (the AGPL-3.0 text) is the single license file.

- Root folder reorganization landed (~98 → 58 entries): strategy docs moved to `docs/plans/` (internal, exempt from public-docs wording rules), `infra/` + `installer/` consolidated under `deploy/`, `fixtures/` → `tests/fixtures/`, `workflows/` → `src/server/workflows/` (the `@/workflows` alias is gone; imports use `@/server/workflows/*`), and `scripts/` split into `ci/` (checks + boundary rules), `qa/` (smoke/rehearsal harnesses), `db/` (migrations/backfills), and `lib/`. `landing-copy/`, orphaned `app.json`/`eas.json`, and the `workers/` cache are gone; `scripts/dev-setup.sh` and `scripts/vercel-ignore-build.sh` stay at root (pinned by docs and the Vercel project setting).

- The web-complexity ratchet learned two behaviors: baseline exemptions are date-stamped (`recordedAt` carried across regenerations; exemptions older than 90 days print a non-blocking burn-down warning), and the new-file LOC check no longer fires on pure renames — a moved file keeps its exemption when the basename matches a vanished baseline path and LOC stays within 10%.

- The agent editor can dock to the side panel or float as a centered dialog from a single toggle in its title row, sharing the same form, border, and chrome so switching never remounts state. Saving keeps the editor open (with a brief "Saved" confirmation) while Cancel discards and closes.

- The Agents roster now opens the most recently used agent per workspace and orders the sidebar by recency (unused agents stay alphabetical). A conversation open that fails no longer sticks on the blank state: it retries, then shows an explicit error with a manual Retry.
- Made Agents the first primary navigation item and default authenticated home. Selecting an agent now opens its conversation directly, while create and edit controls use the shared right-side panel (and its small-screen dialog presentation) instead of the authenticated directory grid.
- Released the Agent Host and bridge protocol together at `0.3.5`, which forwards agents' advertised ACP slash commands (`available_commands_update`) to the transcript; production DM slash menus and the composer slash button activate once connected hosts are restarted on `0.3.5`.
- Unified the list-page UI system: added shared `Tile`, `TileGrid`, `TileIcon`, `TileSkeleton`, `CreateTile`, `ListRow`, and `HeaderSearch` primitives to `@overlay/ui` and migrated the Projects, Knowledge, Agents, and Extensions (Connectors, Skills, MCP Servers) list pages plus the Files/Knowledge header onto them, so tiles, list rows, and page headers share one spacing, radius, hover, and dark-mode language.
- Projects can now be archived and restored from a three-dot menu on each project tile, and the projects sidebar gained All/Archived subpages that list active and archived projects respectively.
- Newly created projects now open straight into inline rename with the title text pre-selected, whether created from the projects page or the sidebar, so the name can be typed immediately.
- Made a pristine Agent Host state store adopt the server's command stream position on first delivery (a previous host incarnation may have consumed earlier sequences), while keeping out-of-order rejection fail-closed once a cursor exists; released the Agent Host and bridge protocol together at `0.3.4`.
- Scoped direct-message and channel creation to the workspace visible in the UI, surfaced connected-agent start failures in chat, expired stale environment health, terminalized rejected host commands, and released the Agent Host and bridge protocol together at `0.3.3` with a pinned Node 24 macOS LaunchAgent that retains access to user-installed adapter CLIs. Agent Host SQLite state is now bound to its environment and workspace, preventing a misconfigured or migrated host from rejecting a new environment's command sequence using stale durable state.
- Enforced manual production releases with a source-controlled Vercel rule that suppresses Git-triggered deployments from `main` while preserving explicit CLI deployment and promotion.
- Made the staging Vercel project branch-only: its Ignored Build Step now fails closed and runs only for the exact `staging` ref, preventing PR, manual, hook, and other-branch builds.
- Retired the obsolete Overlay Vercel projects (`overlay-web-rc`, `overlay-landing-prod-migration-runner`, and the misspelled `overlay-web-postgress`); the canonical Overlay set is now `overlay-landing`, `overlay-web-staging`, and `overlay-web-postgres`.
- Clarified that the Integration agent reuses one long-lived `staging` worktree across pull requests, reserving temporary worktrees for exceptional investigations.
- Added an Integration preflight to confirm the PR base (`staging` or `main`) and Vercel deployment intent before acting.
- Added an owner-only direct-push fast path for `DevelopedByDev` on `main` and `staging`, while keeping force-pushes and branch deletion blocked for every account.
- The automation editor is text-first: instructions are the only authoring surface for new automations and the Flow card no longer renders for auto-derived graphs. Grandfathered automations whose graph was hand-edited in the canvas (`manuallyEdited`) keep the full Flow surface — editable canvas plus live run viewer — and run history stays visible in its own section either way.

### Fixed

- Saving an agent on a non-Claude managed runtime (OpenCode, Codex, Pi, Hermes) no longer fails with a bare `Request failed (500)`: the Convex `harness` validator in `collaboration/agents` and the `workspaceAgentDefinitions` schema only accepted `overlay`/`claude-code`, so arg validation rejected every other runtime before the handler ran. The union now covers the full `WORKSPACE_AGENT_HARNESSES` list, and a workspace-policy denial maps to a real 403 instead of an opaque 500. The dev Convex deploy was also unblocked by tolerating legacy/extra fields (`cancelAtPeriodEnd`, external-surface fields, `platforms`) as optional in the schema.
- The create-agent editor shows the runtime picker again for brand-new agents: creation inserts a draft row then opens edit mode, so locking the runtime on edit mode also locked it for never-saved drafts — drafts now keep the picker until first save while real edits stay locked.
- Managed harness turns survive a reset or expired sandbox. The environment lease stays `running` after reset while its provider reference names a destroyed sandbox; `ManagedAgentSandboxBilling.reserve` reconnected to it unconditionally, so every subsequent turn died at dispatch ("could not start this turn") before the workflow's recreate step could run. `reserve` now treats an unreachable sandbox as a zero-usage baseline (a recreated sandbox bills its full lifetime), and `settle` resolves the lease's *current* provider reference — the acquire step repoints the same lease when it recreates the sandbox. Sandbox settlement is also `.catch`-guarded on every path: it previously rethrew inside `finalizeManagedHarnessTurn` after the reply was already persisted, which marked the written message failed (hiding its text) and the run `model_failed`; the reservation's reconcile path now recovers the cents without failing the turn.
- A hosted agent's runtime can no longer be changed after creation. The runtime is the agent's identity — its environment, binding, and sessions are all per-harness — so the editor renders it as a fixed row; switching runtimes means archiving and recreating the agent.
- Agent saves no longer wedge on a failed computer provision. A `POST /boxes` flake left the bound row in `error` status with no provider ref, after which *any* save that touched the computer — including turning the toggle **off** — died on a 409 ("still provisioning") because `destroy` required a live machine ref. `destroy` now tolerates ref-less rows and treats provider cleanup as best-effort, provisioning retries the provider create once before erroring, and `provision` reclaims a dead `error`/stale-`provisioning` row (machine included) instead of stacking a duplicate on the owner binding — so retrying a failed computer setup actually provisions on the next save instead of failing forever. Server logs now record the underlying provider error for 5xx computer failures instead of swallowing it in `cause`.
- Archiving an agent now destroys its bound computer (best-effort): archived agents previously kept their machine — and its row — running and billing with no UI path back to it.
- Scheduled automations actually fire again. The `sleep()`-based per-automation scheduler workflow could not survive deployments (no durable workflow world is configured), so every enabled automation silently stopped running on schedule. Scheduled dispatch is back on the minute cron: `claimDueRuns` inserts a queued run and atomically advances `nextRunAt` (one claim per due slot — a run missed during downtime fires once late, not repeatedly), then each claimed run is dispatched through the same durable one-shot automation workflow as manual runs, with the workflow's own steps settling run status. `POST /api/v1/automations/{id}/start-scheduler` is now a deprecated no-op; the editor no longer calls it, and Postgres deployments keep their own `automation.schedule-due` job. Automations that were already enabled resume on the first cron tick after deploy — no re-saving needed.
- Image attachments in chat no longer fail to send: pasted screenshots and picked images were inlined as raw base64 data URLs inside the message JSON body, so a multi-MB image blew past the platform request-size cap and the send failed outright. Images are now downscaled to a 2048px edge and re-encoded as WebP client-side before attaching (GIFs pass through untouched since canvas encoding flattens animation; originals are kept when re-encoding can't beat them), with per-image and per-message size caps that surface a clear error instead of a failed send.
- Agent computer and agent-management tools no longer fail with `[tools] Tool not allowed` — `computer_exec`/`computer_read_file`/`computer_write_file`/`computer_list_files`/`computer_open_url` and `create_agent`/`update_agent` were exposed by `build.ts` but missing from the global `OVERLAY_TOOL_IDS` registry, so the defense-in-depth assert rejected every call. `COMPUTER_TOOL_IDS` now lives in `@overlay/tools-core` (re-exported by `tool-groups.ts`) so the registry and the agent group share one list, and a test pins every asserted id.
- Personal Chat Work mode no longer dies on tool schemas carrying JSON Schema `format` annotations: the workflow step that compiles tool schemas runs Ajv in strict mode and threw `unknown format "uri"` on tools like `computer_open_url`, failing every Work run that surfaced them. `format` keywords are now stripped when tool schemas are serialized for durable execution (they are advisory and do not constrain model input), and the chat banner now renders the run's real `terminalError` message instead of the generic "The run could not be completed".
- Settled assistant turns no longer hide answer text: the "Worked for N" collapse used to fold everything before the last text segment — including earlier answers — into the collapsed row. Work segments now fold behind one summary row at the top while every text segment keeps rendering in order, so `answer → tool call → closing line` shows both texts uninterrupted.
- Personal Chat Work mode now runs every model provider durably — BYOK keys, OpenRouter, and NVIDIA NIM no longer fail with `Unknown model: …`. The turn was the last `WorkflowAgent` caller: its internal model step resolved `gateway.languageModel(<id>)`, which only knows AI Gateway ids. `personal-chat-work.ts` now runs the same manual durable loop as automation turns — a `streamText` step rebuilds the model via `getLanguageModel` (BYOK credentials read from the vault at call time, never in the workflow log) and forwards live `ModelCallStreamPart`s so token-level streaming to the client is preserved — then one durable step per tool call, with the approval hook unchanged.
- Agent settings on the agents surface now docks as a right side panel (via the shell's `rightPanel` slot) instead of stacking under the conversation; dialog mode remains available as a toggle.
- Agent DM headers and message avatars now track the agent's live directory name — renames in the editor propagate immediately via `agent-directory-changed` instead of showing the stale "Untitled agent" snapshot stored on the conversation/participant records.
- Agent DM message rows now match personal chat: hover no longer paints a row background, and the action row reveals beneath the message instead of a floating rail at the top. Channels keep the Slack-style floating rail.
- Archiving a one-to-one DM no longer asks "for me or for everyone" — there's nobody else to keep it for, so it archives directly from both the conversation menu and the chat list. Channels and group DMs still ask.

- Fixed display-math fences glued to equation lines (`… × 100$$`) reflowing onto their own lines, stray `$` inside display spans repaired, and TeX grouping parens preserved inside single-dollar spans — sloppy model output now degrades to readable math instead of raw code.
- Fixed currency amounts being eaten by math rendering (`$20/mo **— $100…` tables): dollar signs followed by digits are now classified as currency openers and escaped unless the span carries explicit TeX structure, so prices survive KaTeX. Genuine digit-led math (`$2x + 1 = 5$`, finance examples) still renders.
- Fixed currency clauses containing hyphenated words being mangled into italic math (`$1.4M … For SV pre-seed, frame $` rendered "pre-seed" as a subtraction and let KaTeX eat the rest of the sentence): the math-minus heuristic now requires a spaced minus or single-char operands (`x - 1`, `a-b`), so multi-char word hyphens (`pre-seed`, `18-month`, `user-month`) stay prose.
- Fixed display equations clipping their bottom subscripts/descenders in chat and the notes editor: `.katex-display` is a horizontal scroll container whose tight bottom padding clipped glyph ink that overflows the last line box — bottom padding now absorbs it and the vertical axis degrades to a scrollbar instead of an invisible cut.
- The settled-turn collapse row now reads "Worked for {duration}, called N tools" and the duration survives reloads: it is derived from the assistant message's persisted `createdAt`→`updatedAt` span (the row is written `generating` at turn start and patched through settle on both backends), so reloaded agent replies and personal-chat turns show real timing instead of a bare "Worked".
- Message activity notifications no longer fire for a conversation you are actively viewing: the notification fan-out now skips any participant with a fresh online presence session parked on that conversation (the 45s presence heartbeat already reports it), so an agent answer arriving in the DM you have open does not also badge Activity.
- Fenced code blocks no longer render inside a second padded box: react-markdown wraps the block in `<pre>`, which carried its own background/padding around the code-block chrome — the renderer now unwraps `pre` when it directly contains the code block (bare fences keep the plain `pre` styling), preserving the stream-soften animation.
- Custom OpenAI-compatible providers no longer require an API key — the flag gates the dialog's Test/Save buttons, `POST /connections`, `POST /connections/test`, and the runtime gateway, so keyless endpoints can now be connected and used; when no key is stored no `Authorization` header is sent. (Note: OpenCode Zen's `/models` is keyless, but its completions still require an `OPENCODE_API_KEY` — its `*-free` models are session-locked to the OpenCode client.)
- Custom provider connections are easier to tell apart: the dialog's name field moved up under Provider as "Provider name" and auto-fills from the API base URL's host (e.g. `opencode.ai`) until edited, and connection rows show the endpoint host under the name instead of repeating the generic preset label.

- Pinned the Agent Host CLI restart/service hints, README, and launchd test to the released `0.3.5` line (they still pointed at `0.3.4`), and extended the release-gate script to assert all version pins together so the drift cannot recur.
- Fixed Workspace → People showing the authenticated provider user ID instead of the person’s profile name; existing member principals are repaired from the current browser session, and newly created workspaces start with the correct owner name.
- Allowed `data-remote-agent-commands` parts in the conversation message schema and BFF serialization: the Convex validator rejected connected-agent command events with 500s, and the BFF conversation serializer dropped the commands payload, leaving the agent DM slash menu empty after a page load.
- Fixed the agent DM slash menu ignoring typed input: the composer kept its live text outside React state by design, so the slash-menu hook only ever saw programmatically set text — typing `/` did not open the menu, filtering and selection did not update, and choosing a command left the menu stuck open. Typing now refreshes composer state only while a slash token is on screen, preserving the no-re-render-per-keystroke behavior for normal text.
- Recover personal-chat history when browser sign-in completes after an initially unauthenticated server render. Use the current browser identity for chat actions and clear the old server identity after sign-out.
- Repair bundle measurements for Next.js 16 using emitted Webpack entrypoints rather than removed framework internals; include ancestor layouts and deduplicate shared JS without raising existing budgets.

### Operational

- Production release 2026-09-16: `846d41f43` promoted on `overlay-landing` and production Convex synced. Managed harness agents enabled for every workspace (`OVERLAY_FEATURE_OVERLAY_CLOUD_ENVIRONMENTS=1`, `OVERLAY_FEATURE_MANAGED_HARNESS_AGENTS=true`, `OVERLAY_MANAGED_HARNESS_ROLLOUT_STAGE=general`); the production `VERCEL_TOKEN` (rotated/dead) and empty `AI_GATEWAY_API_KEY` were replaced with valid values so sandbox provisioning and in-sandbox model brokering work.

- Alternate the homepage headline with “Give your agents a workspace.” every five seconds using an upward rolling transition. Both headlines retain colorful section links, the surrounding layout stays fixed, and reduced-motion preferences disable the rolling animation.

### Chief of Staff (pending)




- Let the Chief read recovered assignments by assignment ID, with explicit pending-launch feedback and labeled attempt, child-run and conversation handles instead of guessing whether recovery started.

- Route assignment parent backlinks to the authorized conversation's actual channel, agent thread, or chat instead of always opening the caller agent.

- Show Work's existing no-results message when a search matches neither tasks nor agent assignments, including assignment-only workspaces.

- Return corrective feedback for confirmed assignment-resolution rejections, exposing current attempt IDs only after authorized reads.
- Mark never-launched assignments cancelled when their parent ends, keeping Work status and open counts accurate.

- Defer assignment launch recovery while its parent Harness run is waiting, preserving retry attempts until the parent is running again.

- Return corrective tool feedback when an agent tries to update a nonexistent plan step, including an empty plan after answering a question, rather than surfacing a fatal workflow error. Ordinary replies still do not require a plan.

- Keep Harness assignment, recovery and native turn-queue repository operations in a server-only base class without changing their authorization or persistence behavior.

- Allow generated personal-Chief identities in agent discovery, ask and post requests. The caller-ID limit now matches the target agent-ID limit instead of rejecting valid Chief IDs before authorization and directory lookup.

- Refresh the visible Chief plan when an assignment is accepted or excluded, keeping the delivery and transcript projections aligned with the settled Harness milestone.

- Renew native Chief leases during a blocking child-result read when the conversation projects a child wait but the Harness is still running the read tool. Keep workflow ownership, membership and model-dispatch checks intact.

- Keep room read-marker callbacks stable across live renders so idle chat views stop resubmitting read mutations. Convex's pending-write navigation protection and the notes editor's unsaved-change protection remain enabled.

- Show authorized Chief assignment executions in the Work list as an Agent assignments group of read-only rows drawn like tasks (status glyph, Needs you/Failed/Cancelled tags, parent, agent avatar), with a side panel for the brief, result, reasons and links to the assignment and Chief threads, and as one-line rows under Everything and Needs you in the Work sidebar. They do not create tasks; archived or inaccessible threads are omitted.

- Coordinate Chief assignments with explicit acceptance or reasoned exclusion/cancellation, parent cancellation propagation, durable native-turn queues and duplicate-safe launch recovery. Current-run finalization waits for published, resolved assignment results without adding a verifier or compulsory plan.

- Keep Chief assignment revisions in one isolated thread with immutable per-attempt run handles, persisted publication-aware results and full authorized reply reads.

- Keep delegated reply reads available while the Chief consolidates completed assignments, and refresh expanded agent thread lists when live thread membership changes.

- Add isolated, idempotent Chief agent assignments with ACL-filtered navigation and execution status projections; preserve shared-source resource restrictions across assignment children and native refreshes.


## 2026-10-13 — Release workflow migration

- Complete the release workflow migration: isolated PR previews without a paid custom suffix, native main merge queue, on-demand exact-main candidates, manual web-first production promotion, and verified rollback/restoration. Production release `bee84a33a2d4` passed authenticated smoke; automatic staging Stripe checkout/cancellation and browser storage acceptance passed.

- Submit production builds asynchronously and poll owned readiness; bound other command output at 16 MiB so large build logs cannot terminate release orchestration.

- Parse Vercel CLI 63 structured production output and wait for the owned exact-SHA remote build to become READY before publishing web or pushing production Convex.

- Recognize the primary submodule checkout in production release validation while continuing to refuse linked feature worktrees.

- Preserve consumed credit and existing holds when Stripe cancels a workspace plan, removing unused plan credit as holds settle or release, and disabling automatic top-ups.

- Wait for chat transcript hydration in release smoke, explicitly select Chat, and clean fresh production QA conversations.

- Resolve the actual synthetic workspace in release smoke and clean fresh test chats; consolidate main queue/check protection and restore universal history guards.

- Repair missing PR preview fixtures after interrupted builds, preserving existing personal workspaces and test data.

- Prepare exact-main release candidates only on demand; reuse PR preview infrastructure and accept verified native candidate proof before manual production promotion.

- **Release cutover guards and note conflict correctness.** Dedicated staging rejects missing Sandbox credentials before deploying and stops immediately on a blocked Vercel build. Hosted backend selection no longer depends on the staging hostname. Expected stale-note conflicts use structured Convex application data, preserving HTTP 409 on production-type backends. Process docs now specify PR previews, main merge queue, dedicated main staging and manual web-first production; live staging, queue, production and rollback acceptance passed on the recorded release.


### Fixed

- **Disabled preview providers also disable their capabilities.** Shared Preview settings and release build guards prevent chat from loading an integration provider configured as `none`. Legacy staging Production settings are preserved.

- **Reactive Convex authentication uses its handler contract.** Browser HMAC tokens remain explicit query arguments; the client no longer sends them to an unconfigured platform identity verifier. Staging smoke now checks an authenticated subscription, denial for a different user and browser runtime errors.

- **Edits to legacy notes stay visible.** Canonical text updates remove an older text field that could mask the saved content on subsequent reads.

- **PR checks and previews spend less time on obsolete work.** New revisions cancel superseded code checks; maintenance-only diffs keep CI and docs checks without rebuilding the unchanged app. Hosted previews remain available through an explicit request. Dependency Review now performs its comparison, and paid canary deployment events no longer publish skipped required contract checks.

- **Removed preview wildcard fallback.** Exact PR aliases serve previews; unknown or closed preview hostnames return 404. Trusted control checks the certificate-only wildcard, verifies cleanup, settles superseded builds before deploying newer backend code, and retries transient reads without blindly repeating mutations.
- **Stale note saves report conflicts.** Convex rejection now reaches the existing HTTP `409` response instead of reporting a successful save with unchanged content. Found during isolated-preview CRUD QA.

### Added

- **Prepared the separate production release QA identity.** WorkOS identity/password readback passed, and a deployment-scoped Convex credential is stored privately with a January 10 expiry. The promotion command loads its four settings from the owner's private release JSON after checkout validation, avoiding repeated exports. Production application smoke, deployment, deliberate rollback and restoration passed on the recorded release.

- **Prepared one-command production promotion.** Exact clean canonical main and successful staging proof gate a remote web build, verified web publication, production Convex push and authenticated smoke. Checkpoints preserve partial release state and previous web deployment IDs; the rollback runbook retains schema compatibility. Live activation, deliberate rollback and restoration passed.

- **Boolean release flags default off.** A request-scoped PostHog helper lets unfinished server behavior stay dark, evaluates each requested set once, and falls back to the existing behavior when configuration or the flag service is unavailable.

- **Prepared authenticated staging cutover.** A candidate build can pass sandbox sign-in, note CRUD/conflict, real chat and Files checks before changing staging's canonical alias. Publication requires verified provider isolation and complete proof from the same serialized main workflow, followed by canonical smoke. Live activation passed; the owner waived the calendar soak.

- **Shared preview provider defaults.** Existing LayerNorm sandbox Stripe credentials/prices and isolated Gateway/OpenRouter test keys are configured once for future Vercel builds and new Convex previews, with readback verified. Shared test credentials and release automation tokens have no scheduled expiry; OpenRouter lane keys retain $1 total limits with no reset. Shared Preview hosted-access flags permit fresh model QA; dedicated staging backend test settings are separate. Application reply and reload persistence passed; authenticated R2 upload/download, browser rendering and CORS isolation passed.

- **Dedicated staging candidates from main.** A manually dispatched, serialized workflow pins Divyansh's test project, exact accepted main revision and separate Convex backend. It rejects untrusted or mismatched builds and records web/backend provenance. Native and published staging smoke passed; main merges do not deploy staging or production.

- **PR preview lifecycle automation and merge-queue checks.** Trusted main workflows provision per-PR custom origins, pin builds to the current head, verify alias assignment, and clean up on close or retarget; scheduled reconciliation covers missed events. Wildcard fallback hosts return 404. All required checks support merge groups, including explicit dependency comparisons and existing CLA-signature verification.
  Management credentials are confined to a main-only GitHub environment, and the controller disables automatic domain assignment before provenance checks.
  Open PR backends renew within Convex's retention limit; expired backends force a fresh web build and seed instead of reusing a broken READY frontend.

- **Isolated PR preview foundations.** The staging project's opt-in PR build gate requires its canonical custom URL and a preview-scoped Convex key. Builds keep each PR's backend separate from shared development; internal seed fixtures and a sandbox-only QA login support browser checks. Preview crons, email delivery, and outbound webhooks are disabled. Production web builds continue to leave Convex deployment to the separate promotion step.

## 2026-08-30

### Added

- Added an exhaustive web API route catalog and compact route index ([#72](https://github.com/LayerNorm/overlay-web/pull/72)).
- Added Hermes connected-agent support ([#67](https://github.com/LayerNorm/overlay-web/pull/67)).
- Prepared Bring Your Own Agents for production rollout ([#66](https://github.com/LayerNorm/overlay-web/pull/66)).

### Changed

- Renamed the public Agent Host packages to the product-qualified `@layernorm/overlay-agent-host` and `@layernorm/overlay-agent-bridge-protocol` names ([#78](https://github.com/LayerNorm/overlay-web/pull/78)).
- Simplified agentic development to two roles: Builders submit pull requests to `staging`, and the Integration agent owns staging QA and promotion from `staging` to `main` ([#77](https://github.com/LayerNorm/overlay-web/pull/77)).
- Added reusable Builder and Integration agent prompt files that encode the role boundaries and handoff contract ([#77](https://github.com/LayerNorm/overlay-web/pull/77)).
- Established a worktree-first agentic development process with a designated integration role and history-preserving merge commits by default ([#77](https://github.com/LayerNorm/overlay-web/pull/77)).
- Disabled pull-request Preview deployments in both Vercel projects; hosted pre-production QA runs only from the dedicated staging project's `staging` branch ([#77](https://github.com/LayerNorm/overlay-web/pull/77)).
- Disabled Git-triggered production Vercel deployments; merging `main` now leaves a release merged but not deployed until an explicit production deployment is authorized ([#79](https://github.com/LayerNorm/overlay-web/pull/79)).
- Enabled contextual memory in agent direct messages and channels ([#69](https://github.com/LayerNorm/overlay-web/pull/69)).
- Improved project navigation and made inline project naming safer ([#71](https://github.com/LayerNorm/overlay-web/pull/71)).
- Published agent-host packages under the LayerNorm npm scope ([#68](https://github.com/LayerNorm/overlay-web/pull/68)).
